# Architecture — the end-to-end process and the five planes

**Date:** 2026-09-27, reviewed 2026-09-30 · **Status:** describes the structure that actually works. The guarantees of section 6 apply to the path that runs through the worker; the command-line measurement paths are outside them.

**Plain-language summary.** The system is a general framework for processing documents and emails with AI. Each flow
reads a document or an email, lets code, JEV and OpenAI each make the decisions they are suited to, and stores the
result, together with its to-dos, in a local database. Three reference flows show how it works: document type
recognition, invoice data extraction and email intent. Documents and emails go into work packages, whose runs a
background worker processes, reserving the cost before every paid call; a local service and a browser interface give
access to all of this. This page describes the process end to end, the five planes the flows share and the layers built
on top of them.

**What this is:** a general, multilingual document- and email-processing **AI-flow framework**
(Burr + Pydantic + sidecar + JEV) in which the flows are instances and the framework layer (adapter, registry schema,
call-site schema, policy schema, eval, store, contract, input adapters) is shared. The process below shows the three
reference flows (M1 document type recognition, M2 Hungarian invoice with two paths, M3 email intent); a new flow sits on the same
planes. The reference flows are the yardstick for the efficiency and reliability of the framework and of the
JEV–Pydantic AI–Burr combination. Teachable document processing is a complementary goal: a versioned processing recipe
for a known type, general data-point proposals with source checking for an unknown one. The shared source- and
structure-checking capabilities serve both. OpenAI and JEV can both be used, for documents and emails alike; the division of labour between them is
decided per task by measurement (section 3).

**Package layout (208, plan G-process-core R2).** The code is in one folder per layer, lowest first:
`jav/core/` (the store, the settings, the registries, the decisions log, the call log, the job queue and, since 213,
the process core `jav/core/process/`, section 16),
`jav/documents/` (reading, OCR and the native readers), `jav/ai/` (JEV, GPT, the candidates, the decision rules and the
type packs), `jav/flows/` (the Burr graphs and their registry), `jav/app/` (work packages, runs, results, outputs and
workflows), `jav/runtime/` (the worker), `jav/domains/<domain>/` (a domain's own modules), `jav/service/` (the local
service) and `jav/cli/` (the command line); beside them `jav/evals/` (measurement, with the three stand-alone tools of
section 13), `jav/devtools/` (the preflight, the guards, the generated documents) and `jav/legacy/` (the port of the
legacy project). A layer may import its own and the layers below it, never the ones above; a domain never imports
another domain; only the command line (and a domain's golden sets) imports the measurement. A domain's addresses of the
local service (`api_*`), its commands (`*_cli`) and its golden sets follow the rules of the service, the command line
and the measurement. `tests/test_dependency_direction.py` reads every module's imports, inside functions too: it fails
on a module outside the layers' folders and on a new import against the direction, and lists the ones that exist
today, each with the step that takes it away (the list can only shrink; 227, plan R3c: 15 → 7, the reading's own
rules and input kinds in the documents layer, the conflict error and the secrets of `.env` in the core, the worker's
state read by the service and the command line, the quote's golden set passed to its measurements; 229, plan structure
stage 1, MD1: 7 → 0, the value checks `fact_checks` and the source instances `source_instances` in the documents layer,
the JEV check of the native facts in the AI layer, `jav/ai/native_verification.py`). Since 229 a lower layer does not
name a higher layer's module as text either (an import by name, a registry entry naming the module that owes it):
what it needs from above is handed over when the higher layer is loaded. The application hands the shared registries
its registrations' call (`jav/core/completion.py`; the log events and decisions, the checks and the flows complete
themselves through it, and reading one in a process without the application is `RegistrationsMissing`), the item kinds
tell the native result whether an item is read as a document (`native_results.set_document_check`) and the package
result hands the role-swap check its counted results (`party_history.set_results`). A domain registers its own flows
(the email's intent, the bank table's statement) and claims the item and source kinds it owns (`item_kinds.claim`,
`claim_source`). A module that
moved keeps a saved flow state readable: the state persister reads its classes at their new place
(`jav/flows/persistence.py` `MOVED_MODULES`; 209: a class that left its module, `MOVED_CLASSES`; 227: a renamed class
under its new name, `RENAMED_CLASSES`, so the 2 774 states saved before 188 with the record's old name `InvoiceHU` load
as `DocumentRecord`). The data model is the AI layer's general one (`jav/ai/models.py`); the Hungarian invoice's own field lists and G-path output model are beside it (`jav/ai/invoice_models.py`), and the page
layout that reading makes is the documents layer's (`jav/documents/layout.py`, 209). The
instructions and output schemas sent to the models are config data in `configs/prompts/` (209), not code. A domain's
tables are its own (209: the email tables left the shared store, `jav/domains/email/email_store.py`): a shared module or
another domain reaches them through the domain's registrations, and the same test fails on a module outside a domain
that names one of its tables in its SQL (the measurement and the developer tools excepted). The legacy project's
database is not a runtime dependency.

**Native document path.** Work packages also accept DOCX, XLSX, TXT and CSV. The worker dispatches these to
`jav/flows/flow_native.py`; the readers preserve structured source elements, and `jav/documents/native_results.py` stores immutable
readings and interpretation publications. Model calls use the existing provider adapters, receipts and budget ledger.
The API exposes complete source elements and source-linked facts to the native review views. Corrections retain the
original proposals, approval is bound to the displayed review version, and the shared result datasets include native
facts and reading status. PDF and email items keep their existing flows. A PDF whose recognised type has no fitting
type pack (a scan with the reader's local OCR) continues from detection into the native flow when the recipe's
`unknown_documents` setting is
`facts` (the default, 120); its result is then chosen by its native publication (`native_results.native_item`), not
by its suffix. The storage boundaries, failure states and
source-location contract are described in [Native document processing](guides/NATIVE_PROCESSING.md).

This page is written by hand. The generated parts are the graphs' `docs/flows/<flow>/FLOW.md` (in the repository), and
the JEV call-site catalogue and the state snapshot (local, generated from the store: `python -m jav.cli docs`,
`admin --write`). Rules: `CLAUDE.md` and the [development guide](guides/DEVELOPMENT.md); JEV capabilities:
`docs/JEV_PLAYBOOK.md`; security: `docs/SECURITY.md`; config files: `docs/guides/CONFIGS.md`.

## 1. The process from an email to a decision

```
 Settings → Mailboxes: download now, or a schedule (section 11)
        │
        ▼
 worker: jav/domains/email/mailbox_bridge.py fetch (starts the temporary receiver below)
        │
        ▼
 scripts/mail_bridge_call.ps1
        │
        ▼
 scripts/outlook_bridge.ps1 (148: ported from 10_AIFLOW_V4; the Inbox, its direct subfolders and Sent Items; reads desktop Outlook, read-only; -RepoRoot inbox/.bridge)
        │
        ├─► attachment files: inbox/.bridge/data/inbox/email/<acct>/<eid>/
        │
        │ POST /ingest/email
        ▼
 jav/domains/email/ingest_server.py: temporary receiver on a free port, one-time key (the bridge protocol)
        │
        ▼
 inbox/<mailbox>/<msgid>/message.json (points to the attachment files)
        │
        ▼
 work package with the default processing (a person starts the paid run; the worker runs it)
        │
        ▼
 M3 email_intent graph (jav/domains/email/flow_email.py)
 load_message → classify_attachments → intent → route → tasks → save
                         │                        │              │
                         │                        │              └─► store.emails + email_results + review_queue
                         │                        ▼
                         │            email_rules.email_next_flow (code) ──► m2:<type> · m1:detect · human:* · archive*
                         ▼
 M1 graph for each PDF (jav/flows/flow_detect.py)
 load_pdf [→ ocr_pdf] → detect → save ──► store.documents
                        JEV: doc_type + issuer_hu + language (+ the detailed type)

 folder corpus (PDFs) ──► detect-corpus ──► M1 graph per document ──► store.documents (type × year report)

 store.documents (a type with a type pack; the detailed type) ──► M2 invoice graph (jav/flows/flow.py; the type pack
 configs/types/<type>.json supplies the fields, questions, prompt and validators), two paths in one graph:
     load_pdf [→ ocr_pdf], then
     S: find_candidates (code) → jev_select (JEV Choice, 3 batched requests) → normalize_picks
     G: extract_llm (gpt, Pydantic AI) → jev_verify (code evidence + JEV Noul fan-out) → normalize_llm
        without JEV: extract_llm → code_verify (code evidence only, the word layer too; a value printed nowhere → source:not_found)
        (extract_llm also keeps the token probabilities of the answer's values: llm_token_p)
        (extract_llm's instructions: the pack's prompt, its prompt_note, then the shared block of configs/gpt_extract.json)
        → normalize_llm; when JEV is unavailable, jev_verify keeps the code evidence and the required-field check
     both → validate (code) → decide_route (policy) → ground (code) → save → done | needs_review
```

Each PDF attachment is also an item of the email's work package and gets the full document processing
(M1 + M2, section 10). The email input can still be driven by hand: an old bridge started without `-RepoRoot` posts to
`python -m jav.cli email-ingest-server` and keeps its attachments under the legacy project's `data/inbox/email/<acct>/<eid>/`;
the receiver resolves both attachment roots (`jav/domains/email/ingest_server.py` `host_path`), and `email-inbox inbox/` then runs M3.

The **golden loop** is the same for every flow: golden set (referenced from the legacy project; the PII stays there) → run
with the cache ($0 thanks to the request hash) → report (`runs/*.jsonl` + Markdown) → discrepancy analysis from the raw
run → general rule (config change with a version bump) → rerun → determinism measurement without the cache. From live
data: a manual labelling list (`*_manual_sample.md`) → our own golden set (`golden_labels`). Situations the legacy sets do
not cover (credit notes, receipts without an invoice number; 151: the outgoing invoice) have invented cases in
`configs/golden_synthetic.json` (`golden --synthetic`), run and reported apart, so the legacy results stay comparable.
151: a local case list outside git (`golden --cases <file>`, `evals.load_case_file`) measures a path on real documents
against reference values, such as the other path's results; its run file is `<type>_local_<arm>` and its score is an
agreement, not an accuracy.
188: every golden set is in one registry (`jav/evals/goldens.py`) and one command measures them
(`python -m jav.cli measure [<golden set> ...]`, `--list` names them): the data points of each type pack with golden
cases on each of its paths (legacy and synthetic cases apart), the type recognition, the email intent, and the logic
packs' golden sets (the reconciliation's pairs and incoming money kinds; since 231 A3d the quote's lines, matches and
reply letters are the IT quote process pack's).
204: a logic pack's golden sets are described in its domain's measurement module (`jav/domains/finance/goldens.py`,
`jav/domains/quotes/goldens.py`), which the domain module names (`GOLDENS`) and the registry imports by name
(`goldens.logic_pack_goldens`), in the packs' registration order; until 204 the pack named them in its registration
(`LogicPack.goldens`), so its own module imported the measurement layer. A business module of a domain imports no
measurement code (`tests/test_golden_owners_204.py`).
Each set is measured by its own command's function with the same arguments, so the raw run files stay as they were;
without a budget only earlier answers count, and a paid round names one set. A round writes its sets' reports into
`runs/<stamp>_measure.log` and a receipt with the commit and one line per set into `runs/<stamp>_measure.jsonl`.
189: the coming quote request logic pack's golden set is invented (`configs/golden_quotes.json`, read and laid out
as mailbox folders by `jav/domains/quotes/invented_golden.py`); it joined the shared command with the quote package (191), and since
231 A3d the IT quote process pack owns it.
197: the packs converted from legacy types count with their flat expected records (the ticket's 11 cases, the general
terms' 10), and `facts:<family>` measures the general facts (the native path's source-bound facts, no type pack) on a
type pack's legacy golden documents with the pack withheld (`configs/golden_facts.json`, `jav/evals/evals_facts.py`): the
pack's scored fields are looked for among the facts by value, a repeated file is one document, and the documents are
split into a development and an evaluation half fixed before any measurement; the documents take the native graph's
reading and interpretation, are not published and open no to-do.
Teaching a type (197) starts from such a family: `python -m jav.cli type-propose <family>`
(`jav/evals/type_teaching.py`) reads the development half's measured facts (read only, no AI call) and proposes the names
that recur in at least three of its documents as fields, with their counts and a kind (a date when every value is
exactly one date); code joins only one name's spellings and a bilingual label's first part, never synonyms, and no
value is printed or kept (`--shapes`: the written forms of the development half's labels, letters and digits masked).
A person writes the draft (`configs/type_drafts/<key>.json`); `type-candidate <key>` makes its candidate pack the way
the legacy converter made the converted packs (pack, prompt, schema, a verification site that inherits `verify`),
never picked by detection, and lists the shared registries' entries still missing; `measure taught:<key>:evaluation` measures
the candidate on the family's evaluation half, on the G path (`taught:<key>:development`: on its own development half,
an in-sample check before the evaluation; a draft is corrected from that half only).
Detection picks a pack only by a person's command (199): `type-activate <key> [--replace <pack>]` and
`type-withdraw <key> [--restore]` turn the pack's own detection switch (`auto_detect`) as a new version with a
changelog line, and the files stay for the earlier results. A taught pack is turned on only with the latest shared
measurement of its evaluation half on a clean working tree, every case answered, on the files it has now (the legacy
activation gate); the result is shown and no threshold decides. `--replace` turns the family's pack it takes over off
in the same step, and `--restore` turns that one back on. A pack without detection anchors is turned on only as its
family's one pack, because next to another the anchor score would pick it without a question. The capability
catalog reads whether a type is active from the same switch.

## 2. The five planes

| Plane | What | Where | Control |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Flow** (Burr) | five graphs: the three reference flows, native reading (non-PDF inputs) and the bank table flow; `@action.pydantic` typed state, `run_id` = app_id, local tracker | `jav/flows/flow.py`, `flow_detect.py`, `flow_email.py`, `flow_native.py`, `flow_statement_table.py`; a `CONTRACT` at the end of each | `python -m jav.cli flows` lint + FLOW.md; tracker UI `burr` |
| **Decision** (JEV + GPT) | the families of JEV call sites: detect and detect_detail, email_intent, select (Hungarian, foreign, utility), verify (per type) — one file each under `configs/callsites/`; two generative steps: the G path's extractor (extract_llm) and the email task proposals (`configs/email_tasks.json`); the adapter (`jav/ai/jev.py`) puts the concrete model version into the cache key, calls with a RetryPolicy and passes SDK errors on as `JevUnavailableError` | question sets `configs/callsites/*.json`, registries `configs/doc_types.json`, `intents.json`; code: `jav/ai/detect.py`, `intent.py`, `jev_select.py`, `jev_verify.py`, `extract_llm.py` | `configs` (version, hash), the call-site catalogue (local, generated by `python -m jav.cli docs`), bands and routes in `configs/policy.json` (`bands` / `band_for`, `jav/ai/policy.py`) |
| **Data** (SQLite) | documents · datapoints · emails · review_queue · ledger · golden_labels; also the tables for work packages, runs, the job queue, to-do reasons, the call log, email results, mailboxes and watched folders (full list: the `CREATE TABLE` statements in `jav/core/store.py`, `jav/domains/email/email_store.py`, `jav/app/work.py`, `jav/core/queue.py`, `jav/core/calls.py`, `jav/domains/email/mailbox.py`, `jav/app/app_settings.py`, `jav/app/corrections.py`, `jav/documents/source_layer.py`, `jav/legacy/legacy_import.py` and `jav/app/parties.py`); the process state lives in a separate store (`store/burr_state.sqlite`) | `store/jav.sqlite`, `jav/core/store.py` (additive migration; 204: a module adds the columns an older store lacks with `add_columns`, never by hand; 187: `read_only`, a read-only look that runs neither the schema nor the migrations and creates no store, used by the state snapshot and, since 231, the generated call-site pages; 231 D-d4: a connection a migration opens while the store is set up does not set it up again) | `store` statistics (row counts of the core tables; 209: a module adds its own lines in their place, `register_summary`, the email domain its emails and their intents); the ledger records every AI call with its `config_hash` and, on failure, an `error` |
| **Measurement** (eval) | flow-independent list of judgements from the raw runs: accuracy and band per question, calibration (ECE), top-prob vs. conf, policy re-evaluation without calls, determinism; the measurements without JEV (`*_gpt`, `*_G_nojev`) are flows of their own with the GPT call sites, a GPT field's confidence calibrated against its score, and the silent errors (wrong, no to-do in the recorded run) per flow | `jav/evals/eval_report.py` (input `runs/*.jsonl`), the flow-specific runners `jav/evals*.py`; the golden sets' registry `jav/evals/goldens.py` (188), with the domains' measurement modules (204: `jav/domains/finance/goldens.py`, `jav/domains/quotes/goldens.py`) | `eval-report` → `runs/<time>_eval_report.md`; `measure` for every golden set (188); golden / determinism commands per flow |
| **Administration** | config versions and hashes, models and prices, lint, golden results, review queue; session rules and hooks | `jav/core/cfg.py`, `configs/models.json`, `jav/devtools/admin.py`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/settings.json`, `scripts/hooks/` | `admin` on one screen; hooks at session start, before compacting and at stop (a non-blocking reminder), plus a review agent; see the [development guide](guides/DEVELOPMENT.md) |

## 3. Where each decision is made — the decision hierarchy in practice

1. **Code** (deterministic, tested): candidate finders, validators, body cleaning, anchor features, evidence matching,
   `next_flow`, the review latch. This is the mechanism.
2. **JEV** (judgement over a finite set of options): one of the tools for selection, presence and verification steps. One call site = one JSON config
   + one `build_state` + one `build_questions` + one `jev.ask(...)` through the adapter. Raw probability goes into the state,
   thresholds only into versioned rule files (the shared bands in `policy.json`; 224: a domain's own rules in its own
   file, such as `email.json`): a named band set per call site (`band_for`), Noul no / uncertain / yes,
   Choice auto / uncertain / human (confidence threshold + gap to the second option); the `uncertain` band triggers review only
   with `uncertain_review: true`. Because of the request-hash cache, the same (concrete model version, state, questions) gives the same answer
   for $0; since 200 the configured model is a concrete version (`configs/models.json` `jev.model`, `jev-1.14.0`), so
   the answers do not change by themselves; the provider's moving name (`jev.watch_alias`, `jev-latest`) is only resolved
   with a probe by the daily backup (`runs/cache/_model_versions.json`, 24 h TTL; `jav/ai/jev_version.py`), and the
   start-up check and the System page say when it points to a newer version (switching is a decision with a
   measurement). An alias still works as the model and is resolved the same way. If JEV still
   does not answer after the SDK retries, the adapter writes a ledger entry (`error`) and raises `JevUnavailableError`; the flows turn this
   into a `jev_unavailable:<reason>` review (M1/M3 `final_status = jev_unavailable`) instead of crashing. A request refused
   for the token limit (`max_tokens_exceeded`) is remembered under its key and the concrete version that refused it
   (`runs/cache/too_large/`, 200); it is refused again without a call, with the same reason, so the request-size frame
   (`jav/ai/jev_budget.py`) goes on to its tighter request at once and a free round can answer it. Processing without JEV
   is a guarantee (160, `jav/ai/engine.py`): a flow application built with `jev=False` runs every step inside `jev.disabled()`, so a
   JEV question asked anyway is refused before the model probe, the cache and the call log (`JevUnavailableError("disabled")`,
   the `jev_unavailable:disabled` to-do; `get_adapter()` refuses too, whatever adapter a run installed). In the tests the refusal
   fails the test (`tests/conftest.py`); a test of the refusal itself is marked `jev_refusal` (`tests/test_jev_safety_net_160.py`).
   The engine matrix (`configs/engine_matrix.json`, checked by `jav/devtools/engine_matrix.py` in the preflight) lists every JEV call
   site with its counterpart without JEV or a stated exception, and the tests that cover it; the paired tests
   (`tests/test_paired_*_160.py`) run each flow with both engines. Which engine a run uses: the application's AI engine
   (`app_settings.engine_default`, an `app_options` row; JEV and GPT unless someone changes it) is the default of every new
   processing setting that does not name one (`work_recipes.default_params`, `engine_params`): a package without saved settings, a
   mailbox download's package, a watched folder's package, an assignment without `jev`. Saved settings and started runs keep
   their own (a run freezes its settings when it starts); an assignment saved before the engine setting existed (recipes 1.9.0, 2026-10-02) has no `jev` and stays JEV and GPT.
3. **OpenAI, through Pydantic AI**: the existing G path and the general data proposal produce structured proposals and then apply source checking. The graphs described here neither restrict OpenAI to generating extracts nor make JEV the only tool for every interpretive decision: further interpreting, classifying and verifying roles are also permitted, for documents and emails alike. Any new division of labour must be built in on the basis of targeted measurement; source checking, separate cost accounting and manual activation apply to every role. The Burr states are plain Pydantic models, not Pydantic AI.
4. **OCR pipeline**: `jav/documents/ocr.py` + `configs/ocr.json` — PDF without text → page image (pypdfium2,
   300 dpi) → tesseract word boxes (native tesseract on the machine; the Hungarian + English language packs taken from the old sidecar's Docker image
   into `tools/tessdata`; fallback: the old sidecar image via `docker run`) → the same line/cell builder as for the text layer
   (`jav/documents/pdf.py: build_layout`). A separate graph step (`ocr_pdf`) in the M1 and M2 graphs, a disk cache in `runs/ocr/`,
   raw quality signals in the state, thresholds in the `ocr` block of `policy.json`. The rest of the old sidecar (torch, the matcher)
   has not been ported. When local OCR is weak, `ocr_with_escalation` asks Azure DI (paid): since 121 directly over its REST API
   (`jav/documents/azure_di.py`, standard-library HTTP, ported from the old sidecar's provider) when `AZURE_DI_ENDPOINT` and
   `AZURE_DI_KEY` are set, otherwise through the old sidecar, which sees only the legacy data folder. The call goes through the
   call log (`azure_recognise`): in a worker run the page count is reserved from the run's Azure budget (recipe switch
   `azure_ocr`); an analysis Azure accepted but whose result was lost is an uncertain call. A blocked or failed escalation leaves
   the local text and, unless the recipe switch is off, a to-do (`ocr:escalation_blocked:*`: budget, uncertain earlier attempt,
   no route to Azure, failed call). Since 123 the original Azure recognition (word polygons, page size, unit, angle)
   is kept once in the store, under the sha256 of its canonical JSON (`ocr.save_recognition`, artifact kind
   `azure_recognition`), and the digest travels with the recognition signals (`recognition_sha256`); the text, the
   OCR cache key and the call's request hash are unchanged. Since 124 detection names the recognition whose text
   went on (`ocr_recognition`; an Azure text cached before 123 has no kept original and names nothing), and a scan
   continuing into general facts takes it over in the native reader (`jav/documents/readers/external_recognition.py`, see
   `docs/guides/NATIVE_READERS.md`). Heavy dependencies may only go behind a sidecar.
5. **Isolated PDF reading**: `jav/documents/isolated_pdf.py` + `configs/service.json` `pdf_reader` — the third-party PDF parsers
   (pdfplumber for the text layer, PDFium for the OCR page images, the page sizes and the review page images) run in one
   long-lived helper process per calling process (worker, local service), one request at a time, with a time limit per
   request and a memory limit (on Windows a job object). Our own processing (words → lines and cells) stays in the
   calling process. Over a limit the helper is stopped and replaced; text extraction fails the item with a named error
   (`PdfReaderLimit`, no retry), the OCR page images give a to-do (`ocr:unavailable:PdfRenderLimit`), and a review page
   image is answered with 422. `isolated: false` runs the same functions in-process. 138: the same helper parses a
   tabular statement export (`statement_table.parse_rows`: openpyxl, defusedxml, the CSV reader) within the read time
   limit; a parser error keeps its message and a limit is a named refusal (`StatementTableError`, 422).

## 4. Tuning and changes — the procedure

| Change | Where | What follows |
|---|---|---|
| type or intent description (`what`), boundary (`not_for`), example (`examples`), family (`parent`) | `configs/doc_types.json` / `intents.json` (schema v2), bump `meta.version` + changelog | `detect-golden` / `email-golden` (new hash → live calls), `detect-determinism` / `email-determinism`; `eval-report` (parent-label section); regenerate `docs` |
| JEV instruction, Noul question | `configs/callsites/<callsite>.json` | the same; the local, generated call-site catalogue shows the statistics per version |
| band, threshold, route | `configs/policy.json` (`bands`, `band_for`, routes) | no rerun needed: `eval-report` shows from the raw runs (`runs/*.jsonl`) how many cases would change band |
| model, price, timeout, retry, cache version, alias TTL | `configs/models.json` | bumping `cache_version` invalidates every cache key; a model-version change (detected by the probe) produces a new key by itself |
| graph step | flow module + `CONTRACT` | `flows` lint + FLOW.md |
| code (regex, cleaner, validator) | `jav/<layer>/*.py` + test (TDD) | `pytest`, `recall`, golden |

Pitfalls: a `use_cache=False` run **updates** the cache file (it refreshes the reference), except for the determinism measurement,
which runs under `JevAdapter.no_cache_write()`: no reads and no writes, so the reference answer stays untouched.
The old inbox folders contain attachments only; any change to a Choice criterion means a new cache key. The cache
key contains the concrete model version (introduced with `cache_version` 2): even a fully cached golden run
needs one probe a day (an API key is required; offline, the resolution stored in the file is used, with a warning).

## 5. What Claude needs to know to develop efficiently

- Session protocol: `CLAUDE.md` §2 and the [development guide](guides/DEVELOPMENT.md) §4; the hooks back it up
  (SessionStart / PreCompact / Stop / review). Session start: `python -m jav.cli preflight` (pytest + UI checks +
  contract lint + engine matrix + configs + git state + data guard + language guard + Ruff ratchet + the local state snapshot, among
  others). Never copy state numbers anywhere by hand: refer to the generated state snapshot.
- Designing a JEV question: `docs/JEV_PLAYBOOK.md` (facts from the docs, gap analysis per call site, checklist).
- A new JEV question: config JSON + `build_questions` (registry reference `registry:<name>`), the `docs.typesafe.ai` cookbook, a test
  modelled on `tests/test_cfg.py`, a golden run before and after.
- A new flow: module + `CONTRACT` + `TERMINALS` + `build_app` (tracker project in `models.json`) + lint + a golden runner
  modelled on `evals_*.py`; a flow never imports an SDK, only the adapter.
- Search the legacy project first (`CLAUDE.md` §3).

## 6. Execution layer: work package → run → worker

**Plain-language summary.** Documents go into a work package, the package gets its processing settings (or runs with
the default ones), and the run goes through a
durable job queue in the background. Before every paid call, an entry is written and the cost is reserved. After a stop,
the run resumes from the saved step, and a call that has already been paid for is not repeated. To-dos can be resolved
reason by reason, and a live run can only be approved when it has no open to-dos.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  CLI[command line: wp-*, run-*, worker] --> WORK[jav/app/work.py<br/>work package, recipe, readiness, run]
  WORK --> Q[jav/core/queue.py<br/>job queue]
  W[jav/runtime/worker.py<br/>worker] --> Q
  W --> FLOW[Burr process<br/>flow / flow_detect + state persister]
  FLOW --> CALLS[jav/core/calls.py<br/>call log + budget]
  CALLS --> JEV[ai/jev.py]
  CALLS --> GPT[extract_llm.py]
  FLOW --> REV[store: review_reasons<br/>to-dos per reason]
```

| Layer | File | Guarantee | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| Work package, recipe, run | `jav/app/work.py`, `configs/recipes.json` | version conflict → `RevisionConflict`; readiness blockers; fixed input; idempotent start; approval only in live mode and with no open to-dos. A recipe has a `status`: `active` (offered in the UI; one since 1.8.0: `processing`), `internal` (`invoice-extraction`: a given document type, no recognition; command line and tests), `retired` (`document-processing`, `email-intent`: kept for old runs' titles and old assignments; `assign_recipe` raises `RetiredRecipe`). Without an assignment, readiness uses the default processing with its defaults (`assignment_default`) and `start_run` saves them in the same transaction; `migrate_assignments` / `processing-migrate` moves old assignments onto `processing`, keeping the settings (`carried_params`). 151: a finished live run that is not approved (its reasons pinned until it is approved) can be superseded by a person once a later finished live run of the package has all its items (`supersede_info`, `supersede_run`, `/runs/{id}/supersede`): in one transaction the run becomes `cancelled`, its own open reasons `superseded` (`store.close_run_reasons`), and the package's events get `run_supersede` with the later run; the run view carries `supersede` (the offer, with the human work left on the run) and `superseded` (`work_runs.superseded`). 152: the package view carries `supersedable` (`work_runs.supersedable_runs`: every such run of the package, oldest first, with its offer), so the Processing section offers them too; 153: the package list counts them (`older_runs`). 153: the package's next step, its Review and Result tabs and the list's open to-dos follow the current run (`work_views.current_run`: the newest run, passing over runs a person stopped after it), so a later stopped run hides neither the to-dos nor the approval of an earlier finished run; the view carries `current_run` beside `last_run`, and a workflow step stays done when a later live run was stopped. 153: the package's view, its readiness check, the run's view and the run list read through one connection (`store.session`; a package of 468 documents opened in 4 s with 944 connections, now 0.3 s on a copy of the store), and a run's open to-dos are counted by a range on its item flow ids, served by the index `ix_review_reasons_status_run`. 204 (R1i): leaving out a removed item's to-dos reads only the package's removed items (`ix_workpackage_items_removed`; before, every item of the package for each open to-do: the run list took 10.4 s for one run of 10,000 items with 1,000 open to-dos, now 0.014 s; at 100,000 items more than 300 s, now 0.09 s). 153 (review): a package's to-do counts (the list's "incl. earlier runs" column, the package's to-dos, its items table) leave out the reasons another package's runs raised (`work_views.own_reasons`; a measurement's or the command line's still show): a to-do belongs to the document's content, so the same file in another package carried that package's to-dos in. No run closes another's reasons by itself. 160-C S1 (decisions 163 D2, D3): removing an item is virtual and needs a person and a reason (`remove_item(actor=, reason=)`, `/workpackages/{id}/items/{item}/remove` with `reason`, 422 without one); the item keeps `removed_at`, `removed_by` and `removed_reason`, the package's events get `item_remove`, and `restore_item` (`/items/{item}/restore`, event `item_restore`) brings it back. A removed item stays out of readiness and the next run; a run already started keeps it in its frozen input. The `workpackage_items` dataset shows removed items with the `removed` scope (`_removed` per row). 160-C (decision 163 D7): items removed from the package since a run started are left out of that run's release (`work_runs.excluded_items`, `work_runs.released_items`): their to-dos block neither its status nor its approval, the run list does not count them, and the downloads (tables, JSON, Excel, named copies) and the statement package's approval rule leave them out. The approval then needs the count the approver saw (`confirm_removed`; otherwise 409 `not_ready` with the `removed_items` blocker) and records the items left out (`runs.excluded_items`), so a later removal or restore does not change a released result (decision 160 D1); the run view carries `removed_items`. 160-C S2: a person adds documents to an existing package with `add_to_package` (`POST /workpackages/{id}/items`, the response's `added`: added, already in the package, restored, and the names a live run of another package processed): by content, a document already in use changes nothing (no revision step), a removed one is restored, and an actual change is logged (`items_add`) in the same transaction (`add_entries(event=)`); a statement or reconciliation package refuses it (`SURVEYED_KINDS`, 422). `pending_items(wp, mode)`: the items in use that no non-cancelled run of that mode has finished; after a run they are the package's next step (`pending`). 160-C S3: readiness carries `supplement` per mode when a run of that mode finished some of the items in use and others are pending: the pending items' count, their own blockers and warnings (an earlier item's changed source does not hold it up), budget, plan and input hash. `start_run(scope="pending")` (`StartRun.scope`, `run-start --pending`) freezes only those items (`runs.scope = 'pending'`, the runs dataset's Run scope column); it does not start while another run of the package is queued or running (`run_active`), and its idempotency key counts the package's stopped and failed runs of that mode, so a double click gets the same run and a stopped one can be started again. `work_runs.open_live_runs`: finished, unapproved, not stopped live runs no later finished live run repeated on all their items, oldest first. `work_views.current_run` is now a queued or running run, else the oldest open live run, else the 153 rule, so a later run over the pending items or a later trial hides neither the to-dos nor the approval of an earlier live run; the package view carries `open_runs`, Review opens on the current run and names the other open runs with to-dos, and a workflow step is done only with an approved live run and no other live run still going or open (`workflows._live_run_approved`). 168 (plan F-item-list-and-adding): adding is previewed first. `preview_additions` (`POST /workpackages/{id}/items/preview`, the given files or a folder's documents, with `recursive` its subfolders' too, without the output folder of the named copies; `folder_contents` also names the other files it leaves out) marks each document `new`, `already`, `removed` or `repeated`, and `elsewhere` when a live run of another package processed it, without writing anything; the add request takes the same sources and leaves out what the person unticked (`exclude`: new documents; `restore`, when given: the only removed ones to bring back), counted as `left_out`. `remove_items` / `restore_items` (`POST /workpackages/{id}/items/remove|restore`): several items in one revision step and one log event (`items_remove`, `items_restore`), all or nothing. `item_states(wp, items)`: per item the run whose result counts now (approved live > live > trial, a stopped or superseded run does not count) and the state `approved` / `live` / `trial` / `running` / `failed` / `pending`; the `workpackage_items` dataset shows them (State, Counted run, Run started, Result; a removed item reads `removed`). A statement package's survey and add take `recursive` too (`statement_package.expand_folders`: each subfolder read as a folder of its own, a companion PDF folder left to its parent). | `tests/test_work.py`, `tests/test_flexible_package_160c.py`, `tests/test_package_items_168.py`, `tests/test_work_cli.py`, `ui/src/pendingRun160c.test.tsx`, `tests/test_processing_080.py`, `tests/test_supersede_runs_151.py`, `ui/src/supersedeRuns151.test.tsx`, `tests/test_supersede_package_152.py`, `ui/src/supersedePackage152.test.tsx` |
| Package's current result | `jav/app/package_result.py` | 171: per item of a package, the result that counts now, decided in one window query (`counted_results`): the latest finished one of an approved live run, else of a live run, else of a trial run; a stopped or superseded run and an unfinished item do not count. The item list's state (`work_runs.item_states`) and its name (`naming.package_item_names`) both come from that run. `effective_values` is the one merge of a machine result and the latest correction, used by the item result and the correction check (`corrections`), the run export, the reconciliation's and the duplicate search's document readers. 172: `in_use` gives the same for the items in use, in the package's item order. A result table (`emails`, `email_tasks`, `documents`, `datapoints`, `native_facts`, `line_items`, `utility_cost`, `utility_sources`) takes exactly one of a `run_id` or a `workpackage_id` scope (`Dataset.one_of`): a run's table is the run's, as before; the package's current result reads each item in use from its counted run (`datasets.result_parts`), each row's link naming that run, and is cached by the package's fingerprint (the items in use and their order, each item's counted run and tier, each counted run's own fingerprint). A page of the package's table carries the result's mark (`result_mark`), which Approve and release sends back. `/workpackages/{id}/result` carries the views with data (`tables`, `work_views.result_tables(workpackage_id=)`; the file names only when one run gives every item's result, as the named copies are made per run), `/result/export.xlsx` the current result as a workbook (`package_release.current_workbook`) and `/result/utility-cost` its utility report. 174: `counted_documents` gives each document's counted result row across every package it is in use in, in the same order, with a command-line result (a measurement) below a trial and counting only when no run has finished the document; a run that ended without a result row (a to-do before the data points) gives way to an earlier one that left one, and a document removed from every package it was in has none (decision 160 D3). The reconciliation (`reconcile.snapshot`, `statements`), the duplicate search and through it the own-tax scan, the role history (`party_history`) and a statement's relations (`statement_months.item_view`) read these rows (`result_rows`, with `latest_corrections`); before, each read the store's latest result row, a later trial's too. A statement package's rule after an approval reads the approved run's own statements (`run_documents`, `reconcile.run_statements`). The reconciliation workspace's mark includes each run's mode and whether it was stopped, and the removed package items | `tests/test_package_result_171.py`, `tests/test_package_result_names_171.py`, `tests/test_package_result_tables_172.py`, `tests/test_document_result_174.py` |
| Approve and release | `jav/app/package_release.py` (`package_versions`, `package_version_files`), `/api/workpackages/{id}/result`, `/release`, `/versions/{n}/files/{name}`, `python -m jav.cli package-versions-backfill` | 171: one step approves every open live run of a package (`work_runs.approve_run`, each with the reviewed version the person saw) and records a released package version: the approved items, each from its run, and their downloads (workbook, JSON, the CSV tables) stored as files, so a released version's download never changes. 178: the workbook is made with the package's chosen template, a package with utility bills also stores the utility cost's workbook (`utility_cost.xlsx`), and each workbook's file records the template and its version (`template`, `template_version`; empty for a version released before 178: the built-in template). The person's `expected_mark` (the package result's mark) must still hold, and again after the files are made; open to-dos block it before any approval. The view reads `not_released`, `released`, `partly_released` (an item in use without a released result) or `changed_since_release` (a released item removed or replaced). A package approved before gets its first version recorded afterwards (`backfill`, `backfilled_at`). A reconciliation package is approved in its own statement. 186 (plan G-full-move T2d): a logic pack without runs keeps its own approved versions in the same tables: the content the person approved (`content`; NULL for a package's release), its fingerprint (`mark`), who approved it and when, what it locks while it holds (`locks`) and its reopening (`reopened_by`, `reopened_at`, `reopen_note`); each file is stored in the state it was made in (`state`: `released`; a logic pack's own version: `approved`, `earlier` once reopened; part of the file's key, so an older store's files table is rebuilt once with its rows as released files). The logic pack decides what it approves, what blocks it and what its lock refuses; `record_own`, `reopen_own` and `store_own_files` number and store inside its write transaction, `own_versions`, `own_version`, `active_own`, `own_files`, `own_file` and `own_missing_files` read them back. 224 (plan G-process-core P3c): a logic pack approves and reopens through the shared steps, in one order and its write transaction: `approve_own` (the version, the kind's files, the log's `<kind>_approve` entry with the version, the fingerprint and the kind's details) and `reopen_logged` (the reopening, the kind's files of the earlier version, the `<kind>_reopen` entry); the kind checks its blockers and the fingerprint the person saw before, in its own words. `record_own` refuses a second own version while one holds (`OwnVersionHolds`, a revision conflict), so two approvals in a race cannot both hold, whatever the kind (`tests/test_own_approval_224.py`). The release view and the stage bar list a package's released results only. An older store's own version tables of a logic pack are copied in once by its migration (`copy_older_own`, a `meta` mark per table); the older tables keep their rows, read by nothing, for one release. 172 (DECISIONS 172): the interface approves only here, on the package's result (a run's own result can still be viewed); the package's next step is `release` (Approve and release) for a finished live run and also while an approved live run is newer than the latest version (`unreleased_approval`: an approval the way before v1.20.0, or a release stopped after its approvals); the package list's fingerprint includes `package_versions` | `tests/test_package_release_171.py`, `tests/test_package_result_tables_172.py`, `tests/test_package_release_186.py`, `ui/src/resultRelease156.test.tsx` |
| Job queue | `jav/core/queue.py` | dedup key, claiming, attempts + back-off → `dead`, release, stop, handling of orphaned claims at start-up (modelled on the V4 `jobq.py`) | `tests/test_runtime_queue.py` |
| Call log, budget | `jav/core/calls.py` | reservation of an upper bound before the network call (UTF-8 bytes + a fixed overhead per request, growing conversation retries, transport retries; a call above its reservation stops the run's further calls with that provider); 149: a GPT call whose full bound no longer fits the budget left is sent with its lean bound (`invoke(lean=…)`: one request, no validation retry, still a true bound; the call log notes `lean_bound`), so a tight budget is not left unused; failed calls are logged too; an unknown cost stays reserved; 202 (the owner's decision): a definite failure whose cost is certain is settled at it (`known_outcome`): a JEV request refused as too large (`max_tokens_exceeded`) at 0, a GPT answer cut off at its output cap at the tokens of every request of its run; a timeout or a broken connection keeps its maximum (`tests/test_known_failed_cost_202.py`); an uncertain attempt is not repeated; a GPT question (`reusable`, its `request_hash` an `answer_key` of the model, instructions, schema, settings and text: only what GPT is sent, so a change only JEV reads keeps the answer reusable, and the settings fingerprint stays in the ledger, 128) gets an earlier answer to the same question when the run's earlier-answers setting allows it (`use_run(reuse=…)`, `worker.reuse_answers`): cost 0, noted `reused_answer:<id>`, a cached ledger row (`tests/test_gpt_reuse_090.py`); 160-D: a command-line measurement reuses too (`measurement(reuse=…)`): a golden measurement and a processing command follow their cache setting (`--no-cache` asks again), a determinism measurement never reuses, a reused answer needs no budget, and the measurement prints its live calls and reused answers (`scope_calls`, `tests/test_measurement_reuse_160d.py`); only an uncertain attempt can be settled by hand (one conditional write; a reserved one may still be running), and a late answer does not overwrite a settlement; a successful step is replayed; every reservation names its holder, a lock file next to the store (`call-holders/`) that the reserving process keeps locked for its lifetime, and a worker start makes uncertain only the reservations of holders that have stopped (or of reservations without a holder), so a command-line measurement waiting for its answer keeps its reservation (`holder`, `recover_uncertain`); 204 (R1i): the look for an earlier overrun in a budget is one index lookup (`ix_invocations_scope_note`); the budget sum stays exact (`Decimal`), a running total is left to the budget redesign; 231 (C2a): a `repeatable` call (`invoke(repeatable=True)`: free and without effect, a connector's lookup; noted `repeatable`) is never uncertain: an answer lost after sending, or a reservation its stopped process left open, makes it failed | `tests/test_runtime_calls.py`, `tests/test_connectors_231.py`, `tests/test_runtime_adapters.py`, `tests/test_reservation_holder_092.py`, `tests/test_gpt_reuse_key_128.py`, `tests/test_lean_reservation_149.py`, `tests/test_run_scaling_204.py` |
| Connectors | `jav/core/connectors.py` (`ask`, `of`, `catalogue`, `use_transport`, `ConnectorUnavailable`, `ConnectorError`), `configs/connectors.json` | 231 (plan stage C, C2a; glossary: Connector): each external service described once as data: its address, the fixed headers of its requests, what leaves the machine (`sends`), the price of one question, the timeout, the retries of an unreached service (`retry_wait_s` apart), the questions a minute it may get and the largest answer read; `ask` keeps to the description and writes every attempt into the call log under the connector's name (run `connector:<name>`, step `<purpose>:<random>`, the answer's size and digest as its receipt, not its content); its failure is named (`unreachable` with the transport's error class, `too_large`, `busy`: over the rate limit, nothing sent). Only lookups so far (`kind: lookup`, asking without changing the other system): a free one is a repeatable call, a priced one reserves its price from the running work's budget and is refused outside one; a message that changes another system goes through the process core's handover record instead. A test never reaches the network: the test setup installs a refusing transport for every connector. The first connector is the MNB exchange rate service (`mnb_rates`); 231 D-d5: two invented lookups on this machine for the tests and the trial (`invented_company_register`, `invented_sanctions_list`, answered by `jav/devtools/invented_services.py` from `configs/golden_registers.json`, as a local web service with `invented-services` or inside the process with its `transport`) | `tests/test_connectors_231.py` |
| Worker | `jav/runtime/worker.py` | stable identifier + Burr state persistence (`burr_state.sqlite` next to the store); resumption from the next step; stop at a step boundary; a changed source is rejected. 204 (R1i): the run's status after each item (`work_runs.refresh_run_status`) is read from which states the run's jobs are in (`queue.states`, one index lookup each) and through the run's state index (`ix_runs_state`, as its row holds the frozen input before those columns), and an unchanged status is not written again, so it no longer reads every item of the run (31 ms per refresh at 10,000 items; now about 0.1-0.3 ms at any size); the run's size counts only once none of its jobs is queued or claimed (an item without a job, the run's own open to-dos) | `tests/test_runtime_worker.py`, `tests/test_work_cli.py`, `tests/test_run_scaling_204.py` |
| Flow registry | `jav/flows/__init__.py` (`FlowSpec`, `get`, `specs`), `jav/runtime/worker.py`, `jav/devtools/admin.py` (`flow_lint_targets`), `jav/app/work_readiness.py` (`native_flow`, `_item_arm`) | 173: one entry per flow under the name stored in every run's frozen recipe (`invoice`, `doc_detect`, `email`, `native`, `statement_table`; `document` is the recipe flow made of the stages `doc_detect` and `invoice`; the two learning flows have a lint graph only). An entry names the flow's module, how the worker builds its graph for one item, how the contract lint builds one, whether the flow takes its budget and path from the native reader's estimate, and where an item's document type is known before the run (the recipe's settings or an earlier detection). The worker's flow map and building, the contract lint's targets and the run plan's and budget's native branches read it instead of their own lists; an unknown name is `UnknownFlow` | `tests/test_flow_registry_173.py`, `tests/test_characterisation_171.py` |
| To-dos | `jav/core/store.py` `review_reasons` | each reason is opened and closed by the step that raised it; human decisions carry an author | `tests/test_review_reasons.py` |
| One connection per operation | `jav/core/store.py` (`session`, `one_connection`); marked operations: the worker's item (`jav/runtime/worker.py` `process`), a run's start, state, open to-dos, resolution and approval (`jav/app/work_runs.py`), a package's read, creation and creation from a folder (`jav/app/work.py`), the reconciliation's snapshot (`jav/domains/finance/reconcile_store.py`), its package's workspace, context, accounts, creation, allocation and marks (`jav/domains/finance/reconcile_package.py`), its statement (`jav/domains/finance/reconcile_statement.py`) and AI proposals (`jav/domains/finance/reconcile_ai.py`), an item's result (`jav/app/corrections.py`), a workflow's creation, view and step (`jav/app/workflows.py`), the quote draft's view (`jav/domains/quotes/quote_draft.py`) | The list views read through one connection (`session`). 203: an operation marked `one_connection` runs in one session (an item in the worker opened 35-37 connections, each open and close a few milliseconds on Windows; the test suite opened about 74 000). Each `connect()` block entered outside a transaction still commits at its end, or rolls back on error, so what a failed operation's finished steps wrote stays, as before. 207 (plan G-process-core R1k): a block entered inside an enclosing block's transaction is a savepoint of it (`_shared_block`): it neither commits nor ends the write lock, and its error undoes only its own part; `begin_immediate` does nothing there. Before, it committed the enclosing work: the run start released its lock right after reading the package. A module registered inside a transaction gets its tables as part of it; the full set-up, which commits, waits for the next block outside one. A session's connection is used only on the thread that opened it: a library that copies the caller's context into its own thread gets a connection of its own (before, it got the session's and failed). In read-only mode a session opens nothing (a read-only connection is never shared) | `tests/test_store_session_203.py`, `tests/test_store_transactions_207.py`, `tests/test_view_speed_153.py` |
| Partial OCR | `jav/ai/policy.py` `ocr_coverage_reasons` | a skipped page is always a to-do | `tests/test_ocr_coverage.py` |
| Email receiver | `jav/domains/email/ingest_server.py`, `jav/domains/email/emails.py`, `jav/core/path_text.py` | paths stay inside the root (153, 167: a network path, a drive-relative one or, from the bridge, anything but a plain relative path after `/data/` is refused by its text before it is resolved), size and structure limits, mandatory key (without one it prints a one-time key at start-up), requests from a browser are rejected, replay protection by content hash | `tests/test_ingest_security.py`, `tests/test_container_path_167.py` |
| Cost view | `jav/app/costs.py`, datasets `run_items` (cost columns), `runs` (`cost_usd`) and `package_costs`, the run view (`costs`), `ui/src/views/Runs.tsx` (`PlanVsActual`), `ui/src/views/PackageCosts.tsx` | the cost per item, run and package is read from what is already recorded, nothing is stored twice: the call log (a flow's call by the item's flow identifier, an Azure recognition by the document's fingerprint in its step) and the ledger (questions answered from an earlier JEV answer or an earlier Azure recognition, 0 USD); per provider and model: paid calls, failed and open among them, the known cost, and apart the reserved maximum of the calls without a known cost (not added to the cost); the pre-start overview is saved with the run (`runs.plan`) and each provider is shown as expected / possible / not expected next to the actual calls, with a warning when a provider was called against the overview | `tests/test_cost_view_082.py`, `ui/src/costView082.test.tsx` |
| Operational safety net | `jav/runtime/worker.py`, `jav/core/queue.py`, `jav/domains/email/mailbox.py`, `jav/app/app_settings.py`, `jav/app/work.py` | the worker loop does not stop on an unexpected error (the job is closed and the error logged); an orphaned job is restarted at most 3 times and then marked dead; a stop requested while the worker was down is completed at start-up; a stop cancels the run's queued jobs and asks the running one to stop in one transaction (so the worker cannot finish the running item and refresh the run, or claim the next item, half-way through the stop), and a job that fails or is released after a stop request is cancelled instead of going back to the queue, so a stopped run always ends as stopped; an interrupted run start is completed on retry and is not "done" until then; emails that arrived before a download was interrupted still go into a package; a watched folder marks a file as seen only after it has been added successfully, and only one process scans a folder at a time; PDFium calls run under a lock | `tests/test_stability_063.py`, `tests/test_cancel_status_082.py` |
| Log, backup | `jav/core/applog.py`, `jav/runtime/backup.py` | permanent, rotating log (`runs/logs/`); store backup even while runs are in progress, with an integrity check (`python -m jav.cli backup`) | `tests/test_stability_063.py` |
| Daily backup, store thinning | `jav/runtime/backup.py`, `jav/flows/persistence.py`, `jav/runtime/worker.py`, `scripts/backup-task.ps1`, `configs/service.json` `backup` | scheduled daily backup, verified copy to a second location, status file and UI warning; the internal working documents (`internal-docs.zip`) and, since 231, the machine's local settings with the keys (`local-env.zip`, `with_env`) in the backup too; of a closed item's process states only the last one is kept (Burr `load` also reads only that one); `burr-prune` for a one-off thinning and compaction of the old store | `tests/test_ops_064.py`, `tests/test_backup_env_231.py`, `ui/src/ops064.test.tsx` |

**Limits.** The call log and the budget only apply on the path that runs through the worker (`calls.use_run`). The
command-line measurements (`golden`, `determinism`, `run` …) make their calls outside it, so that measurements stay
comparable with earlier ones; they do log failed GPT calls. As many workers run as `configs/service.json` `workers` allows (one by default),
each in a slot guarded by a lock (section 7; several workers: stage E). The email process is part of the same processing (the `processing` recipe chooses the flow per item kind, section 11), and it includes the attachments.

## 7. Local service

**Plain-language summary.** The user interface and the command line use the same gateway: a service that can only be
reached from the local machine. It does not run anything itself; it only queues work and reads from the store. The
separate worker does the processing, so closing the browser or the service does not stop a run. A request that comes
from another machine or a web page, is too large, is not JSON or contains unknown fields never reaches the business
operation. Documents can be added from any existing local folder; a setting can restrict this to permitted
folders. Field corrections are versioned: a save based on an outdated version is rejected.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  UI["user interface"] --> API[jav/service/api.py + jav/service/api_*.py<br/>local service]
  CLI[command line: wp-*, run-*] --> VIEWS
  API --> VIEWS[jav/app/work_views.py<br/>shared views]
  API --> CORR[jav/app/corrections.py<br/>field correction]
  VIEWS --> WORK[jav/app/work.py]
  CORR --> WORK
  WORK --> Q[job queue]
  W[worker + lock] --> Q
```

| Layer | File | Guarantee | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gateway | `jav/service/api.py`, `jav/service/service_shared.py` (204: the input types, the actor and path checks), `configs/service.json` | loopback address only; `Host` and `Origin` checks; write requests must be JSON; body size limit, also for chunked transfer; Pydantic schemas that reject unknown fields; identifier patterns; folders and files: an existing path (links resolved), anywhere — with the `restrict_paths: true` setting, only under a permitted root | `tests/test_api.py` |
| Shared entrance (229, plan structure stage 1, SZ2b) | `jav/service/service_shared.py` (`access_guard`, `action_of`, `acting`), the app's dependency in `jav/service/api.py` (`_new_app`), `jav/core/access.py`, `jav/core/identity.py` (`anonymous`) | every address, the service's and every domain's, goes through one entrance: it names the operation (its theme and function, e.g. `workpackages.approve_run`, `finance.reconcile.<function>`, `service.health`; one name per operation), knows who acts (the person `X-Actor` names, or nobody: `anonymous:request`), keeps it on the request and asks the authorisation check, which allows everything today; a refusal is 403 `not_authorised`. It reads the header itself, so the service's description is unchanged; an operation that needs a person still refuses a request without one (`human_actor`), and (SZ2c-3) gets the actor the entrance knows, whose stable id it records | `tests/test_service_access_229.py`, `tests/test_actor_ids_229.py` |
| Browser security headers | `jav/service/api.py` (`_SecurityHeaders`, the outermost layer) | on every response, including the gateway's rejections: framing forbidden (`X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`), `nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, COOP/CORP `same-origin`; on the UI's HTML a content security policy that allows only the app's own origin (`data:` for the embedded font and the favicon, `blob:` for downloads); `/api/` responses are `no-store` (page images included), with a locked-down policy; on the source PDF the policy only forbids framing (because of the browser's PDF viewer) | `tests/test_security_headers_071.py` |

Each module names the actions it records in a package's or a workflow's log, and the log refuses an action no module named (`UnknownEvent`), so every action has its label (the package log's in `configs/datasets.json` `activity_action`, the workflow log's in the interface). "My work today" reads its registered sources in their order; since 175 (the owner's decision) they include a person's duplicate decisions, statement choices and own workflow steps (a log's own actions are labelled by action, `workflow_<action>`; a step the workflow starts by itself is recorded for "<maker> (auto)" and is not the person's); 231 (stage U, U2d; the owner's decisions of 2026-10-28: one work list, the cases) and a person's own steps and decisions on the cases, read from the process core's history (`case_<kind>`: a case started, a task decided, a step started again or out of order, a case closed, held, resumed or reopened, a follow-up started, a task assigned or given a deadline; the machine's work is nobody's day; `activity.CASE_ACTIONS`), each named by its case, as the before/after snapshot reads their days too. A run's reviewed version is made of the registered decision kinds (corrections, task-proposal decisions, a native result's publication, duplicate, reconciliation and statement decisions), each part present wrapping the earlier ones; a kind a logic pack adds joins the reviewed version and the run's dataset fingerprint, and a run without it keeps both. The task kinds of an email's task proposal come from the settings only, and the decisions on a proposal and on a duplicate pair are one list each. 209 (plan G-process-core R2c): a source or a decision kind that reads a domain's tables is the domain's: the email domain registers the task decisions, done marks and mailbox downloads of "My work today" right after the closed to-dos, and the task decisions of the reviewed version after the corrections, with their own part of the run's dataset fingerprint (`DecisionKind.fingerprint`, the form it always had); the finance domain registers its duplicate decisions and statement choices right before the workflows (`register_source(..., after=, before=)`), so the order is the one the shared module had, whichever domain registers first | `tests/test_activity_kinds_175.py`, `tests/test_characterisation_171.py`, `tests/test_activity_labels_171.py` |
| Shared decision handler | `jav/core/decisions.py` (`Kind`, `register`, `kinds`, `record`, `state`, `states`, `check` (227: its `RevisionConflict` is the work package's `work.RevisionConflict`), `run_state`, `package_state`, `check_package`, `history`, `run_approved`, `decided_in_approved_run`, `one_at_a_time`; table `decision_journal`); the kinds registered by `jav/domains/email/email_items.py` (`email_task`; until 231 C3h `mailbox.py`), `jav/domains/finance/duplicates.py` (`duplicate`), `jav/domains/finance/statement_months.py` (`statement_choice`), `jav/domains/email/email_routing.py` (`email_routing`), `jav/app/outputs/templates.py` (`output_template`), `jav/domains/finance/reconcile_package.py` (`reconcile_allocation`, `reconcile_mark`, `reconcile_pair`, `reconcile_source`, `reconcile_exclusion`; `DECISION_KINDS`, `journal_state`, `page`); `DecisionState` in `jav/service/api.py`, `Decided` in `jav/domains/finance/api_reconcile.py` | 187 (plan G-full-move T2c): what every kind of a person's decision shares. Each kind keeps its current decisions in its own table with its structure unchanged, so a run's reviewed version and every stored approval stay the same. Every change also goes into one journal (`decision_journal`: kind, key, action, value, package, run, who, when, why), written by the owner inside its write transaction, so no earlier decision is lost (before 187 a changed task decision or duplicate decision overwrote the earlier one). A decision's state is its latest journal row (0 before any): a view gives it as `decision_state`, the interface sends it back as `expected_state`, and a different one is `work.RevisionConflict` (409); a request without one is not compared. The checks inside the transaction are shared: an approved run's decisions are frozen (`run_approved`), and so is a pair decided in another run that is approved by now (`decided_in_approved_run`; 187: a statement choice too, which before could change that run's reviewed version). A run's dataset fingerprint adds its latest journal row (a task's done mark was not part of it), and a run without one keeps its fingerprint. `one_at_a_time` makes the service run one change of a scope at a time, for a kind whose change does part of its work in transactions of its own: the email routing adds a document to a package or makes a new package before it records the decision, so a routing package's changes run one after the other, and the decision's write refuses an attachment decided meanwhile (187: before, two acceptances at once could make two packages and the later one overwrote the first one's decision). A reconciliation's page changes many decisions of one package, so its state is the package's: the latest journal row of its five kinds (`package_state`), given with every workspace and the statement (`journal_state`, read fresh beside the kept workspace; the workspace's `decision_state` still says what its decisions read) and compared inside the write transaction beside the reconciliation's own check of what the request read (`_write`); revoking a pair decision checks its run's approval inside that transaction (before, outside it). An accepted source is journaled when it is new. 215 (plan G-process-core P2c): a decision that settles to-dos closes them in its own transaction (a duplicate pair's decision, a statement pair's choice, an email's task decisions, an email's corrected intent, a document's confirmed field; before, a second transaction, so a stop between the two left the decision with its to-dos open); the operation runs on one connection, so the to-do's own blocks are savepoints of it | `tests/test_decisions_187.py`, `tests/test_decision_todo_one_transaction_215.py` |

The proposal is the open package whose scope fits (Scopes and package scopes), else a new package for the party, the day's calendar month and the role. A blocker keeps an attachment from a proposal: not read yet, read but without data (183), no own party, an unclear direction, an invoice between two own parties, no date, a suspicious email (the attachment's to-do, 154). An incoming invoice in a sent email (a forwarded one) is proposed with a note (183). A person accepts (one, several, every clear one: one new package per party, month and role), puts an attachment into a package of a role it fits, leaves it unrouted, or takes a decision back (recorded in the package's log, `routing_*`). Accepting adds the document to the package (`work.add_to_package`, a statement package by its own survey) and starts no run. The package is done when every attachment it can route is decided; it has no release and no download. An email package's page offers it (the email intent's proposal: a person starts it); the template `mailbox_routing` runs it after a mailbox download, the routing step taking the email packages of the steps it waits for (`StepStart.packages`); neither needs a party or a period. A workflow's scope is made of registered parts (the own party and the period); a template asks for the parts its steps' kinds need (`workflows.template_needs`), so the three reconciliation templates need a party and a period as before, and a template whose steps need neither makes a workflow without them (its name then the template's title, its repeat the same scope). An older store's workflows table is rebuilt once with the party and the period optional and a `scope` column for further parts, its rows kept. A package that can collect documents for a role has a scope: a person's (any processing package; recorded in its log), else its logic pack's (a statement package: its party, its period, bank statements), else its workflow step's (the workflow's party and period, the step's `role` in the template). The roles (incoming invoices, outgoing invoices, bank statements) are the finance domain's, each with the documents that fit it by type and direction and the package kinds that hold it. `fitting` gives the open packages whose scope fits a document (the email routing's targets). A party merge moves a package's scope; a deleted package's scope goes with it. 186 (plan G-full-move T2b): one store for every package's scope, a person's and a logic pack's alike (`workpackage_scopes`): the party, the period and the role in their columns, a logic pack's further parts beside them as JSON (a reconciliation's `accounts`, a routing package's `emails`), and a revision that steps with each change. A logic pack writes its package's scope with `save` inside its own write transaction, with its own checks and log entry (a reconciliation passes the person's `expected_revision`; a different one is `work.RevisionConflict`), and reads it with `stored`; a logic pack's own scope comes first in `scope_of`, and a person can neither give nor take one. The one party reference, the deletion with the package and the cache marks therefore cover every logic pack. An older store's own scope table of a logic pack (`statement_scopes`, `reconcile_scopes`, `email_routing_scopes`) is copied in once by the logic pack's own migration (`copy_older`, a `meta` mark per table, in one write transaction checked again inside it); the older table keeps its rows, read by nothing, for one release; a package found in two places stops the copy (`ScopeCopyError`) instead of losing one | `tests/test_scopes_181.py`, `tests/test_package_scope_186.py`, `ui/src/views/packageScope181.test.tsx` |
