The interface: Settings › Output templates (`ui/src/views/settings/OutputTemplatesPanel.tsx`: upload with the system's file picker, the check's problems by cell with an unknown marker's reason in words, each version's state, activation, retirement, preview and file, the markers to copy) and the chooser beside each download (`ui/src/components/TemplateChoice.tsx`: the full Excel package, the utility cost's own download, the reconciliation's draft; shown only when an own template can be chosen, a retired choice said) | `tests/test_output_templates_178.py`, `tests/test_output_wiring_178.py`, `tests/test_output_markers_179.py`, `ui/src/views/settings/outputTemplates179.test.tsx` |
| Own Word templates | `jav/app/outputs/docx.py` (`check`, `fill`, `shown`, `field_name`, `MEDIA`), `jav/app/outputs/templates.py` (`engine`, `media`, `extension`), `jav/app/outputs/builtin.py` (`register_word`), `jav/app/outputs/fill.py` (`Lookup`, `as_decimal`, shared) | 196 (plan F-Word-output W1, DECISIONS 196): an output whose contract says `format: docx` is a Word document. A Word template uses the markers of an Excel one and is uploaded, versioned, previewed and chosen the same way; the store checks and fills it by the output's format. Filling works on the package's XML: the body, the headers and footers, the text boxes and the notes; a table row holding a table's markers repeats per row with its formatting, so does a paragraph outside a table (a list); without rows it goes, and a table left without rows goes too. Only the text nodes a marker spans change, so the text around a marker keeps its formatting; the value takes its first character's. Values are text formatted by code (a non-breaking space between thousands and a decimal comma, a date as 2026. 10. 22.; with the output's `language` value `en`, 1,234.50 and 22 October 2026), a line break becomes a break, a character XML cannot hold is left out, and a value is never read as a marker. The check refuses a file that is not a Word document or too large, macros, embedded objects, imported content (`altChunk`), an external relationship other than a web link or the attached template, a field not listed in `configs/outputs.json` `templates.word_fields` (a calculation above all), an unknown marker, two tables' markers in one row, a value marker in a table's row, the months, a vertical merge in a table's row, a repeated row inside another, or no marker; the attached template is a warning and is removed when filled. A Word output's built-in template is registered by its domain and filled like an own one. Ported from the legacy `sidecar/app/render/docx_render.py` | `tests/test_word_output_196.py` |
| Code loaded at start | `jav/runtime/preload.py`, `jav/runtime/worker.py`, `jav/service/api.py` (`serve`) | the worker and the local service import every module of the application (not the three stand-alone measurement tools, `preload.EXCLUDED`) when they start, so a running process is one consistent state of the code it started from: a module changed on disk later (development in the same working tree) cannot mix in through a deferred import; a module that cannot be imported stops the start (`PreloadError`); 231 (an incident of 2026-10-25): the configs are frozen then too (`cfg.freeze`: their list and contents), so a config that appears, changes or goes in the folder later, or a cleared cache, changes nothing in the process, and `/api/health` names what the folder holds differently (`config_drift`, `cfg.drift`; None in a command or a test, which read the folder) | `tests/test_preload_091.py`, `tests/test_config_freeze_231.py` |
| Start-up | `scripts/dev.ps1 start/status/stop` | service + the configured workers (one by default) in the background, logs under `runs/dev/` | manual test |
| Folder and file picker | `jav/service/local_picker.py`, `jav/service/picker_dialog.py`, `configs/service.json` `picker` | `POST /api/local/pick-folder` and `/api/local/pick-files` open the operating system's own picker on the service's machine (the browser is on the same machine). The dialog runs in a separate process, one at a time (409 `picker_busy`); one left open past `timeout_s` counts as cancelled; no window possible → 503 `picker_unavailable`. The chosen path is checked like a typed one (403 `forbidden_path` under `restrict_paths`) | `tests/test_folder_browse_081.py`, `ui/src/folderBrowse081.test.tsx` |
| Package from a folder | `jav/app/work.py` `create_from_folder` | the folder's direct PDFs, or with `recursive` those of every subfolder; the output folder of the named copies is left out, and so is a link pointing outside the folder; in the views a subfolder document is titled with its path below the folder | `tests/test_folder_browse_081.py` |

**Error codes:** 404 unknown identifier · 409 version conflict, cannot be started or cannot be approved · 413 body too large ·
415 not JSON · 422 invalid input or missing author · 403 another machine, foreign origin or forbidden folder. Human
operations require the `X-Actor` header: decisions (approval, correction, closing a to-do) and also creating
and changing a package, the recipe, and starting or stopping a run or the worker. The machine-readable list of endpoints
on a running service: `/api/openapi.json` (the clickable `/api/docs` is switched off because it would load
program code from an external host).

**Limits.** There is no login and no permission system (a single-user local tool). We follow the V4 endpoint names
(`workflow`, `readiness`, `start`, `runs`); the work package resource is called `workpackages` instead of the V4 `intake-batches`. The report
endpoint returns the run's per-item result (machine data, correction, merged value); the export and the utility-cost report
are described in section 10 (Reports row).

## 8. User interface

**Plain-language summary.** The browser UI opens at the local service's address (`http://127.0.0.1:8930/`), with no
separate server. The main menu is **Work packages** (*Munkacsomagok*) + **Settings** (*Beállítások*) (section 10, UI structure row).
The UI contains no business rules. The service makes every decision (version, readiness, approval); the UI only displays
and forwards. Corrections are made next to the source document and are not lost when a save fails.

| Part | File | What it does | Behavioural guarantee (test) |
|---|---|---|---|
| App shell | `ui/src/App.tsx`, `ui/src/styles.css` | sidebar (two menu items), worker status, **Who is working?** (*Ki dolgozik?*) (with a non-empty name list, choosing from the list is mandatory; a **My work today** (*Mai munkám*) link; 216: before it **Waiting for me** (*Rám vár*) with the person's count of open live tasks and anyone's, an attention count above zero, refreshed every 30 s) | `ui/src/users.test.tsx`, `ui/src/waiting216.test.tsx` |
| Routing | `ui/src/route.ts`, `ui/src/hooks.ts` | the selected package, run and item are in the URL (bookmarkable) | a package that has disappeared is not replaced by another one; an old response does not overwrite a newer one |
| Work packages | `ui/src/views/Workpackages.tsx`, `WorkpackageDetail.tsx` | list, creation from a folder or from files, **Items** (*Tételek*), Processing (processing settings, readiness with the pre-start overview, trial or live start) | on a version conflict the view reloads and the settings are kept |
| To-dos | `ui/src/views/ReviewWorkspace.tsx`, `ui/src/review/FieldPanel.tsx` | item list, source PDF, own and earlier to-dos (a field's to-dos at the field, the document's at the top), resolution per reason, ✓ / ✗ per field, field correction; 150: an earlier reason carries `trial` (`work_runs.items_reasons`: raised by a trial run, `work_runs.trial_runs`), and the review page leaves those out unless the person's switch is on (`ui/src/review/trialReasons.ts`; the run items' hidden earlier count leaves them out too) | the working copy survives network errors and conflicts |
| Run | `ui/src/views/Runs.tsx` | list, detail view with live updates during a run, budget bar, job queue, call log expanded, stop, approval | – |

**Technology.** React 19, Vite 8, TypeScript 5.9; tests: Vitest 4 + Testing Library (jsdom). 137 npm packages in total (2026-09-30),
no component library. The font (Geist) comes from a local package, so no internet access is needed. The build goes into `ui/dist/`
(git-ignored), and the service serves it at the root. The browser always re-requests the entry HTML. For development,
`npm run dev` (port 5173) forwards `/api` calls to the service. Preflight runs the UI's type check and tests if
`ui/node_modules` is installed.

**Limits.** The **Who is working?** name is not a login (there is no password) but the active user: with a non-empty name list, the service accepts only a name on the list for human operations. The Hungarian
field names are in a shared label configuration; a test checks that every field of every type pack has one. Section 9
describes how the source is displayed; the browser's PDF viewer has been retired.

## 9. Source-grounded review

**Plain-language summary.** When a run reads the text, it also saves where each word is (the word layer), and for every
extracted field it computes where the field is on the document (the source location). The review UI draws a box around
the selected field on the document's page image. It also shows the other candidates, and a field can be filled from words
selected on the image. All of this happens in code, without AI calls. If the location is ambiguous, there is no box,
only an explanation: no box is better than a wrong box.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  LOAD[load_pdf / ocr_pdf] -->|word boxes| SL[(source_layers)]
  DR[decide_route] --> G[ground<br/>jav/ai/grounding.py] --> SAVE[save<br/>datapoints.provenance]
  SL --> G
  SAVE --> API[/api/runs/…/items/…<br/>provenance, source/]
  SL --> W[/…/words/]
  PDF[source document] --> IMG[/…/pages/n.png/]
  API --> UI[PageViewer + FieldPanel]
  W --> UI
  IMG --> UI
```

| Part | File | What it does | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| Word layer | `jav/documents/source_layer.py`, `jav/documents/pdf.py`, `jav/documents/ocr.py` | page-relative boxes from the reader's set of words (text layer, local OCR, Azure), with a content-based identifier; the lines and the JEV requests are unchanged | `tests/test_source_layer.py` |
| Source location | `jav/ai/grounding.py`, `configs/grounding.json` | S path: the selected candidate in its own line (text wrapped onto the following lines is followed in the same column) + the other candidates with their probabilities; if it cannot be found anywhere, an approximate box on the chosen line (`approximate`); search: comparison by kind, label context (V4 dictionary), part of a larger number excluded, names, addresses and texts over up to `multi_line_max_lines` lines of one column; an address also with its words in another order inside one column block (`address_unordered`) and without regard to punctuation; an identifier glued to a label word (never inside a longer number); several identifiers listed in one field found part by part (`search_parts`); a currency sign glued to an amount; a country's code against its printed name (`countries`); a label belongs to the first value after it, so a label with its own value beside it labels neither the lines below it nor the words beyond its value (for words, not for amounts and dates); a party heading (seller, buyer) also supports that party's address, tax number and country; several positions → no box, the positions become alternatives. The code's own source check of the G path without JEV (`code_verify`, also the fallback when JEV is unavailable) asks the same search (`printed`) when its line search finds nothing, so a field with a box is never reported as not printed; since 160 the JEV verification asks the same search before it reports a field unsupported (`jev:unsupported`), its evidence lines and questions unchanged (`tests/test_verify_word_layer_160.py`) | `tests/test_grounding.py`, `tests/test_grounding_frames_092.py` |
| Process step | `jav/flows/flow.py` `ground` | `decide_route → ground → save`; on an error the run continues without a source location | contract lint, `test_flow_run_saves_provenance` |
| Correction with a location | `jav/app/corrections.py` | selected words (`sources`) with a version; the valid location: manual > search for the corrected value > machine; the old box of a corrected field is only an alternative | `tests/test_api.py` |
| Endpoints | `jav/service/api.py` (normalisation, display settings), `jav/service/api_workpackages.py` (page image), `jav/service/api_items.py` (word layer), `jav/service/api_settings.py` (settings), `jav/documents/page_image.py` | page image (PNG, 72–200 dpi, hash-protected), word layer, normalisation, settings | `tests/test_api.py` |
| UI | `ui/src/review/` | page image, box in the band's colour, clicking on the image, alternatives, selection (word, rectangle), working-copy store, splitter, keyboard shortcuts | `ui/src/behaviour.test.tsx` |

**Limits.** Old runs and documents read from the old OCR cache have no word layer (the layer is built during the
run). Line-item positions on the image: section 10, Boxes and line-item positions row. A correct box does not mean accuracy: the
box shows where the value comes from, not that the value is correct.

## 10. Unified document types

**Plain-language summary.** All 23 document types of the legacy project are complete type packs. After the broad category,
recognition picks the detailed type, extraction runs with that type's pack, and old results can be imported for
comparison.

| Element | File | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Pack format | `jav/ai/typepack.py`, `jav/ai/models.py` | `list` field with an item description (`list_fields`), `boolean`, enumerated values (`enums`; a violation is a to-do); `arms`, `parent`, `auto_detect`, `detect` (old keywords) |
| Work packages, recipes, readiness, runs | `jav/app/work.py` (sources, packages and their items, hiding, renaming, deleting), `jav/app/work_recipes.py` (recipes and their assignment), `jav/app/work_readiness.py` (readiness, the run's plan, budget, engine and flow per item), `jav/app/work_runs.py` (start, item states and results, to-dos, cancellation, approval, superseding) | 202 (plan G-core-generalisation M3b): the 1 858-line `jav/app/work.py` split by its sections; each moved name is called through its new module (no re-export, so a test's stand-in acts where the code runs). The four modules import each other as modules and resolve names at call time. Every view, item and request is unchanged (before/after comparison and replay) | the work package tests, `tests/test_users.py` (the three modules' clocks) |
| The interface's service client | `ui/src/api/`: `client.ts` (`request`, `ApiError`, the person working), `types.ts` and `work.ts` (the shared records and calls: packages, items, runs, the review, results), `workflows.ts`, `outputs.ts`, `datasets.ts`, `system.ts`, and the domains' `finance.ts`, `quotes.ts`, `email.ts`; `index.ts` puts every theme's calls together as `api` | 202 (plan G-core-generalisation M3c): the 1 622-line `ui/src/api.ts` split by theme, as the service's own modules are. A caller keeps importing from `./api` and calling `api.<call>`; every line of the old file is in one of the new ones. Type check, the interface tests and the main bundle's size (505.69 kB before and after) are unchanged; no screen changed | the interface tests |

| Detailed type | `jav/ai/detect_detail.py`, `configs/callsites/detect_detail.json`, `policy.json detect_detail` | a receipt whose issuer the recogniser judged foreign (the `no` band of its own issuer question) → the foreign invoice pack (`detect_detail.foreign_issuer_pack`, method `issuer`); the category's own pack as the only candidate → that one; a single pack narrower than its category (receipt → the tax authority's receipt, ticket, contract, tax return) → the question below with `none`, so it is never taken blind (`tests/test_detect_single_pack_122.py`); the old anchor score at the start of the document (V4 `anchor_check`), code decides when the lead is clear; otherwise JEV Choice with `none` (without JEV: the same question to GPT, method `gpt`); a type left open = to-do. 149: an uncertain choice between an outgoing and an incoming invoice pack (`detect_detail.direction_sides`, the runner-up on the other side) is settled after the extraction by the direction the own parties alone give (`flow.confirm_detail_by_direction`, `policy.detail_direction`; the worker passes the detection's probabilities on): a match closes the uncertainty to-do, a contradiction is a `detect_detail:direction_mismatch` to-do (`tests/test_detail_direction_149.py`) |
| Recognition without JEV | `jav/ai/gpt_choice.py` (the shared call and probability arithmetic), `jav/ai/detect_gpt.py`, `configs/gpt_detect.json`, `policy.json` `detect.doc_type.gpt` / `detect.detail_type.gpt` | the three questions of the JEV recognition (type, issuer's nationality, language) and the detailed type asked of GPT in one structured request each, with the JEV call site's document excerpt and question texts; each option offered with the first clause of its description; confidence from the answer's token log-probabilities (the product over the chosen value's tokens; an alternative token's branch credited to the one option it leads to); without log-probabilities the confidence is unknown and the type is a `detect:confidence_unavailable` to-do; a failed call (no budget, provider error) is a `detect:gpt_failed` to-do; a type or detailed type that presumes a Hungarian issuer while GPT's own issuer answer is in the `no` band is a `detect:issuer_mismatch` to-do, the type kept (a cross-check in code, `policy.issuer_mismatch_reason`, `policy.json` `detect_issuer`: GPT's type errors are confident, so its confidence cannot flag them); every call through the call log and the OpenAI budget (`tests/test_gpt_detect_086.py`, `tests/test_gpt_type_check_090.py`) |
| Field confidence without JEV | `jav/ai/extract_llm.py` (`extract_scored`), `jav/ai/token_confidence.py`, `jav/flows/flow.py` (`_gpt_confidence` in `ground`), `policy.json` `gpt_field_confidence` | the extraction is a native structured answer with token log-probabilities (the saved answer carries a format mark; an answer saved before has none and is read without probabilities, and the reuse key includes the format); each top-level value is measured on the tokens that print it, quotes included (`joint` = product, `min`, `first`); on the G path without a JEV verification a field's confidence is the configured measure, capped by a failed field check (the source location does not cap it: GPT rewrites values, and in the measurement that cap flagged many right fields for few wrong ones), with its basis (`confidence_basis`) in the source location; without a token probability only a cap below 1 is shown; its own display band (`gpt_field_confidence.bands`, sent with `/api/settings` as `confidence_bands.gpt`; the review picks it by the basis, `bandsFor`); below `review_below` an accounting field (the pack's scored, not informational, and high-stakes fields, less its `info_only` fields: an outgoing invoice's buyer address, 164) with a value and no failed check is a `gpt:low_conf:<field>:<p>` to-do, raised in `decide_route` from the same token probability (`policy.apply_gpt_confidence_policy`), and such a field without a token probability is a `gpt:confidence_unavailable:<field>` to-do (160; on the golden measurement of 2026-10-02 every accounting field with a value had one); a run with JEV never gets it, not even on the code's fallback when JEV does not answer; the JEV path keeps 1 − the strongest flag (`tests/test_token_confidence_091.py`, `tests/test_gpt_extract_logprobs_091.py`, `tests/test_gpt_field_confidence_091.py`, `tests/test_gpt_todo_092.py`, `tests/test_gpt_confidence_unavailable_160.py`) |
| Email intent without JEV | `jav/domains/email/intent_gpt.py`, `configs/gpt_intent.json`, `policy.json` `email.intent.gpt` | the questions of the JEV email-intent call site (the intent among the registered intents, the yes/no signals, the urgency level) asked of GPT in one structured request, with the same email excerpt; each intent offered with its registry description and what it is not for; confidence and P(yes) from the answer's token log-probabilities, as above; the signals route and flag as with JEV (`email.signal`); an unmeasurable confidence is an `intent:confidence_unavailable` to-do, a failed call an `intent:gpt_failed` to-do; a test never sends such a question to OpenAI (the suite's guard in `tests/conftest.py`; `tests/test_gpt_intent_089.py`) |

159: the list's fingerprint and its reconciliation packages' next step use their own 15-minute time slot (`reconcile_package.LIST_KEEP_SECONDS`), so the list no longer builds every reconciliation workspace again every two minutes when nothing has changed (5-6 s on one core, slowing every other request); **Line lists in the UI.** Correcting a `list` field replaces the whole list, and each cell is
checked against the item field's kind and enumerated values (`jav/app/corrections.py` `list_columns`, `_check_list`). The item
result (`item_result`) also returns the list's columns (`lists`) and the pack's checks on the corrected data (`checks`, with `rows` for
errors that point at rows). In the UI the right panel's tabs are **Fields** (*Mezők*) / one tab per list (`ui/src/review/ListTable.tsx`); a
list tab keeps its own image-to-panel ratio. List rows have boxes on the image (the Boxes and line-item positions row). Tests: `tests/test_list_corrections.py`,
the line-list block of `ui/src/behaviour.test.tsx`.

`documents.doc_type` is the broad category and `documents.detail_type` the detailed type; `datapoints.doc_type` is the
pack used for extraction. The old type copies remain as sources (hash-checked); the separate old runner was retired in 204, and every
catalogued type runs from its converted type pack.

## 11. Mailbox reading and scheduling

**Plain-language summary.** In the **Mailboxes** (*Postafiókok*) section of Settings you choose which mailbox to read
and for which period. First you can ask for a free preview of the message count, then start a one-off download or a schedule (default: hourly). The worker does the download with the old Outlook script. New
emails become a work package with the default processing; a person starts the paid processing. Outlook must be running
on the machine.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  UI["Settings → Mailboxes"] -->|preview| API[jav/domains/email/api_mailbox.py /mailbox/count]
  API --> W[scripts/mail_bridge_call.ps1] --> B[outlook_bridge.ps1 -CountOnly]
  UI -->|download / schedule| Q[(job queue: mail_pull)]
  T["worker: tick()"] --> Q
  Q --> F[mailbox.fetch]
  F --> R[temporary receiver, one-time key]
  B2[outlook_bridge.ps1] -->|/ingest/email| R
  R --> I[inbox/&lt;mailbox&gt;/&lt;message&gt;/message.json]
  F --> WP[work package: email items + default processing]
```

| Element | File | What it does | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| Preview, download | `jav/domains/email/mailbox.py` `count`, `fetch` | the script ported from the legacy project (148: it reads the Sent Items folder too, by the sent time, and a sent message's `message.json` has `folder: sent`; a folder name it cannot find is an error, not an empty folder); its project root is `inbox/.bridge` (attachments, "already read" list), not the legacy project; all messages (`-AllEmails`); a work package from the new / changed messages | `tests/test_mailbox.py`, `tests/test_mail_sent_items_148.py` |
| Temporary receiver | `jav/domains/email/ingest_server.py` `make_server(0, token=…, on_ingest=…)` | free port, one-time key; the existing replay protection (identical content = replay) | `tests/test_mailbox.py`, `tests/test_ingest_security.py` |
| Download log, schedule | `jav/domains/email/mailbox.py` (`mailbox_pulls`, `mailbox_schedules`; 185: `JOB`, `TICK`), `jav/runtime/job_kinds.py` (`JobKind`, `Tick`, `register`, `register_tick`, `of`, `kinds`, `ticks`), `jav/runtime/worker.py` | every download is a job-queue task; the worker calls `tick()` on every loop; an error goes to the log and is not retried. 185 (plan G-full-move T1c): the download's kind of job (what processes it, what settles it when it ends without being processed) and the schedules' tick are the email domain's, registered in the worker's registry (`jav/runtime/job_kinds.py`), so the worker names only its own run items | `tests/test_mailbox.py` |
| Email item | `jav/app/work.py` `add_items(kind="email")`, `jav/app/work_runs.py` `review_subject`; `configs/recipes.json` `processing`; `jav/domains/email/flow_email.py` (`run_id`, state persistence) | item = the email's `message.json`; the subject of the to-dos is the email's identifier | `tests/test_mailbox.py` |
| UI | `ui/src/domains/email/Mailbox.tsx` (231 C3e), `ui/src/review/EmailReview.tsx` | form, preview, download, schedules, log; in the review workspace, the email and its intent | `ui/src/behaviour.test.tsx` |

Limitation: the download runs in the worker's thread (possibly for minutes), and document items make no progress in the
meantime. Manual intent correction and attachment processing are described in section 10 (Emails in the processing
row): a PDF attachment runs in its email's package with the same processing, not in a separate document work package.

## 12. Data checks, limits and safeguards

**Plain-language summary.** The system is deliberately strict in several places. It checks tax numbers against
recognised formats and the check digit, it does not claim false certainty for a field with no candidates, a damaged
glyph does not turn into a negative amount, and a document without a type pack gets a to-do. The
input limit stops documents that are too large, and the PDF parsers run in a separate helper process with a time and
memory limit, so a broken PDF cannot hang the worker or the service. On the code side the data guard protects what goes to GitHub, on the
browser side the security headers protect the UI, and the running version is visible. The full security picture:
[security notes](SECURITY.md).

| Element | File | Guarantee | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tax number | `jav/core/taxid.py`, `jav/ai/validators.py` `tax_id` | recognised format (Hungarian domestic, Hungarian EU VAT (*közösségi adószám*), other EU and some non-EU); the label is stripped; for Hungarian numbers the check digit, VAT code and county code are checked; anything unrecognisable is a to-do, which names the field (`validator:<code>:<field>`, as every failed field check does, so it stands at that field in Review) | `tests/test_taxid_069.py`, `tests/test_properties_067.py`, `tests/test_validator_field_090.py` |
| Field without candidates | `jav/ai/jev_select.py` | a "no estimate" flag and a presence question; an empty field never gets 100% | `tests/test_no_candidate_field_069.py` |

Ported selectively from the legacy `payables.py` 0.1.1. A person's decision (`reconcile_decisions`, one per pair): confirmed (`paid_by`) or rejected with a reason (`not_this`); a rejected pair is never proposed again, and a confirmed pair takes its line and invoice out of every other proposal (one line pays one invoice). 137 (DECISIONS 137): the decisions are made in the reconciliation package; the processing opens no pair to-do on the documents, and the review page's pair panel, the run's Reconciliation view and the command line's `--write` are retired. The panel's decisions of 129–136 stay valid and part of the reviewed version of their run (`decisions_for`, `corrections.review_version`). **Card payments in another currency:** a forint line of a credit card statement (`fx.statement_types`) and an invoice of another currency form a candidate with a signal and a line within the card window around the issue date (`fx.window`, -3 ... +10 days); the invoice amount is converted at the MNB rate of the issue date (`jav/domains/finance/fx.py`), and the pair is proposed when the line is within `finance.json` `reconcile.fx_tolerance` (-2% ... +5%; until 224 in `policy.json`) of it (`fx_within`), listed but not proposed outside it (`fx_outside`, `amount_differs`) or without a known rate (`no_rate`, status `rate_missing`); the card accounts count in a foreign-currency invoice's coverage too; the rates come in the snapshot, so the core stays pure; the command line and the reconciliation package's recomputation fetch a missing rate, a view only reads the stored ones. **Bank lines in forints and another currency (149):** a forint line of a bank account (`fx.bank.statement_types`) meets an invoice of another currency (`meets` → `fx_bank`; not a card purchase printed with its original currency) in the invoice's payment window; it is compared at the MNB rate of each day of `fx.bank.rate_days` in turn (`bank_rate_days`: the invoice's fulfilment day, else its issue day, then the line's booking day), each with its own band (`finance.json` `reconcile.fx_bank_tolerance`, ±0.5% and ±2%), the first within giving `fx_within` and the conversion's `basis`; outside every band (or without a rate) the pair is listed only when the line names the invoice (`named`), and the amounts are compared before the name signals. The snapshot asks for the fulfilment days and every forint bank booking day per foreign currency (`bank_rate_needs`); CHF is a handled currency; the forint bank accounts count in the coverage of an outgoing invoice of another currency (`tests/test_reconcile_fx_bank_149.py`). 151 (engine 1.16.0): a forint bank line naming two or more invoices of one other currency, none proposed alone, is compared with their sum (`convert_combined`: each invoice at its own fulfilment day's rate, then all at the booking day's, each with its band) and within it they are proposed together (`combined`, each pair's `fx` at the deciding day and the group's `combined_fx`); the package allocates such a group in whole, the line split in the ratio of the converted amounts (`split_line`, `reconcile_package._converted_groups`), with a reason outside the band and refused without a rate for every invoice (`tests/test_reconcile_fx_combined_151.py`, `ui/src/domains/finance/reconcile/fxCombined151.test.ts`). **Payment method, equal amounts, learnt names:** the invoice's payment method fitting a configured channel and the line naming its payment app is a signal (`payment_channel`, `payment_channels`: the postal cheque); alone it ties no other amount; a line with exactly an invoice's amount in one currency, in its window, without a signal, is listed for a person (`amount_only`, status `amount_only`, never proposed, not for a converted amount and not beside a proposed pair of its line or invoice); a confirmed pair ties the line's counterparty name without reference numbers (`name_key`) to the supplier's tax number and name (`supplier_keys`), a signal for later pairs (`learned_name`, from `learning.min_confirmations` confirmations; a payment app line teaches nothing). **Looser names, reference numbers, strength (134):** the supplier's name is compared without an address printed after its legal form (`name_words`); a distinctive word of it glued to a suffix on the line, cut short (holding `fuzzy_name.min_prefix_share` of it) or one letter off, or the same whole word leading the name (its brand), is the weak `supplier_name_fuzzy` signal, which alone ties only an equal amount; a word in the names of many suppliers or many lines (a city) ties nothing (`common_words`, computed per snapshot; `signal_context` gives it and the learnt names to every caller); the invoice's customer id, customer code, order or contract number (`invoice_fields.references`) in the memo is the `reference` signal; a utility bill's `supplier_bank_account` is the supplier's account too; every candidate carries a 0-100 `strength` with `days_after_issue` and `by_due` (`strength` weights), which orders the candidates on the pairing page and is no threshold. **A card purchase's original amount (137, engine 1.6.0):** a bank account line that carries `original_amount` and `original_currency` (the Erste export completed from its PDF statement) meets an invoice of that currency (`meets`: `same`, `original`, `fx`; `pair_window`) in the card window and is compared on the original amount exactly (`equal` / `different`, the candidate's `original`), without the MNB rate; it is allocated only in whole, and such an account counts in the coverage of a foreign-currency invoice's card window; without a package (the command line) nothing is registered and the result is unchanged. **141 (DECISIONS 141, the sports club's trial; engine 1.9.0):** a line whose memo names two or more invoices whose amounts left add up to it exactly proposes them together (`amount_relation` `combined`, `combined`: the group in the order of issue, at most `combined.max_invoices`; A profile names the transaction table by its column headers, an account by its title with the currency, and the labels of the account number and the opening and closing balances in the summary; the n-th summary of a title belongs to its n-th table. Reading stays within the configured limits (the workbook archive is inspected first, `readers.limits.inspect_package`) and records its repairs (CSV records in the first cells, UTF-8 read as cp1250, HTML entities). Each account becomes a statement with the fields of the bank statement type packs (the counterparty's name from the description column, the category as the description, a positive amount with its direction); code checks the running balance line by line, the total row and the closing balance, and reports a line it cannot read. The account's key is its number with the currency (one number may hold several currencies) or its title with the occurrence. A work package gets the chosen accounts only (`work.create_from_statement_table`, `work.add_statement_tables`; `POST /statement-tables/survey`, `/statement-tables/workpackage`, `/workpackages/{id}/statement-tables`; since 140 the UI makes such items through the statement package, `jav/domains/finance/statement_package.py`): each account becomes a canonical statement file (`statement_table.derive`, next to the store) and an item of the `statement_table` kind, which the processing recipe routes to `jav/domains/finance/flow_statement_table.py` (verify the frozen file, the type pack's checks, save as the item's document and data points with `doc_type` `statement_table` and `arm` `code`, to-dos for failed checks and unreadable lines) with an empty item budget; the reconciliation reads it like an extracted statement (`configs/reconcile.json` `statement_types`). The type pack `configs/types/statement_table.json` (fields and checks of the bank statement packs, `arms` `code`, `auto_detect` false) is not offered to detection or as a type to extract (`typepack.extractable_keys`). **137 (DECISIONS 137): a ledger export and its PDF companion.** A profile with `layout: ledger` reads an export that lists every line with its own account and statement number (the Erste Excel 2003 XML export, SpreadsheetML read with defusedxml; amounts as machine numbers): one statement per account and statement number, the period from the file name's month. Such an export has no balance and a card line names no merchant, so the profile's `companion` finds the same month's PDF statement next to the export or in a `pdf` subfolder and reads its text layer (the isolated PDF reader) with the profile's patterns, without the repeated page footer and header: the period, the account, the opening and closing balances and every line with its running balance, the merchant and a foreign purchase's original amount and currency. The export's lines are matched to the PDF's by booking date and amount, in order; the export stays the list of lines (partner, partner account, memo, transaction type), the PDF adds the balances, the merchant of a card line and `original_amount` / `original_currency` (type pack 1.1.0); a line on one side only, or a PDF of another account, is a finding (`companion_mismatch` to-do). Without a PDF the balances cannot be checked: one `statement_table:balance_unchecked` to-do, and the statement stays unverified for the reconciliation. A folder is read as a whole (`read` on a folder, at most `limits.max_files` exports; the files no profile reads are listed); the statement file keeps its own export and companion with their fingerprints. The key of a ledger statement is its account digits, the currency and the period's start. **141 (DECISIONS 141): a statement printed as text.** A profile with `layout: text` reads a statement from a PDF's text layer (the isolated PDF reader) or from the bank's fixed-width text export (`.txt`, in one of the profile's `encodings`): the CIB current account and investment client account statement. The profile knows its statement by `signature`; `header` gives the statement number, period, currency, giro and IBAN numbers, the opening and closing balances and the printed totals; the lines between `lines_start` and `lines_end`, without the page breaks, are a first row (booking date, kind, signed amount, running balance), a value date row (the counterparty's account, then the next unlabelled row is the name; a foreign transfer's original amount; or the bank's description of a fee, kept as the memo when no memo is printed) and labelled rows (the counterparty's name and account, the memo). Texts keep single spaces, so the PDF and the text export of a month give the same statement. Code checks the running balance, the printed totals and the closing balance (`parse_text_statement`, `_text_account`); the key is the account digits, the currency and the period's start. A folder's PDF or text file no profile reads is not listed as skipped: a PDF may be a companion or a statement to extract. A second text profile reads the CIB credit card statement: a row with the booking and purchase days and the amount, no running balance (`checks_ok` then takes the closing balance), the merchant the first unlabelled row after it (`name_from: first_unlabelled`), a purchase's own-currency amount its own row (`original_row`), and `drop_rows`; a number the PDF prints with every character twice (the first row after a page break) is read once (`_undoubled`). **141: a bank's ISO 20022 camt.053 statement** (`layout: camt053`, `parse_camt` in the isolated reader's helper with defusedxml, `_camt_account`): the IBAN, currency, number, period, booked opening and closing balances and printed totals of each statement, and every entry's dates, signed amount, running balance (`POSTBALANCE`), counterparty (the creditor of a debit, the debtor of a credit) with its account, and memo; a bank known by its BIC (`banks`) adds the texts of its transaction codes (`kinds`) and its card details (`details`: merchant, original amount), another bank's statement keeps its codes; the key is the account digits, currency and period start, as for the Excel export of the same month. **144 (DECISIONS 144): a line's facts beyond the money** (type pack 1.2.0, `LINE_DETAILS`; a line keeps only the ones its statement prints): the bank's reference (the CIB kind cell after its semicolon in the profile's `reference` shape; the camt.053 `TxId`, else `AcctSvcrRef`), the order id and the parties' end-to-end id (CIB labels; camt `EndToEndId`), a card purchase's card number, day and time (the CIB detail row `card_row`, kept even where a digits-only drop would remove it; the credit card's card number head (`card_section`) names the card of the purchases below it; the camt and Erste PDF card block, `details.card`), the terminal and the authorisation code, the exchange rate, the fee (the bank's text, else the camt charges; the Revolut fee column) and a transfer's acceptance time (camt `AccptncDtTm`). A full card number never leaves a reader (`read` masks the texts with `jav/core/card_numbers.py`: sixteen digits in one run or in four groups not inside a longer grouped number, with a right Luhn check digit, kept as the first six and last four digits; 167: the groups may be separated by spaces, hyphens or dots, a 15-digit American Express number (34/37) counts too, and the data guard uses the same pattern, `card_numbers.CARD`); the reconciliation masks the texts of a statement extracted by AI after taking the line's id (`reconcile._masked`). The camt reader is chosen by the root element (`_is_camt`), not by a namespace the head merely declares: the Erste Excel export declares a `camt` prefix on its `Workbook` root. **153 (review C1, C3): bounded input.** The size limit is checked before a file is read (`_within_size`; also a companion PDF), a CSV stops at `max_cells` while it is read, a text statement's lines (and a companion PDF's) pass `_bounded` (space runs of three or more become two, a line still over `LINE_MAX` = 2,000 characters is left out with a repair), a camt.053 entry's additional information is cut to `LINE_MAX`, `_xml_amount` reads at most 40 characters by a pattern that does not backtrack, and the Erste `rate` pattern reads a bounded number (`statement_tables.json` 1.5.1) 174 (review 160): a folder's monthly exports of one account are each kept (another month's key gets its period; the statement file keeps the key without it, so its id is the one it has read alone, M-7); a cell longer than a title (`TITLE_MAX`) is never tried as an account's title, whose pattern takes quadratic time on a long cell (M-8); a statement whose balances come from its own lines, with no total row, carries `balances` `lines`: the flow opens a `statement_table:balance_from_lines` to-do, records its closing check as a notice (`closing.from_lines`), and it stays unverified (M-12) | `tests/test_statement_table_136.py`, `tests/test_flow_statement_table_136.py`, `tests/test_statement_erste_137.py`, `tests/test_statement_cib_141.py`, `tests/test_statement_camt_141.py`, `tests/test_statement_fields_144.py` |

Of two statements of one account and currency that book the same days (a card cycle starting on the previous closing day is adjacent), the better source by `replacement.ranks` (read by code and checked by its balances, extracted and checked, unchecked) replaces the other when it covers the other's whole period; an equal rank or a partial cover is a conflict, and the later processed statement gets a `statement:overlap:<other>` to-do in a worker run. A person's choice (`statement_choices`, the latest per pair counts, until the run is approved; part of the reviewed version) wins over the rule and can keep either, or hand it back to the rule; it closes the pair's to-dos on both documents. A statement that came as an attachment of a suspicious email (`suspicious_source`) never replaces another by rule, and a clean reading of the same lines replaces it. A step's source (folder, files, mailbox request, existing package, none) and budget ({provider: usd}) are checked when it is made (the service checks the paths like typed ones). `advance` moves the chain: a step whose `after` steps are satisfied (the package's latest live run approved, a reconciliation's version approved, or nothing new) is claimed (`waiting` → `starting`, a guarded update) and started: documents from the folder or files (an empty one is deleted and the step is `nothing_new`), the statements of its survey's proposed ticks, a mailbox download (`pulling`, then its package), the reconciliation for the party's accounts with statements and the period. A run starts as a live run only when its readiness estimate fits the step's budget, otherwise `needs_budget` with the estimate; the budget changes only by a person's `start_step` (recorded). A failure stays on the step (`failed`, its reason) for a person to retry. The worker calls `tick` in its loop (at most every 10 s, the open automatic workflows with a step to move); creation and a person's start advance at once. `view` gives the control view (each step's status from its state and its package's next step, what needs a person first, the events); `workflow_of` names a package's workflow. 147 (DECISIONS 147): `repeat_draft` gives the workflow for the next period without writing (`next_period`: whole calendar months by as many months, otherwise as long right after; the sources and budgets carried, a documents step with `only_new`, a mailbox's own date range moved; a linked package's step takes the package's folder or no source), served at `/workflows/{id}/repeat`; `create(previous=…)` links the two (`previous_id`; `view` gives `repeat_of` and `repeated_as`). A documents step's `only_new` leaves out the documents a live run has processed (`work.unprocessed`: by content hash, an item done in an `apply` run; a trial or a failed item does not count) and records how many (`left_out` event); a folder of processed documents only is `nothing_new`. No AI call here: the runs it starts call them within their budgets 153 (review D1): a workflow is a reference of its party (`parties.register_reference`: a merge moves it to the party merged into, and a party a workflow names cannot be deleted), and the package of an open workflow's step cannot be deleted (`work.register_delete_guard`, `workflow_step`; it can be hidden, or the workflow archived first, after which the step lets its package go). 153 (review D2): a start interrupted after its claim (the process stopped) releases the claim to `failed`, and the worker's turn releases a claim older than `STALE_START_S` (`release_stale_starts`), so a person can start the step again; before, it stayed "starting" for good. 154: the wizard's and a step's package picker read the brief package list (`GET /workpackages?brief=true`: the packages as stored, without their to-dos and next step), so a picker no longer waits for every reconciliation workspace to be built. 160-D: a step that makes and runs its own package (a folder, files or a mailbox) may name its AI engine (`workflow_steps.engine`: jev | gpt); `_give_engine` puts it on the package (keeping its other settings) before the readiness check sizes the budget, the wizard's estimate and the repeat follow it, and a step without one follows the application's engine when it starts. 181: what each step kind does is asked from the step kinds' registry (Workflow step kinds); the templates the wizard reads (`/workflow-templates`, `templates_view`) carry each kind's registration. 181: a workflow has a party and a period only when its steps need them (Scopes and package scopes). 213 (plan G-process-core P1b-1): a step's change and its event are written in one transaction (`_set_logged`; before, the event was a second transaction). 214 (P1b-2): every workflow is a case of the process core (`jav/app/workflow_cases.py`, section 16), started in the transaction that makes it, adopted when made before, held while archived. 214 (P1b-3): the core moves the steps on (the step kind `workflow_step`; `advance` and `tick` are the case's turns, a person's start, link and the chain switched on reach the case in the workflow's own transaction; section 16). 215 (P2b): a step over its budget asks a person as a human task in its case, decided by the person's start; the approval of a run of a step's package sends the case an event, which the worker takes at each of its rounds (`take_events`), so the chain moves on at once; the check timer stays as the safety net (section 16). 227 (R3d): a step's phase is in its core row, not in `workflow_steps` (section 16). | `tests/test_workflows_146.py`, `tests/test_workflow_one_transaction_213.py`, `tests/test_workflow_phase_227.py`, `tests/test_workflow_case_214.py`, `tests/test_workflow_core_steps_214.py`, `tests/test_workflow_tasks_215.py`, `tests/test_workflow_steps_181.py`, `tests/test_workflow_repeat_147.py`, `tests/test_workflow_engine_160.py`, `tests/test_package_picker_154.py`, `ui/src/views/workflows/workflows146.test.tsx`, `ui/src/views/workflows/repeat147.test.tsx` |

## 13. Measurement tools outside the command line

**Plain-language summary.** Three measurement tools run on their own, outside the command line: a free estimate of a
paid JEV measurement, a data-extraction measurement on a local case list, and a probe for instructions hidden in
documents. They measure the production flows and change nothing in them. The September experiments and learning
branches (document and email learning, claim assessment, matter review, the legacy-pack runner and the stack trials)
were removed in 204; the git history keeps them. Teaching a new document type is a production feature
(`jav/evals/type_teaching.py`, section 10).

The worker and the local service never import these tools (the start-up preload skips them, `jav/runtime/preload.py` `EXCLUDED`). 208: they moved from `jav/experiments/` into the measurement package, `jav/evals/`.

| Tool | File | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Free JEV estimate | `jav/evals/jev_estimate.py` | runs a measurement's own commands with JEV as a stand-in: a cached question gets its saved answer, a new one is only counted with its size; nothing is written to the JEV cache, and the results go into a temporary store |
| Extraction on a case list | `jav/evals/extract_cases.py` | data extraction on a local case list with each document's expected type pack, as a paid measurement under a hard budget (`calls.measurement`); nothing is approved or corrected |
| Injected-instruction probe | `jav/evals/document_injection_probe.py` | made-up invoices in a clean and an injected variant, through type detection and both paths in a temporary store; the key number is a wrong value without a to-do (`configs/experiments/document_injection.json`) |

## 14. Content-based file names

**Plain-language summary.** After a run, the system can make copies of the processed documents under uniform names built
from their content, for example `2026-09-12_SZAMLA_Minta-Kft_SZ-2026-001234.pdf`. The original files are only read,
never renamed or changed. The name comes from the run's valid data (the machine value with any human correction), in
code, with no AI call. A copy whose name rests on something uncertain goes into a separate review subfolder, and a
manifest lists which original became which name.

| Part | File | What it does | Guarantee (test) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Naming rules | `configs/naming.json`, `jav/app/naming.py` (`name_for`, `review_reasons`) | one pattern per type (date first, then the type's token, the partner and the identifier; the first non-empty of several fields; modifiers for a date inside text, the last eight digits of an account and the street part of an address); the default pattern (first date, type, original name) for types without one; ASCII only, `_` separates the parts; a name's long legal form is shortened, an address keeps its street (without a comma: without the postcode and town) in one letter case; length limits with a cut at a word boundary; `__2`, `__3` on a collision | every type pack and detection type has a token; a pattern names only fields of its type pack; a broken pattern is rejected on load (`tests/test_naming_078.py`) |
| Review rule | `jav/app/naming.py` (`review_reasons`) | review subfolder when the item did not finish, the type is unknown or uncertain, a name field is empty, or an open to-do concerns a field the name was built from and no person corrected it; a to-do about the whole document (failed model call, weak OCR, hidden instruction) counts even after corrections; which to-do prefixes reach which parts is configuration (`reason_scope`) | a to-do on a fallback field that was not used does not count |
| A run's copies | `jav/app/naming.py` (`plan`, `write_zip`, `write_to_folder`) | the run's document items (the emails' PDF attachments included, the emails themselves not); a document without extracted data is named by its recognised category; the bytes come from the document's source instance (section 15) with the same full content check as the source view (`work.read_verified`); a copy whose bytes no longer match is skipped and listed; the manifest follows the download rules (BOM, `;`, formula guard) | the copy is byte for byte the processed document; the originals keep their bytes and modification time |
| Output folder | `jav/app/app_settings.py` (`app_options` table), `/api/settings/output-folder` | set in Settings › Work folders; it may not overlap a watched folder in either direction or the application's own data folders (`store`, `inbox`, `runs`); every write makes a new subfolder `<package>_<run>` (`__2` if it exists) and opens files exclusively, so nothing is ever overwritten or deleted; the full path is kept within 250 characters by shortening the names | overlap in both directions is refused (`folder_overlap`) |
| Access | `/api/runs/{run}/named-copies.zip`, `POST /api/runs/{run}/named-copies`, dataset `file_names`, `python -m jav.cli run-names`, UI Result › File names | the ZIP is built in a temporary file and streamed; writing to the output folder needs a named person; the result view shows the new name, the folder and the reason in words | `ui/src/naming078.test.tsx` |
| Unified names in the item lists | `jav/app/naming.py` (`run_item_names`, `package_item_names`), datasets `workpackage_items` and `run_items` (optional scope `names`), `GET /api/runs/{run}?names=unified`, `ui/src/names.ts`, `ui/src/components/NameCell.tsx` | the same names as the copies (`plan`), shown as the file name without the review folder; an item that has not finished has none (`pending`), an email never (`none`); a package's item takes its name from the latest run that finished it (newer runs first, a run's names are computed only when needed; the run's records come from the dataset cache); the service puts the chosen name into the `name` column, so search, sort, filter and download follow it; the choice is kept per person in the browser, unified by default | `tests/test_unified_names_082.py`, `ui/src/unifiedNames082.test.tsx` |

A partner's name is the extracted name with its legal form shortened and cut to 40 characters; there is no partner register.

## 15. Source instances

**Plain-language summary.** When a document is added to a work package, the system keeps an unchanging copy of it, and
everything afterwards works from that copy: processing, the document shown in review, its page images and the named
copies. What a person checks is therefore exactly what the result was made from, even if the original file is later
changed, moved or deleted. A change to the original is shown as a warning, not treated as an error. The copies go into
the daily backup once each.

| Part | File | What it does | Guarantee (test) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Store | `jav/documents/source_instances.py` (229: the documents layer's; until then the application's) | a content-addressed folder next to the store, `store/sources/<first two hex digits>/<sha256><suffix>`; one copy per content, however many packages hold it; `freeze` copies with a single read and takes the fingerprint from the copied bytes, then moves the file into place atomically; a damaged copy with the same fingerprint is replaced | the copy is byte for byte the original; the same content in two packages is kept once (`tests/test_source_instances_079.py`) |
| Intake | `jav/app/work.py` (`add_items`, the `instance` column of `workpackage_items`) | every document item gets an instance when it is added (an email attachment too), unless it is over the input limit: such a document is not copied (the size is checked before the copy, and the copy stops at the limit should the file grow meanwhile), it is added with the original's fingerprint and stopped by the named size error when processed; an email (`message.json` under `inbox/`) does not, because it is already the system's own copy and the email flow reads its whole folder; items added before source instances existed keep working from their original file, as before; a failed intake releases the copies it made | the recorded fingerprint is the copied bytes'; nothing is left behind by a failed intake; a document over the input limit fills no disk (`tests/test_audit_fixes_085.py`) |
| Reading | `work.source_file`, `jav/runtime/worker.py`, the `read_path` of the document flows (`jav/flows/flow.py`, `jav/flows/flow_detect.py`) | the worker checks the full content hash of the instance and the flows read their bytes from it; the file name (part of the type-recognition request), the year hint and the stored document path still come from the original path, so the model requests stay byte-identical; Azure recognition, which can only read files under the legacy sidecar's data folder, gets the original instead when it lies there and is unchanged (`ocr.azure_alias`); the email flow's own detection of a PDF attachment reads the attachment item's instance too (`attachment_reads`, supplied by the worker) | a run finishes with the original changed or deleted, with the same requests (all answered from the cache) and the same document identifier |
| Readiness and review | `work_readiness.readiness`, `work.original_state`, `corrections.item_result` (`source_file`) | only a missing or damaged instance blocks a start (`instance_damaged`); a changed or missing original is a warning (`original_changed`, `original_missing`); the review view says when the original is no longer what was added; the source view, the page images and the named copies are served from the instance | a swapped original does not change what is shown (`tests/test_source_identity_075.py`, `ui/src/sourceInstance079.test.ts`) |
| Release | `source_instances.release`, `work.delete_workpackage`, `work.add_entries` | deleting a package without runs releases each of its instances that no other item (in any package, removed items included) refers to; a package with runs can only be hidden, so its instances stay. 167 (review R-7): a copy made for a document being added has no item yet, so a deletion (or another failed intake) may release it in between; the intake checks its copies inside the transaction that records the items (release deletes only under the same write lock) and makes a released one again from the original, refusing an original whose content changed meanwhile (`work.SourceChanged`) | a shared instance survives the deletion; `tests/test_instance_race_167.py` |
| Backup | `jav/runtime/backup.py` (`_sync_sources`) | a shared `sources/` folder beside the timestamped backups, locally and in the second location; each instance is copied once and checked against the hash in its name, never pruned (instances never change); a copy already there is checked by its content hash on every backup, and a damaged one is replaced from the intact instance (`repaired`); a damaged instance is not copied and is named in the manifest (`damaged`); a damaged copy with no intact instance to replace it fails the backup; the check starts from the instances the saved store copy refers to (`_referenced_instances`, `_check_required`): one intact only in the backup is `preserved`, one intact nowhere is `missing`, which fails the backup and stops pruning in both locations, while the store copy still goes to the second location | a second backup adds nothing; pruning leaves the folder alone; a same-size corruption is found and repaired (`tests/test_audit_fixes_085.py`); a missing referenced instance fails the backup, and a restore from the backup alone opens every referenced document (`tests/test_audit_fixes_1002_n05_n03.py`) |

Retention of the instances beyond package deletion (how long a hidden package's documents are kept) is not decided yet.

## 16. The process core (213, plan G-process-core P1a)

**Plain-language summary.** A case is one business matter followed from start to finish, possibly over days. Its
process definition names its steps; the core keeps the case's steps, its history, the events it receives, the
handovers it sends and its timers in the store, each change with its history row in one transaction, and workers move
cases on under claims that no live process takes over. Its own tests are the engine trial's failure tests (212) and a
second invented case. Since 214 every workflow (section 6) is a case under its own id, started with the workflow or
adopted from the state an earlier workflow reached, and the core moves its steps on. Since 215 the core has human
tasks: work waiting for a person, with its assignee, deadline, reason rule and the result version it is about; in a
case a task is a running step's wait, and its closing moves the step on. The core knows no domain: step kinds and
task kinds are registered by the layers above it.

The last column names the process core's ten operating rules (written down before the engine was chosen): 1 a case
runs on the definition version it started with; 2 a person may override the order, never a gate; 3 a step builds on a
named result version; 4 a change, its history and its outgoing handover in one transaction; 5 every incoming event has
a stable id; 6 a paid call keeps its id and an unknown outcome is not repeated; 7 an event and a deadline never both
win; 8 who acts is recorded; 9 authorisation at the moment of the operation; 10 one owner for every task, timer and
recovery.

| part | module | rule |
|---|---|---|
| Process definition | `jav/core/process/definition.py`: steps in order; a step names earlier steps by order (`after`, a person may override it, recorded) or by gate (`gates`, nobody may); a canonical form and its fingerprint | 1, 2 |
| Who acts | `jav/core/identity.py` (229: the shared core's; before, the process core's `actor.py`): a stable id (`local:<name>` today), the name shown, human or machine, the organisation, the request; a machine names whom it acts for | 8 |
| Authorisation and observability | `jav/core/access.py`, `jav/core/observability.py` (229: the shared core's; before, the process core's `hooks.py`): one check per operation (allows everything today); one sink for duration, outcome category and attempts, off by default; a failing sink never stops work | 9 |
| Step kinds | `kinds.py`: `run(ctx)` returns `Done(result)`, `WAIT`, `Close(outcome)` or `Fail(note)`, outside any transaction and repeatable; `events` + `on_event` (applied or refused) and `on_timer` inside the engine's transaction | — |
| Case, steps, history, inbox | `cases.py`: tables `process_cases` (a copy of the definition the case started with), `process_steps` (waiting, ready, running, done, failed, stale; the result and its version; 227: the step kind's own phase, `set_phase`, which the core keeps through its own changes and never reads), `process_history`, `process_inbox` (an event's id and content fingerprint: a repeat is a duplicate, other content a conflict, a closed case's event is ignored); reopening a done step makes the steps built on it stale; 214: `start_in` starts a case inside the caller's transaction, `adopt` makes one from a state reached outside the core (each step's state, a running step's wait and timer; one history row), `hold` and `resume` put a whole case aside and take it up again; `override_order_in`, `retry_in` and `reopen_in` write a person's action in the caller's transaction, and reopening a step of a case closed as done opens the case again; 215: `send_event_in` receives an event in the caller's transaction, and `on_leave` runs what else must change when a step stops running or the case closes | 1, 3, 4, 5 |
| Human tasks | `tasks.py` (215): `process_tasks` and `process_task_history`; a task's id is its kind and subject, so opening it again while open changes nothing and opening a closed one starts a new round; an assignee (a person's or group's id, or anyone), a deadline, whether a decision needs a reason (fixed when it opens), the result version it is about; it ends decided (once, by a person; `StaleResult` for another result version), expired or withdrawn; every change with its history row in the caller's transaction, and the authorisation check before anyone acts. In a case a task is a running step's wait (the step's kind takes its closing event, `task_closed` unless the opener names another), its deadline is the case's timer (expired when due, never by a woken turn), its closing sends the case the event in the same transaction, and when the step stops running or the case closes its open tasks are withdrawn without an event | 3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 9 |
| Timers | `timers.py`: `process_timers`, a stable id per case, step and name; armed with the wait, cancelled when the wait ends (in the event's transaction), fired once; a timer on a step that moved on only leaves a history row; a timer without `on_timer` is a retry that keeps the wait | 7 |
| Handovers | `outbox.py`: `process_outbox`, recorded with the case, then sent under the same id; a handover found sent without an answer, or whose answer was lost, is uncertain, never sent again by itself, and settled by a person | 4 |
| Engine | `engine.py`: `process_claims` (a claim names the process's call-log holder; a stopped process's claim is free at once); a turn fires due timers, takes the events steps wait for, then runs the runnable steps in order; a step's kind the running code lacks makes the case wait for a migration; `start`, `send_event`, `state` and `cancel` are the durable workflow engine's plug-in point; 214: `turn` moves one named case on now under its claim (`claim_case`), a woken turn fires its waiting steps' retry timers at once (a deadline only when due), `due_cases` lists the cases with something to do, and a claim is exclusive between the threads of one process too; 229: `turn_due` is the worker's round, a turn for every case with something to do of any process definition (a case whose turn raises is left to the next round) | 6, 10 |

The engine does not use the job queue (since stage E the queue, too, ties a claim to the process holding it). New SQL is portable (`ON CONFLICT`;
the write lock only through `store.begin_immediate`). Tests: `tests/test_process_core_213.py` (each rule on its own,
with a Hypothesis test of an event racing its deadline), `tests/test_process_trial_213.py` (T01 x3, T02, T03, T04, T06,
T07 x2, T08 with separate worker processes over `tests/process_quote.py` and `tests/process_worker.py`),
`tests/test_process_claim_case_213.py` (parallel checks, a reminder and a deadline; 215: a sign-off that goes to the
team lead when the clerk's deadline passes), `tests/test_process_adopt_214.py` (adoption, hold and resume) and
`tests/test_process_tasks_215.py` (human tasks, with a Hypothesis test of a decision racing its deadline).

**The core's boundary (229, plan structure stage 1, SZ1a–SZ1b).** The worker's round turns every case with something
to do, of any process definition (`engine.turn_due`); before, it looked only at the workflows' cases. Each module of
the core lists what code outside it may use (`__all__`): the operations a caller makes in its own transaction, the
reads (229: `find_case_in`, `find_step_in`, `without_case_in`, `steps_in_phase_in`, `tasks.of_kind_in`,
`tasks.ids_of_kind_in`), the step and task kinds' registries and the plug-in point's four operations. Code outside the
core uses only these and never names the core's tables in SQL; it reads them through the core, as the domains' tables
are read through their registrations (211). The two tools that read a store copy of any version
(`jav/devtools/admin.py`, `jav/evals/before_after.py`) are the stated exceptions. Tests:
`tests/test_process_worker_229.py`, `tests/test_process_boundary_229.py`.

**Who acted, by a stable id (229, plan structure stage 1, SZ2c).** Every column of the store that names who acted (a
decision, an approval, a correction, a closed to-do, a setting changed) has the actor's stable id beside it
(`<column>_id`). A business operation takes who acts as `identity.Who`: the actor the shared entrance knows, or a name,
which is the local person of that name; it writes the name and the id (`identity.name_of`, `identity.id_of`). Code that
acts again as someone a row recorded (a version backfilled with its runs' approver, a workflow's packages owned by its
maker) takes both back (`identity.recorded`). When an id column is added to an older store, its earlier rows get
`local:<name>` from their names once (`store.add_actor_ids`); a row written afterwards keeps its own id. The process
core keeps the whole actor already, and a worker's claim names a process, not a person. A human task's assignee is the
package owner's stored id. The service hands every operation the actor its shared entrance knows (`human_actor` returns
`identity.Actor`; SZ2c-3), so the company sign-in will change only the entrance (`service_shared.acting`). The machine
acting for a person has the machine's id, the name the lists always showed and the person's id
(`identity.acting_for`): a workflow's automatic step (`workflow-chain`, "<maker> (auto)") and a scheduled mailbox
download (`mailbox-schedule`, whose package the schedule's saver owns, by the saver's stored id); a person's start of a
workflow step keeps the person's id with the mark. Printed in a log line or a note, an actor is its name. Guard tests
(`tests/test_actor_ids_229.py`): every such column (47 in 33 tables) has its id column, every statement that writes one
writes the id too, and an insert into such a table names its columns.

**Workflows as cases (214).** `jav/app/workflow_cases.py`: a workflow's case has the workflow's id; its process
definition is the workflow's saved template (one step per template step, all of the core step kind `workflow_step`
with the template's step kind as its parameter, the template's `after` as the order), versioned by the saved
template's fingerprint. The case starts in the transaction that writes the workflow. A workflow without a case is
adopted by the store's schema change helper (`adopt_missing`, a savepoint per workflow, so one it cannot read never
stops the store from opening): waiting → waiting; starting → running; pulling → running, waiting for its fetch with a
check timer; started or linked → running, waiting for its package's approval with a check timer; needs_budget →
running, waiting for a budget; nothing_new → done; failed → failed. An archived workflow's case is on hold
(`archived`), held and resumed in the transaction that archives or restores the workflow.

The core moves the steps on: `jav/app/workflow_advance.py` (until 231 C3i `workflows.py`) registers the step kind `workflow_step` on import (a process that
turns a workflow's case must know it, or the case would wait for a migration for good). Under the case's claim it does
what the step's phase (227: the core step's own `phase`, see below) asks for: nothing
new is done; started or linked is done once its package's latest live run (a reconciliation's version) is approved,
otherwise it waits for that with a check timer (`TICK_EVERY_S`); pulling checks its download, otherwise it waits with
a check timer; a `starting` it finds was left by a stopped process (nobody else holds the claim) and fails at once;
waiting for a budget waits for a person; failed fails with its note; waiting starts when the chain is automatic,
otherwise it waits for a person. `advance` is one woken turn of the case; `tick` advances the automatic workflows with
a step to move as before (a woken turn each) and then turns the other workflow cases the core has something for (a
check timer that came due). A person's action reaches the case in the transaction of the workflow's own change:
`start_step` (`person_starts`: the order overridden and recorded, a failed step retried, a wait ended, a done step
reopened; the start is marked on the step's result and its next run takes it once, in the person's name), `link`
(`person_links`), `update(auto=True)` (`chain_on`: the steps waiting for a person go on); then the case's turn runs.
Two behaviours differ on purpose: a start left by a stopped process fails at the next turn (before: after
`STALE_START_S`), and a download a person started finishes its step while the chain is off (before: only once the
chain was switched on). Tests: `tests/test_workflow_case_214.py`, `tests/test_workflow_core_steps_214.py`,
`tests/test_process_turn_214.py`.

**A step's state in one place (227, plan R3 R3d).** A workflow step's whole state is its row in the case: the core's
state and wait, and where its start stands (waiting, starting, pulling, started, linked, needs_budget, nothing_new,
failed) as the core step's `phase`. The core's state alone cannot take the phase's place: a step that went out of
date loses its wait, and a fresh start, a start a stopped process left and a step woken by its package's approval are
all "running, waiting for nothing"; the phase tells the step how to go on. `workflows._update_step` writes the phase
(a claim with `expect`) with the step's own data in the same transaction; `workflow_steps` keeps only that own data
(source, budget, name, engine, package, download, run, note), and the control view's `state` is the phase. A case
starts with each step's first phase (`linked` or `waiting`); an adopted step's state of before becomes its phase. A
store of before moves the column `workflow_steps.state` into the core rows and drops it in one write transaction
(`workflow_cases.move_phases`, after `adopt_missing`), only once every workflow has its case. Two changes made outside
a turn fail the step in its case too (`workflow_cases.fail_outside`; a done step is reopened first): a deleted
package's step (its open budget task is withdrawn), and a start left by a stopped process that `release_stale_starts`
releases, now under the case's claim. Tests: `tests/test_workflow_phase_227.py`.

**A step built on a reopened step (218, plan P3 P3l).** Rule 3 reaches the application: a done step's result names
the approval its package was done with (`ref`: a logic pack's own version and fingerprint, a processing package's
approved live runs; none for a step that found nothing new or a package done without an approval of its own, the email
routing) and the approvals its earlier steps were done with (`built_on`); the core's `step_done` history row names
their result versions. When the package of a done step is reopened, `workflow_cases.package_reopened` reopens the step
in the transaction of that change (a new live run of the package, `work_runs.start_run`; its approved own version
reopened, `package_release.reopen_own`), so the steps built on it go stale. The control view shows such a step as out of
date (`stale`, with `stale_of`: the earlier steps whose result changed) and does not count it as done. The owner's
decisions of 2026-10-23: the later step's approved version and its lock stay while the earlier step is open; once the
earlier step is approved again, the step's first look keeps the approvals that no longer count (`old_approval`: the one
it was done with and the one its package has then) and counts only another, so an approval given while the earlier step
was open does not count either: a logic pack approved as its own version is reopened with a reason through its
registered `LogicPack.reopen` (its approval task opens again), a processing package needs a new live run a person
starts. A step that goes out of date before it was ever done keeps what its earlier steps had then (`built_on`, written
by the core's `on_leave` in the transaction that marks it). A stopped new run leaves the earlier result, so the approval
built on it counts again. Nothing runs by itself. Tests: `tests/test_workflow_reopen_chain_218.py`, `ui/src/views/workflows/staleChain218.test.tsx`.

**Today's work as human tasks (215, P2b).** `jav/app/human_tasks.py` names whom a task is for (the owner's rule: the
package's responsible person; without one, its creator when on the Users list; otherwise anyone) and keeps the run
approval task `run_approval:<run>`: opened in the transaction in which a live run's status changes to a finished one
unapproved (`work_runs.refresh_run_status`), decided in the approval's transaction (`approve_run`), withdrawn when the
run is superseded or stopped. It belongs to the run, not to a workflow step (a run waiting to be superseded outlives its
step); when the run's package is a workflow step's and the step waits for its package's approval, its approval or
withdrawal sends the case an `approval` event in the same transaction (`workflow_cases.package_settled`). A step over
its budget asks a person with `workflow_budget:<workflow>:<step>`, a task in the case opened with the wait
(`workflow_cases.ask_budget`) and decided by the person's start, whose closing event ends the wait. The step kind takes
both events and refuses one of an earlier round of its task (`task_event`). The worker turns the cases with such an
event at each of its rounds (229: the core's own round, `engine.turn_due`, which turns every case with something to do,
of any process definition; before, `workflows.take_events` looked at the workflows' cases only), not only when
`tick`'s throttle allows; the check timer stays as the safety net (a run approved without its task, as by an earlier version).
No task has a deadline (the owner's decision). Tests: `tests/test_workflow_tasks_215.py`.

**To-dos and a logic pack's own approval as human tasks (215, P2d).** Every open to-do item is a task,
`review:<item>`, its mirror: the store calls the hook the application registers (`store.set_review_item_hook`,
`human_tasks.review_item_changed`) in the transaction that raises an item (`review_enqueue`) or closes it
(`_sync_review_item`, from `review_close`, `close_run_reasons` and `review_resolve`), so an item and its task are
written together or not at all, and `review_enqueue` keeps its signature. The task is for the package of the run that
raised the item last (`store.run_of` of the item's run; a measurement's item has no package and is anyone's) and keeps
what it is about and the kind of run that opened it (live, trial, measurement); when another package's run raises the
item, the task goes to that package's person. It is decided when a person resolves the item's last reason (the
outcome lists the reasons people resolved) and withdrawn when a machine closes it. An item never reopens (the next
to-do of its subject is a new item), so its task stays in its first round. A logic pack a person approves as its
own version (`LogicPack.own_approval`: a reconciliation; 231 A3d: a case's quote opens it from its draft step; the email routing is done when every attachment is
decided and has none) waits for that approval as `own_approval:<package>` from when the package is made
(`work.create_workpackage`, by the pack's kind), and again when its approved version is reopened
(`package_release.reopen_own`); the approval decides it on the fingerprint the person approved
(`package_release.record_own`) and, for a workflow step's package, sends the case the same `approval` event as a run's
approval; deleting the package withdraws it, archiving it does not (restoring it needs the same approval). Since
stage D0 (D0-b) a case's approval is its approving step's: `own_approval:<case>:<step>` (`human_tasks.own_subject`), and
an own version records its step (`package_versions.step_id`): each approving step of a case waits, is decided, holds a
version and is reopened on its own (`package_release.record_own`, `reopen_own`, `own_approval` with `step=`), while the
version numbers stay one sequence per package (a version's files keep their names). The case's steps that approve are
`process_runtime.own_version_steps` (those it waits for from its start: `approval_steps`); the reconciliation's
statement is its `pair` step's, the quote its `compose` step's (`process_runtime.step_using`). A task written for the
whole case before moves to its case's one approving step when the store opens (`human_tasks.own_approvals_per_step`,
through the core's `tasks.resubject_in`: its rounds, assignee, opening time and history stay) or when its package is
