taken over as a case (`human_tasks.case_adopted`); a version recorded before has no step and counts as its case's
single approving step's (`tests/test_own_approval_steps_231.py`). D0-d: an own version records its basis, the result
versions of the steps its step builds on (`package_versions.basis`, `process_runtime.basis_of`); when one changes the
approval is out of date (`process_runtime.outdated`) and the approving capability reopens it by itself with that reason
(the quote's composition, the reconciliation's pairing), so a person approves it again - until D0-d the step went on with
its old approval. A person may return a step's result with a reason instead of approving it (`package_release.reject_own`,
table `own_rejections`; `POST /api/cases/{id}/steps/{step}/approval/return`); the approval's state of a step - missing,
approved, out of date, returned - is one view (`package_release.approval_state`, `GET .../approval`), which the shared
`approval` element shows (`case_views`: data only for steps that approve, `ElementData.applies`;
`tests/test_approval_subject_231.py`, `ui/src/elements/approval231.test.tsx`). A package's
new responsible person takes its open tasks (`work.set_owner` → `human_tasks.package_owner_changed`: its runs'
approvals, the items its runs raised last, its own approval, its workflow step's budget), each move with its history
row. The work made before has its tasks opened by the store's schema change helper (`human_tasks.adopt_missing`,
registered after the version columns it reads by `jav/app/package_release.py`; opened by the machine
`human-task-adoption`, by the same rule), and a task left open while a version that keeps none settled its subject (an
item closed, a run approved or stopped, a package approved or deleted) is withdrawn by it; so a store that the code
before 215 and this code both use comes back into step at the next start. The state page counts the open tasks by
kind. Tests: `tests/test_review_tasks_215.py` (with a Hypothesis test that the open items and the open to-do tasks are
always the same).

**Waiting for me (216, P2e).** The interface's list of what waits for a person (`#/waiting`, `ui/src/views/Waiting.tsx`;
the header's link counts it) is the dataset `waiting` (`jav/app/datasets.py`), whose rows `jav/app/waiting.py` reads
from the open tasks for the person and anyone's (`tasks.open_for_in`): the kind, what it is about (a to-do's item title
in the package of the run that raised it last, its first open reason and how many are open; a run's approval; a logic
pack's approval; a step's budget), the package, since when it waits, its origin (live, trial, measurement; an approval
and a budget are live) and whether it is the person's own or anyone's. Each row's `_wp` and `_stage` lead to where it
is done (a to-do's review page, a run approval's package result, the package page). By default only live work shows;
the scope's `all` adds the to-dos of trial runs and measurements (the owner's decisions of 2026-10-23). The list reads
only. Tests: `tests/test_waiting_list_216.py`, `ui/src/waiting216.test.tsx`.

**Calculation snapshots (218, P3a).** `jav/core/rulebook.py`: every process takes one snapshot at start-up
(`jav.runtime.preload`): the application's version, the commit and whether the working tree had uncommitted changes,
and the content fingerprint of every file under `configs/` with each JSON config's `meta.version`; every JSON config is
loaded through `cfg.load` at that moment, so the rules the process reads afterwards are the ones it names
(`cfg.reload` takes a new one). Tables: `rule_files` (each distinct content once, by its sha256, with where and when it
was first seen), `calc_snapshots` (the snapshot's id is the fingerprint of its version, commit, dirty flag and files) and
`snapshot_links` (a subject's latest snapshot). Linked in the subject's own transaction: a run when it starts
(`work_runs.start_run`, the service's snapshot), each finished item when the worker records it
(`work_runs.record_item_result`), a case when it starts (`cases.start_in`), a package version when a person approves a
logic pack's own version or releases a package (`package_release`). When some items of a run were processed with
another snapshot than the run started with (a release during the run), `work_runs.rule_changes` says how many and which
files changed with their versions, and the run's view carries it as `rule_changes` (only then, so other views are as
before); the package's Approve and release and the run's own line show it, and nothing stops the approval (the owner's
decision of 2026-10-23). `python -m jav.cli snapshot` reads them: this process's, `--id`, `--run`, `--diff A B`,
`--restore <sha256> --out <file>`. Tests: `tests/test_rulebook_218.py`, `tests/test_calc_snapshot_links_218.py`,
`ui/src/ruleChanges218.test.tsx`.

**Decision trace (218, P3b).** `jav/core/trace.py`: a machine decision point is registered by its owner module
(`trace.register(Point)`: its code, what it decides, what it is not for, the rule files under `configs/` it reads, and
its rule: a pure function whose value for the stored inputs is the stored outcome). A decision writes one row of
`decision_trace` in the transaction that records its result (`trace.record_in`): the point, its subject (kind and id),
the run and the case, the inputs the rule read and their fingerprint, the outcome, the rule files and keys it used with
each file's version and content fingerprint, and the process's calculation snapshot. When the row cannot be written,
the decision is not written either. `trace.replay(row)` runs the rule again on a row's inputs (the basis of the planned
preview of a changed rule, P3d). A person's decision has its own journal (`jav.core.decisions`), whose state the pages
send back against stale edits, so the machine's trace is a table of its own. The modules that register points on import
are listed in `registrations.DECISION_POINT_MODULES`, so the registry is complete in every process. Points so far:
`workflow.budget_gate` (a workflow step's estimate per provider against its approved budget, `workflows.budget_gate`;
traced with the step's wait for a budget, or with its start and the run it started); `detect.doc_type` and
`detect.detail_type` (M1: whether the recognised type waits for a person, and the detailed type's to-dos including the
issuer cross-check, `flow_detect.doc_type_rule` and `detail_type_rule`; the recognition's save, its document row, its
to-dos and both traces, is one write transaction, the store's own writes joining it as savepoints in `store.session`);
`email.intent` and `email.next_flow` (M3: whether the recognised intent waits for a person with its signals' to-dos,
and the email's next step, `flow_email.intent_rule` and `next_flow_rule`; the email's save, its row, its run's result
row, its to-dos and the traces, is one write transaction too); `extract.route` (M2, one row per document and run:
whether the extracted record is accepted or waits for a person, and the to-dos its decision raised;
`policy.decision_inputs` keeps what the decision read in the flow state, `policy.route_rule` replays the decision checks
of the check registry in its order, each through the pure rule it applies to the flow state (`Check.rule`: the picks,
the verification, the GPT confidence, the failed record checks), with the cross-document checks' to-dos and the to-dos
raised before the decision as inputs; the rule files are `policy.json` and the type pack, named by a pattern,
`types/*.json`; the extraction's save, its document row, its record, its to-dos and the trace, is one write transaction,
before four to six); `ocr.escalation` (whether a document's weak local OCR text is asked of the paid engine and which
text goes on, `ocr.escalation_rule`: the escalation keeps what it read beside the text's signals, the attempt's outcome,
blocked or read, as an input; traced by the recognition's and the extraction's save, and by their `needs_ocr` step,
now one write transaction too); `finance.duplicate` and `parties.own_tax_as_supplier` (the cross-document checks: the
same invoice as another document of the store, by family, number and supplier, with its kind; an own party's tax
number as the supplier's; `duplicates.duplicate_rule` and `parties.own_tax_rule`, their inputs the document's key
fields with the other documents of its family and number, and the two tax keys with the own party the supplier's key
names; a cross-document check gives its trace as a `checks.Traced`, which the extraction keeps in its state,
`check_traces`, and records in its save; the store scans of the command line, `duplicates --write` and
`own-tax --write`, record it with each to-do they open, in one write); `parties.detail_direction` (whether the
direction the own parties give an extracted invoice settles its detailed type's uncertainty between an outgoing and an
incoming pack, `parties.direction_rule`, the direction found an input; traced with the to-dos it closes or opens,
inside the extraction's save). The rule keys of a band decision come from `policy.band_keys`. `python -m jav.cli trace` reads it (`--points`, `--point`,
`--subject KIND:ID`, `--run`, `--case`, `--last`; each row with `same`: its rule gives the stored outcome again); the
status page counts it by point. Tests: `tests/test_decision_trace_218.py` (a new point must be listed there with its
test).

**The rules' read-only view (218, P3e).** Settings › Rules shows, per registered decision point, what it decides and
what it is not for, the rule values it decides with, the version of each rule file it reads and since when that
content is in use here, and how many decisions the trace holds (all, those made with the rule files in use now, the
latest). `jav/app/rules_view.py` builds the `rules` dataset (`jav/app/datasets.py`): the plain texts and which keys
to show come from `configs/rule_help.json` (English source, translated by the interface); each value is read from
the rule file the process loaded (`rules_view.resolve`, a key as the trace names it, `recipes[processing]` naming a
list's item by its id), a code given by the interface's own labels (a document type, a field, an intent) or, for an
email's next step, by its code; a file's version from the process's calculation snapshot and its date from the
file's changelog, the time it came into use from `rule_files.first_seen_at`; the counts from one grouped query of
`decision_trace` (a row counts as made with the rules in use now when each file it names has the content in use now).
It only reads; price lists and output templates keep their own pages. In the interface
(`ui/src/views/settings/RulesPanel.tsx`) each decision is a table row of at most four lines, and its title opens the
whole rule below the table. Tests: `tests/test_rules_view_218.py`, `ui/src/views/settings/rules218.test.tsx`.

**The rule files by domain (224, P3c).** `configs/policy.json` (1.26.0) keeps what every flow shares: the bands
(`bands`, `band_for`), the "none of these" label, the OCR thresholds, the type details and a GPT field's confidence,
read by `jav/ai/policy.py`. A domain's own rules are in its own file, read by its own module: the email flow's routes
and signals in `configs/email.json` (`jav/domains/email/email_rules.py`: `email_next_flow`, `email_sent_route`,
`email_signal_reasons`, `email_signal_route`), the duplicate search and the reconciliation's currency bands and sure AI
threshold in the finance domain's `configs/finance.json` (`duplicates`: `jav/domains/finance/duplicates.py`;
`reconcile`: `jav/domains/finance/reconcile_rules.py` `fx_tolerance`, `fx_bank_tolerance`, `ai_sure_probability`). They
judge with the shared bands; the type roles read their lists where they are. So a domain's rule change does not change
another's fingerprint (a data point's `config_hash` names `policy.json` only), and the shared AI layer imports no
domain. The bands are not in `configs/reconcile.json`, whose fingerprint a reconciliation's statement prints: the
interface then showed an approved statement whose fingerprint differs from the current one as changed since its
approval, so moving them there would have marked every approved statement; the reconciliation's workspace counts
`finance.json` among its inputs. Since 227 (DECISIONS 224) the statement's answer says what changed since the
approval (`reconcile_statement.since_approval`): its content without the rule fingerprint and the engine's version
(`content_fingerprint`; a warning), or only those (a note); the approved fingerprint itself is formed as before
(`tests/test_reconcile_rule_change_227.py`). The decision points name
the files they read (`email.intent`, `email.next_flow`: `policy.json` and `email.json`; `finance.duplicate`:
`finance.json` and `fact_checks.json`). Tests: `tests/test_rule_files_split_224.py`.

**The rules read when they apply (227, plan R3b).** A decision point's rule file is read through the config loader
whenever its rule applies, never copied when a module is loaded: a module's rule table is a read-through view
(`jav/core/cfg.py` `Section`: `policy.BANDS`, `BAND_FOR`, `DETECT_DETAIL`, `DETECT_ISSUER`, `GPT_FIELD_CONFIDENCE`;
`email_rules.INTENT_ROUTE`, `EMAIL_SIGNAL_ROUTES`; `ocr.ESCALATION`), and a single value or a fingerprint is a
function (`policy.config_hash`, `human_max_conf`, `auto_min_conf_high_stakes`, `review_flag_p`,
`detect_low_confidence`, `intent_human_max_conf`; `email_rules.config_hash`, `m2_types`, `jev_unavailable_route`,
`sent_route`, `signal_review`; `ocr.config_hash`, `quality_limits`). So a reload (`cfg.reload`) reaches every decision
without a restart. The loader rereads no changed file by itself: the service runs from the working folder, and a
half-edited rule must not reach it; where a released or edited version comes from is the rule editing's to decide.
Two reads stay as loaded, by design: the none option's key (part of every S-path request) and the OCR engine's own
settings (they change the text and its cache key); the registries, the call sites and the prompts stay as loaded too
(a change is a new AI request). Tests: `tests/test_rule_reader_227.py` (no module copies a decision point's rule file
at import; a reloaded copy of `configs/` reaches the bands, the email routes and the OCR escalation).

**The rule preview (224, P3d).** `jav/evals/rules_preview.py` tries a rule file's proposed version on the decisions
the trace holds, for free (`python -m jav.cli rules-preview <file> --candidate F | --ref REF`). It takes every decision
point whose files match the rule file (`trace.Point.files`), reads its rows read-only (the active store or `--store`),
and runs each row's rule (`trace.replay`) twice, each time in a process of its own: with the rule files in use now, and
with a copy of `configs/` in which the candidate takes the file's place (the child sets the config loader's folders
before any rule module is loaded, because the modules read their rules when they are loaded). It reports per point the
decisions, how many would change, how many were made with other rules or code than today's (the stored outcome differs
from today's rule, `differs_from_stored`), the to-dos that would open or close by kind (`todo_kinds.code_of` of an
outcome's `reasons`) and a few examples (each differing part of the outcome). A rule that raises is reported per row.
Nothing is written and no AI is asked; a decision the trace does not hold is not counted. `--replay` shows a whole
item's effect: the replay of a sample of a backup (`before_after_replay.running_snapshot`, earlier answers only) twice
on the same fresh copy, with the rules now and with the candidate, each in its own process, and the views that differ
(`before_after.diff`). Only JSON rule files read through the config loader are honoured, which is what the decision
points read. Tests: `tests/test_rules_preview_224.py`.

**The capability registry (231, plan stage A, A2).** The owner's rule of 2026-10-24: every process is built only from
general capabilities, described as data in a process pack (planned for the next slices). A capability
(`jav/app/capabilities.py` `Capability`) is one registered building block: its general name and version, the module
that owns it, its kind (`machine`, `human`, `wait`), a one-line summary, the pydantic model of what a process sets for
it (it forbids other fields), the paid services it may call, the rule files it reads and its effect (`pure`, `store`,
`external`; an external effect only through the handover record). One that can run gets its own step kind in the
process core, `cap.<name>.v<version>`, which checks the step's parameters before the capability runs, so a case that
started on one version keeps running on it; a capability only described has no step kind yet. A paid step outside a
work run spends from its own budget scope per case, step and round (`step_budget`, `case:<case>:<step>:<round>`), as
business work through the call log, not as a measurement. Before the process core calls a step kind unknown
(`kinds.unknown`), it makes the application's registrations (`jav.core.completion`), since the capabilities register
there. Today's process-specific parts are a frozen list that may only shrink (`tests/test_process_strictness_231.py`).
The first six are the quote's back half (231 A3c, `jav/domains/quotes/capabilities.py`): `record_assembly`,
`catalogue_match`, `line_pricing`, `line_draft`, `priced_document`, `letter_draft`; their fixed values became parameters
with today's values as the defaults (A3b: the request's type and list field, `price_lists.chosen`, the output, the
letter frame). Five of them run as the IT quote's steps since A5; `line_pricing` is described only (no runner), and a
pack cannot use it (D0-a). Since stage D0 (D0-c) `priced_document` is the shared layer's (`jav/app/approved_document.py`:
it prints the approved version of the step it names with the builder its output's domain registers), so the quotes
domain holds five, four of which run. Since stage D0 (D0-b) a capability that can run says what its state belongs to
(`Capability.state`): `step` (its proposal, draft, approval, task and budget are the step's, so it may stand twice in a
case; the rule) or `case` (one for the whole case, as the reconciliation's pairing and the email routing; a pack uses
such a capability once). D0-c: it names the form of its result (`Capability.output`, a `Result` model, with `output_version`); a step reference says which fields of the other step's result it reads and which versions of its form it takes (`step_ref(reads=, versions=)`), which the pack check compares with the other capability's form; the step kind checks a step's result against its form before it is kept, and `read_result` checks the result a step reads before it uses it (`ResultMismatch` stops the step before any effect); `DatasetRef` is the form a large list is handed on in, without its rows. Tests: `tests/test_capabilities_231.py`, `tests/test_step_state_231.py`, `tests/test_result_contract_231.py`.

**Process packs (231, plan stage A, A4).** A process pack (`configs/processes/<id>.json`, read and checked by
`jav/app/process_packs.py`) is one kind of business process described as data: its scope parts (231 adds `record`, the
package item a case starts from), its intake (an email intent that offers to start a case, or a person), its resources
(plain values for now), its steps (each a capability `name@version` with its parameters; a paid step starts with a
person), its tabs built from `configs/screen_elements.json`, its outputs and golden sets. The check refuses an unknown
capability, parameters that do not fit, a paid step without a person's start, an unknown element, scope part or
output, a dangling reference and any string naming a Python module. Since stage D0 (D0-a) it also refuses a step whose
capability cannot run (it is only described, as the line pricing), a step parameter declared as naming another step
(`capabilities.step_ref`: the send step's, the printed document's, the line composition's and the letter's `from_step`) that names no step or one the step
does not wait for, directly or through another, an element the catalogue lists as planned, and an element that would
show nothing: no data registered for its type and its step's capability, or for its type alone (`case_views.gives`).
Stage D (D-a; the reassessment after D0, P02): a step reference names the kind of step it reads
(`step_ref(of=)`: the line composition a match, the letter a composition); the check refuses a reference to a step of
another kind, and one left out where the pack has several such steps (it would have read the first while its
approval's basis named the one it waits for) or where the step does not wait for the only one
(`tests/test_step_source_231.py`). D-b2 (P05, T03): a reference may name the form it expects (`step_ref(expects=)`,
the printed document's: a version number); the check refuses a field the giver does not give, gives as another type (a
text where a number is expected, a bare number where money is) or may leave empty where the reader needs a value (a
subclass fits), and `read_result` checks the result against that form before any effect (`expected_result`;
`tests/test_step_types_231.py`). `process_runtime.start_case` reads the pack first, so a refused pack starts no case; a case page that still meets an
element it has no component for names it (`tests/test_pack_gate_231.py`, `ui/src/elements/elementCatalogue231.test.tsx`
compares the interface's components with the catalogue). `definition(pack)` is the process core's
definition of the pack's version (`pack_<id>`), each step's kind its capability's step kind, the pack's own settings
as `_`-prefixed parameters the capability does not see. The first pack is the IT quote (`it_quote.json`); its cases
start in A5. Tests: `tests/test_process_packs_231.py`.

**Starting a case by hand, and a process's example material (231, stage U, U0).** The processes list
(`GET /processes`) says for each pack whether a person may start a case by hand (an intake `manual`), what it is for
(the pack's `summary`, shown on the case list's New case page), what a case of it cannot start without now
(`process_settings.missing_at_start`: each resource of `needs_at_start` with no active version, without trying a start)
and whether it has example material. A pack's `examples` names that material as data: the sets of versioned things it
installs, each of a kind a module registers (`jav/app/examples.py` `register`; `jav/app/product_sets.py` registers
`product_set`, the invented product set), and a file of invented documents in packets (`configs/examples_<name>.json`:
each document's printed pages, made from a golden set by `python -m jav.cli examples --write`; a test keeps them equal to
what the measurement prints). Installing makes the examples' machine (`machine:examples`) each thing's maker and the
person its approver, so a product's four eyes hold; a thing the machine made reads as an example in the registry
(`example`). Removing takes the set out in one transaction, and is refused while any case keeps one of its things (the
core's `cases.holding_in`) or a rule proposal run names one, and for a thing a person made. A packet's documents are
written beside the store (`<store folder>/examples/<pack>/<packet>/`) and a case starts on those files. The product and
tariff parameters offer the registry's active things as choices, so a process's own value is picked, not typed.
(U1e) Two more kinds of sets: the invented price list of the quotes' golden set (`jav/domains/quotes/price_list_examples.py`,
`price_list`) and the invented own parties with their accounts (`jav/domains/finance/party_examples.py`, `own_party`; a
party goes only while no case and no record names it). A packet's document is either printed pages, written as a PDF,
or a text written as it is (the invented party's camt.053 statements), and a pack may add a sentence on how its material
is tried (`examples.note`: the reconciliation pairs what the documents and the bank statements processes read). An invented
mailbox (`configs/example_mailboxes.json`, `jav/domains/email/mailbox_examples.py`) is read by the runner its module
registers with the mailbox bridge (`mailbox_bridge.register_invented`) instead of the Outlook script: it sends each
message to the temporary email receiver as the Outlook bridge does, so the package is made as for a real download.
`scripts/dev.ps1 start` also starts the invented registers (`invented-services`, 127.0.0.1:8941) the insurance case's
lookups ask.

**A condition document read from its PDF (231, stage U, U-A2).** `jav/app/condition_import.py` reads an insurer's
condition document into the product registry's form by code alone (nothing goes to a model): its pages as a rule
proposal reads them (`jav/documents/page_text.py`: the text layer, else local text recognition; a page set in two
columns is read column by column, its left column's lines then its right one's, decided from where the text's pieces
stand: none across the middle but a few, the right ones starting at one place), the clause headings at the start of a
line (`3.1. Title`, `3.1.`; a table of contents' dot-leader lines are none), of which the longest run whose numbers rise
in the order printed counts (a stray number, a numbered item or a table of contents falls out), and the day it is in
force, the first date after "hatályos" on its first page. `python -m jav.cli condition-import <pdf> --id --kind --title
[--install]`: the machine (`machine:condition import`) adds it as a draft; a person makes it active.
A real product's source (`jav/app/product_sources.py`, `configs/product_source_<name>.json`: its documents by file
name and fingerprint, its frame and, U-A4, its tariff): `python -m jav.cli product-source <name> --folder <dir>
--install` reads the files and the machine (`machine:product assembly`) adds the documents and the product's frame as
drafts; once a person decided on the rules proposed from those documents, `--assemble` drafts the product's next
version naming the latest version of each document, every rule a person accepted or corrected (a rejected one never)
and the tariff (no new decision: nothing new). A person makes the documents, the rules and the product active, the
product by another person than its maker.

**A process pack's cases (231, plan stage A, A5).** `jav/app/process_runtime.py` `start_case(pack_id, scope, name,
actor)` makes the case's container (a general work package of kind `case`, so every table keyed by a package keeps
working) and, in one write transaction, its scope and the process core's case of the pack's version under the same id.
The core moves the case on through the capabilities' step kinds. A step the pack marks `start: person` waits for a
`step_start` human task; the decided task starts it, and the step then runs for that person (the machine acting for
them, `identity.acting_for`, as an automatic workflow step does). The quote's capabilities run on the container
(`jav/domains/quotes/capabilities.py` `run`): the request read with the pack's record type, the proposal job queued and
waited for with a retry timer, the draft started from the proposal and waited for until a person approves it (since
stage D0 the composition step's own approval task, `own_approval:<case>:<step>`), the priced document from the approved version the pack names (`from_step`), the letter
job queued and waited for. The IT quote's cases give the results the quote package's path recorded before stage A
(`tests/test_quote_case_231.py`, `tests/fixtures/quote_path_231.json`).

**A case's page as data (231, plan stage A, A6a).** `jav/app/case_views.py` `view(case_id)` is a case's page: the case,
its steps (state, phase, whether it waits for a person's start) and its pack's tabs, each element with its data. An
element's data is registered once for the pair (element type, capability), or for the type alone (`ElementData`), so
the element a step shows comes from the step's capability: the quote's capabilities register the quote view the quote
package's page had for their elements, and any pack whose step uses them gets it. `start_step` decides a step's start
task; the worker moves the case on. The service: `POST /api/cases` (pack, record, name), `GET /api/cases/{id}`, `POST
/api/cases/{id}/steps/{step}/start` (`jav/service/api_cases.py`); the quote's endpoints accept a case's container.
Tests: `tests/test_case_service_231.py`.

**A case's page in the interface (231, plan stage A, A6b).** `#/cases/<id>/<tab>` (`ui/src/views/CasePage.tsx`) shows a
case with the work package page's header and numbered tabs; the tabs are the pack's, each element rendered by its type
from `ui/src/elements/registry.tsx`, which may also give the tab's status line and the page's summary. The quote's
elements (`ui/src/domains/quotes/QuoteElements.tsx`) render the quote's parts from the quote view their data
carries, with the quote's status lines; asking for the proposal or the letter starts the case's step. The
email's "Start a quote from this email" starts an IT quote case (231 C1a: "Start a case from this email", the general intake), and the quotes made from a request list its cases too.
The interface's translation check reads the packs' title, tab labels and step titles. Tests: `ui/src/views/case231.test.tsx`.

**Older pack versions and the accepted letter (231, plan stage A, A7a, A7b).** A case keeps the version of its pack it
started on, its screens and outputs too: `process_runtime.pack_for(case_id)` reads the pack's file as it was from the
calculation snapshot the case was linked to when it started, so a case of 1.0.0 shows its own tabs after 1.1.0. The
`letter_draft` step is done once a person accepted the letter, not when the paragraph is written. Tests:
`tests/test_case_versions_231.py`, `tests/test_quote_case_231.py`.

**Sending (231, plan stage A, A7c).** The general `send@1` capability (`jav/app/sending.py`) sends what a step made by
the mode its pack names (the owner's decision of 2026-10-24: each process chooses): `output_folder` (a file under
`<output folder>/<pack>/<case name>_<case id>/`, never overwriting another file), `outlook_draft` and `outlook_send`
(their sender comes with the Outlook bridge, A8; until then such a step fails as not available). A message builder
(`MessageBuilder`, registered by its capability's domain: the quote's accepted letter with its Word document,
`quote_letter`) makes the message from the result of the step the pack names (`from_step`). The message is recorded
with the case first (the process core's handover record, the file's bytes with it) and handed over once under its id
(`<case>:<step>:<attempt>`). A refused handover fails the step, and a person's retry sends it again as a new attempt; a
handover whose outcome is unknown opens a `handover_settle` task the step waits for, and the person's answer
(`settle`) settles it as arrived or not. The real send reaches only the recipients on the allowlist of
`configs/send.json`, which is empty; only an owner's decision adds one. Tests: `tests/test_send_231.py`.

**Waiting for a reply and a person's actions (231, plan stage A, A7d).** The general `wait_reply@1` capability
(`jav/app/replies.py`) waits for the other side's answer: the `reply` event (`replied` or `not_needed`), sent today by
a person and later by the email intake (stage C). The step arms its `remind` timer for the days its pack sets (the
owner's decision of 2026-10-24: 5 for the IT quote); when it fires, a `follow_up` task opens once, without a deadline,
and the step waits for it: the person records the reply, or that they reminded the other side (`keep_waiting`), after
which the step waits on with no further reminder. A capability may offer a person's actions on its step
(`capabilities.Action`: a name, an English label, whether the step waits for it now, and what it does): the send step's
"it arrived" / "it did not arrive", the reply step's "a reply arrived", "no reply is needed" and "I reminded them".
A case's page lists each running step's available actions, and `POST /api/cases/{case_id}/steps/{step_id}/actions/{name}`
(`case_views.act`) takes one. The "Waiting for me" list opens a case's page for a task of a process pack's case (a
step's start, an uncertain handover, a follow-up), named by its step. Tests: `tests/test_replies_231.py`.

**The IT quote's send and follow-up, and the case lists (231, plan stage A, A7e).** The IT quote's pack 1.1.0 adds the
send step (`send@1`, a person starts it, the accepted letter with the approved version's Word document into the output
folder, the mode a resource of the pack) and the follow-up (`wait_reply@1`, 5 days), and three tabs: Sent
(`handover_view`: the mode, the output folder, each attempt), Follow-up (`reply_wait`: since when, when the follow-up
opens, the reply) and History (`timeline`: the case's events without the core's own bookkeeping; `cost`: what each
step spent, from its own rounds' budget scopes or the ones its capability names, `Capability.spent`, as the quote's
proposal and letter jobs keep theirs). A case of 1.0.0 keeps its three tabs (A7a). The case's next step comes from the
first step that is not done, through the elements that show it. A failed step offers its retry (`retry`) among its
actions. Two datasets (`jav/app/case_lists.py`): `cases` (every open case, the closed ones on request: its process,
the step it is at and what that step waits for) and `handovers` (the open cases whose step's last handover failed or
has an unknown outcome). The interface: a "Cases" entry in the side rail (`#/cases`, `ui/src/views/CaseList.tsx`, the
handovers to check above the cases while there are some), and the general elements (`ui/src/elements/CaseElements.tsx`,
their status lines in `caseStatus.ts`). Tests: `tests/test_case_send_231.py`, `tests/test_quote_case_231.py`,
`ui/src/views/case231e.test.tsx`.

**The send's Outlook ways (231, plan stage A, A8).** The email domain registers the senders of the `outlook_draft` and
`outlook_send` modes (`jav/domains/email/outlook_drafts.py`): the recorded email file is unpacked into a temporary
folder (its attachments by name only) and handed to `scripts/outlook_draft.ps1`, which puts it into the desktop
Outlook already running as a draft in the Drafts folder, or with `-Send` sends it (the sender asks for that only on the
real send's mode and checks the allowlist again). Outlook not running: nothing is written, exit code 3, the handover is
refused. A script that stopped midway or did not answer in 90 s leaves the outcome unknown, and a person checks the
Drafts folder. The mailbox download (`outlook_bridge.ps1`, `mail_bridge_call.ps1`) stays read-only, which a test checks
in their text. Tests: `tests/test_outlook_draft_231.py`.

**Golden sets and the package list without the quote package (231, plan stage A, A3d).** A golden set may be a process
pack's: layer `process_pack`, owned by a pack that names it among its golden sets (`jav/evals/goldens.py`
`logic_pack_goldens`, after the logic packs'); the quote's three (`quote:lines`, `quote:matches`, `quote:letters`) are
the IT quote pack's, under their keys of before. A container kind gives the package list's cache mark too
(`LogicPack.list_mark`, `logic_packs.containers`): a case's row reads the process core's cases, steps and tasks
(`cases.marks`) and the marks of its page's element data (`case_views.ElementData.mark`, `case_views.marks`; the
quote's are its price lists', proposal's, draft's and letter's). Tests: `tests/test_golden_owners_204.py`,
`tests/test_case_send_231.py`.

**The quote package retired (231, plan stage A, A3d).** The quote package's logic pack, its page and its creation are
gone: a quote is a case of the IT quote's process pack. The quote's own addresses (`jav/domains/quotes/api_quote_package.py`,
which a case's page uses) are the quotes domain's (`SERVICE`), and accept a case's container only; the draft's, the
proposal's and the letter's operations work on it as before. The wizard's group Quotes and its kind of work `quote`
stay (`quote_package.STEP`; its form starts a case, and its package is a case's container) until the wizard lists the
process packs themselves. The quote's
status lines read the quote view (`ui/src/domains/quotes/quoteStatus.ts`). The recorded path of stage A runs on a case's
container now, with the same record (`tests/test_quote_path_231.py`). The frozen list of process-specific parts lost
the logic pack `quote` and its interface entry (`tests/test_process_strictness_231.py`).

**A pack's parameters reach the code (231, plan stage B, B0).** The draft step's catalogue and the letter step's frame
were declared in the IT quote's pack but not passed on (the defaults happened to be the same): `quote_draft.start`
takes `catalogue` (a draft from the request is priced from the list it names) and `quote_letter.request` takes
`frame`, kept with the letter (`quote_letters.frame`, an additive column) and used for its body, subject and file
name; an unknown frame is refused. Tests: `tests/test_quote_draft_192.py`, `tests/test_quote_letter_196.py`.

**The partner register (231, plan stage B, B1).** The shared register of the outside parties the processes deal with
(`jav/app/partners.py`; tables `partners`, `partner_versions`): each partner with the roles it plays
(`configs/partners.json`: customer, supplier, insured, broker), every change a version through the shared versioning
(`jav/core/versions.py`: a save inserts a draft and makes it active, the one before is superseded; retired rather than
deleted, restored with the version it was retired with). The same tax number (its party key) or, without one, the same
name (its significant words, the own parties' rule) as another active partner is a `PartnerConflict` naming it; a save
from a page that shows an older version is one too. The register starts empty (the owner's decision of 2026-10-24);
a domain proposes partners through a registered suggestion source (`SuggestionSource`): the finance domain gives an
incoming invoice's supplier and an outgoing invoice's buyer, never an own party (`party_views.partner_suggestions`),
and the register leaves out the parties already recorded. The service: `GET/POST /api/partners`, `GET
/api/partners/suggestions`, `GET/PUT /api/partners/{id}`, `POST /api/partners/{id}/retire|restore`
(`jav/service/api_partners.py`; a conflict is 409 with the other partner's id). Tests: `tests/test_partners_231.py`.

**A case's partners and the register's page (231, plan stage B, B1).** A process pack names the partners its cases are
about (`ProcessPack.partners`, `PartnerLink`: a role, the step whose result holds the record's fields, and which fields
give the partner's name, tax number, email, phone or address; the pack check refuses an unknown role, a missing step
or a link without a name). The IT quote's pack 1.2.0: the request's customer in the customer role, from the request
step, whose result now carries the record's own fields. The shared element `partner_link` (`case_views._partner_link`)
shows each link: the linked partner, or the values the record gives with the active partner they seem to be
(`partners.match`); a person links one (`case_views.link_partner`, `POST /api/cases/{case_id}/partners`), which the
case's scope names in the general part `partners` (`scopes.PARTNERS`, {role: partner id}). The interface: Settings ›
Partners (`ui/src/views/settings/PartnersPanel.tsx`: the list with search and a role filter, a partner's values saved
as a new version, its versions, retire and restore, a conflict leading to the other partner, the domains' proposals
on request) and the case page's partner card (`ui/src/elements/CaseElements.tsx` `PartnerLink`: link the partner it
seems to be, or record the values as a partner and link it). The client reads a conflict's `other_id`
(`ApiError.detail.other`). Tests: `tests/test_case_partners_231.py`, `ui/src/views/settings/partners231.test.tsx`.

**A process's own values (231, plan stage B, B2).** A process pack names its resources in its file (which price list,
the letter frame, the output, the send mode, and `template:<output>`, the output template its cases use); a process
may set its own value over any of them, kept in the store (`jav/app/process_settings.py`, table
`process_setting_versions`, keyed `<pack>/<kind>/<key>`, every change a version through the shared versioning). A
person saves a value as a draft (`propose`), checked as the pack would be with it: the pack check runs with the value
over the resources, so the capabilities' own checks refuse a price list with no active version, an unknown letter frame
or an unknown output (their parameter validators, `jav/domains/quotes/capabilities.py`); a template must be an active
own template of that output or the built-in one. A draft changes nothing until it is made active (`activate`, checked
again); retiring the process's value brings the file's back. Who may make a value active is a rule
(`configs/process_settings.json`: `editor` by default, the owner's decision of 2026-10-24; `owner`, the listed owners;
`second_person`, anyone but the one who saved the draft), refused with `SettingRefused` (403). A case takes the values
active when it starts (`process_runtime.start_case` builds its definition from `effective_resources`, and keeps them in
its data, `resource_of`), so a change reaches the cases started after it; an output with no template chosen on the
package takes the one the process set (`outputs.templates._chosen`). The service: `GET /api/processes`, `GET
/api/processes/{pack_id}/settings`, `POST /api/processes/{pack_id}/settings/{key}`, `POST
.../{key}/versions/{version}/activate`, `POST .../{key}/retire` (`jav/service/api_processes.py`; an unknown pack is 404).
Stage B3 adds a rule's values under the same table (kind `rule`). Tests: `tests/test_process_settings_231.py`.

**A process's own rule values (231, plan stage B, B3).** A capability names the keys of its rule files a process may
set for its own cases (`Capability.rule_keys`, `capabilities.RuleKey`: the config name among its `rule_files`, the
dotted key, a whole number between bounds or one of some options, and one English line); the registry refuses a key the
file does not hold or whose file value does not fit. A key that would change what is asked from a paid service, or a
budget, is never one: those stay changes of the file. The IT quote's: how many requested items one proposal matches
(fewer only), how many lines a quote holds, the unit a forint and another currency's amounts are rounded to, and how
long the model's letter paragraph may be. A person saves a value (`process_settings.propose(..., kind="rule")`, the
same versions, activation rule and service addresses as a resource's, under `/api/processes/{pack_id}/rules/{key}`,
the key `<config name>.<dotted key>`). A case keeps the values active when it started (its data: `rules`, and
`rule_settings`, each value's stored setting and version). While code works on a case, its values lie over the rule
files: `cfg.overlay` is a context of the call, and `cfg.load` returns the file with them in place (a key the file no
longer holds is left out with a warning), while `config_hash` and `version` stay the file's. The context is set by the
capability's step kind (its run, events and timers, from the case's data), by the worker for a job whose payload names
a case's package (`worker.handle`), and by the service for a request whose address names a case or a package
(`api.CaseRoute`, the parts `case_id` and `wp_id`; `capabilities.case_rules`). A decision traced meanwhile records the
values beside the file it read (`process_values`: key, value, setting, version), and `trace.replay` runs the rule with
them again (or with other values, `over`). The quotes' two decision points read such keys: an approved quote's totals
(`quote.amounts`, recorded at approval, its rule `quote_pricing.amounts` computing them again with the currency's
rounding unit) and the check of the model's letter paragraph (`quote.letter_check`, its length and the patterns found,
never the text, against `letter.max_chars`). `process_settings.preview(pack_id, key, value)` (`POST
/api/processes/{pack_id}/rules/{key}/preview`) tries a value on the process's traced decisions that read the key
(`trace.reading`): each rule run again with the values it was made with and with the value in place, the decisions,
the changed ones with examples and the failed ones counted; nothing is written and no AI is asked. A key no traced
decision reads yet (a quote's lines, a proposal's items) previews as no decision. Tests:
`tests/test_process_rules_231.py`.

**A case keeps the versions of its start (231, stage D0, D0-e; the owner's decision of 2026-10-26).** A case runs to
its end with the price list, template and rule files it started with; a newer one reaches only the cases started after
it. At its start (`process_runtime.start_case`, and a case taken over or wrapped likewise) each resource naming a
versioned thing is resolved to its version then (`process_settings.kept_resources`): a parameter whose capability keeps
it (`Capability.at_start`, `capabilities.AtStart`: the quote's `catalogue`, `newest_active` or a named list becoming
`<list>@<version>`) and an output's template (`template:<output>`, an own template's active version; the built-in one
is the code's). A version reference is the shared versions' (`versions.split`, `ref`, `VersionTable.at`, `kept`), so
`price_lists.chosen` and `templates._chosen` read the version named, whatever its state now; the quote's steps and its
service addresses price from the step's own list (`quote_steps.catalogue`: a person asking again, a draft started from
the request, the proposal's staleness and the price list the quote page shows). The case names in its data the rule
files its steps' capabilities read (`capabilities.KEPT_RULES`); where one changed since, its work reads the copy its
start's calculation snapshot holds (`capabilities.case_overlay` with the case: `cfg.Overlay.base`, `cfg.Kept`, built
once per pair of snapshots), with a key added to the file since taken from the file (`cfg.filled`), so newer code reads
what it expects; `config_hash` and `version` name that copy, and a traced decision records it (`kept: true`, its
version and fingerprint) and replays with it. A kept copy of another main version is not read differently: reading it
raises `cfg.RulesUnfit` with the file and both versions, which stops the step that reads it (`kinds.Fail`), and the
command `cases-kept` lists, before a release, the open cases it would stop (`process_runtime.unfit_cases`; on a copy of
the live store, `--store`). The case's history names on its start what it keeps (`process_runtime.kept_of`, the
timeline element). A case started before D0-e keeps nothing and reads the files and the active versions, as before.
Stage D (D-a; the reassessment after D0, P04): a step parameter naming a versioned thing itself (a price list's id or
`newest_active`, not a resource) is kept at its version too (`process_settings.at_start`, which the start, the taking
over and the wrapping of a case use); a pack names the resources a case cannot start without (`needs_at_start`), and
with none of them to keep (no active version) the case does not start (`MissingAtStart`, before anything is made),
rather than read another version later (`tests/test_case_tariff_at_start_231.py`). D-a (P03): a key added to a kept
file since takes the value of the first later version that had it (`rulebook.later_in`; a service or worker stores its
configs when it starts, `jav.runtime.preload`), so a later change of that value does not reach an old case; a decision
traced meanwhile stores the content it read (`effective_sha256`, `rulebook.store_content_in` under
`<path>#effective`), which its replay reads again (`tests/test_case_rules_over_time_231.py`). A new key that changes the
business meaning is a new main version (`docs/guides/CONFIGS.md`).
Limits: only the rule files the capabilities declare are kept (a non-JSON file, a module's copy taken when it loaded,
and the process pack itself, which a case keeps by its version, are not). Tests: `tests/test_case_keeps_versions_231.py`,
`ui/src/elements/caseKept231.test.tsx`.

**Typed values (231, stage D, D-b1; the reassessment after D0, P05).** `jav/core/measures.py`: an amount of money
carries its currency (`Money`: sums and comparisons in one currency only, `CurrencyMismatch` otherwise; rounded by a
named `Rounding` unit and mode), a quantity its unit (`Quantity`), a rate whether it is per hundred or per thousand
(`Percentage`, `PerMille`, which never mix), a version its days (`Validity`); each round-trips through JSON exactly
(an amount as text), and a float, NaN or infinity is refused. A result hands such values on instead of bare numbers
(`tests/test_measures_231.py`). D-b3 (P06): `jav/app/facts.py`: a fact catalogue (`configs/facts_<id>.json`,
a rule file a case keeps) names the levels of what is assessed and each fact a rule may read, typed; an assessed unit
(`Unit`: durable id, level, parent, typed facts, each with its `SourceClaim`: value, original form, source, how sure,
which reading or correction made it) and the checked input a person accepted (`CheckedInput`: the units, a concrete
product version, the day cover starts) are typed by it, and a wrong unit, currency, level, code or parent is refused
with what is wrong (`tests/test_facts_231.py`). No process or business is named in the code: the catalogue is data.
D-b4 (P06, P10): `jav/app/assessed_units.py`: a step keeps its assessed units (table `assessed_units`, keyed by case,
step and a durable unit id); every change is a decision journal row (kind `assessed_unit`: add, save, remove) with the
facts and their sources, a change on an older state is refused (`RevisionConflict`), a removed unit stays visible and
takes no correction, and a fact that does not fit writes nothing; the units list page by page (dataset
`assessed_units`), a result names them by a `DatasetRef` (`reference`), and the checked input frozen from them does not
follow a later correction (`tests/test_assessed_units_231.py`).

**Products, condition documents and rules (231, stage D, D-c1).** `jav/app/product_rules.py`: a product (its cover
modules, the condition documents and rules it is offered under), each condition document (kind, days it applies,
clauses and pages) and each rule (a decision table over a catalogue's facts on one level, giving catalogue outcomes,
with its source: document version, page, clause) is a versioned thing on the shared versions (tables
`condition_documents`, `product_rules`, `products`). A rule's own activation checks it against its catalogue (facts it
reads exist on its level or above, comparisons typed with the right unit and currency, known outcomes); a product
version is the release manifest naming exact versions, made active only by another person than its maker
(`SecondPersonNeeded`) and only when no rule is a draft or missing, each cites a document of the product and a clause it
has, and the modules a module requires are there (`ManifestRefused` with every problem). A superseded or retired
version stays readable by its reference; a case keeps the product version of its start (`kept`, `named`)
(`tests/test_product_rules_231.py`). D-c2: `jav/app/rule_evaluation.py` evaluates the rules that apply at quoting to
a requested module on every unit of their level; a unit sees its own and its ancestors' facts; a decision table gives
the first or every matching row's outcomes; a row a missing fact leaves undecided makes the rule's outcome unknown and
names the fact (one false whatever it is, is skipped); a unit's outcomes combine by the catalogue's rule (worst
verdict, largest or smallest, union, exactly one: two different are a blocker), and the assessment's verdict is the
worst of its units'. Each rule's outcome on a unit is a trace row (point `rules.outcome`: the rule, its table and the
facts it read, typed), whose replay gives the same outcome (`tests/test_rule_evaluation_231.py`). D-c3: a tariff
is a versioned thing of the registry too (kind `tariff`, table `tariffs`; the owner's decision of 2026-10-26: its own
store), and `jav/app/rating.py` prices the requested modules in a fixed order: the basis (a money fact) times the
module's rate per thousand, its factors in their order (each from a fact the unit sees or an outcome of the rules on it
or its ancestors, through the factor's table), rounded once per line, the module minimum, the modules summed in the
tariff's currency, the total factors (read from the top unit), rounded again, the policy minimum; every step
itemised. A missing factor, a value no row takes, a cover start out of force or another currency gives no total but a
blocker. The calculation is a trace row (point `rating.premium`, the tariff inline) that replays the same
(`tests/test_rating_231.py`). D-c4: a capability's parameter naming a product or a tariff keeps the version active at
the case's start like a price list (`product_rules.keeper(kind)`, its `at_start`), so a newer version reaches only the
cases started after it; a pack refuses a `needs_at_start` resource that no step keeps at a version (a plain value has
none, and the check would pass silently) (`tests/test_product_at_start_231.py`). D-c5: a product set is kept as data
(`jav/app/product_sets.py`; the invented insurer: `configs/golden_products.json`): condition documents with each
clause's text, rules, tariffs and products, checked to hold together (every rule against its catalogue and the cited
page and clause, its quote found in the clause's text, every reference within the set), installed into a store as first
versions, drafts by one person and made active by another (a store holding another content of one of its things refuses
the whole set before writing), with reference cases whose hand-worked verdict, outcomes, lines and total are compared
with the evaluation and the rating (`product-set` command; `tests/test_product_set_231.py`). A tariff module may price
only the units whose facts fit its `only` (burglary does not price a building); a unit without such a fact is a
blocker. D-c6: the registry on the local service (`jav/service/api_products.py`: the products, documents, rules,
tariffs and catalogues, `product_rules.overview()`; a version made active, a thing retired) and in Settings (Products,
Tariffs: `ui/src/views/settings/ProductsPanel.tsx`, `TariffsPanel.tsx`, their shared parts `RegistryParts.tsx`). A
draft lists what keeps it from being made active (`product_rules.problems_of`); the registry's and the fact catalogue's
problems are sentences a person reads in the interface's language (`jav/core/user_messages.said`: a `str` with its
template, parameters and where it applies, the counterpart of `explained` for an error; the interface's `saidText`), and
a refused activation sends every one (422 `manifest_refused`). The fact catalogue names how its levels and codes read
(`labels`), and a tariff has a title (`tests/test_product_registry_service_231.py`,
`ui/src/views/settings/productRegistry231.test.tsx`). D-c7a: a set's condition documents are written as PDFs, every
clause on the page it says (`product_sets.write_documents`, `product-set --write-pdfs DIR`; the invented PDF writer
writes several pages, `synthetic_pdf.write_unicode_pdf_pages`, and one page byte for byte as before), so a rule
proposal reads the invented documents as it will read a real file; read back, each rule finds its quote on the page it
cites (`tests/test_product_set_pdfs_231.py`). D-c7b: a document's text by page (`jav/documents/page_text.py`: the text
layer, else local text recognition, never the paid one; a page carrying a marking line, "Internal", is left out whole and
never sent to a model) and the code's checks of a proposed rule before any model judges it (`jav/app/rule_checks.py`,
`configs/rule_proposals.json`): it fits its catalogue; it cites the document it was read from, a page that was read and
(for a registered document) a clause on that page; its quote is on the cited page; and each number of its decision table
stands in the document (in the quote, on the page, elsewhere, as a word, derived from a stated percentage or a boundary
one away from a stated count, a factor of 1) or is listed for a person to confirm (not found, or read by text
recognition). A clause's own number at the start of its heading, a page number and either side of a slash are not
read as a rule's value; a thousands group the line broke is joined first. Every finding is a translated sentence;
a blocking one keeps the proposal from being accepted as it is (`tests/test_rule_checks_231.py`). D-c7c: one structured
answer from GPT through the call log and a budget, for a new call site (`jav/ai/gpt_answer.py`: the model from the
shared settings, the call reserved, a repeated question answered from the saved one, every attempt in the ledger, a
stand-in model for a block; nothing asked without a budget; D-h: an answer cut off at its output cap ends the call at
once, and a call that failed after an answer came back is settled at that answer's cost with why it was not taken,
through `jav/ai/output_cap.py`, which the extraction shares; the answer's form is strict, so the provider holds the
model to it, every field required), and the rule proposer (`jav/app/rule_proposals.py`,
`configs/gpt_rule_proposal.json`): a document's pages a few at a time (never a page its marking keeps out), after the
fact catalogue and the cover modules, answered with rule records (each value as JSON text of the catalogue's type, the
page, clause and quoted words), each turned into a registry rule (or kept with why it is none) and checked by code. A
stand-in proposer answers with a product set's rules for the pages asked, free (`tests/test_rule_proposals_231.py`).
D-c7d: JEV's judgement of how far the cited page supports a proposal code found nothing blocking in
(`jav/app/rule_support.py`, `configs/callsites/rule_proposal.json`): one request asks which kind of rule the page states
there (or none), whether it states each row of the decision table and whether it names something the rule leaves out,
with the page verbatim and the rule written out by code in English; the answer is a mark (supported, to check,
contradicted) on the default bands, never an acceptance. Without JEV the proposal is not asked and keeps the code's
checks (the engine matrix's `skipped`: GPT judging its own proposal would be no independent check)
(`tests/test_rule_support_231.py`). D-c7e: proposal runs (`jav/app/rule_proposal_runs.py`, tables `rule_proposal_runs`,
`rule_proposals`; the worker's job `rule_propose`, which the worker's layer names for the application,
`jav/runtime/app_jobs.py`): a file read page by page under the run's budget, every proposal kept
with what was answered, its rule or why none, the code's findings, where each number stands and JEV's mark (and the
pages as read, so a correction is checked again on the same text). A person accepts a proposal (nothing blocking, every
number the document does not print confirmed), corrects it (checked again) or rejects it with a reason; an accepted or
corrected one becomes a draft rule of the registry in the same transaction (`product_rules.add_in`), its origin naming
the run and the proposal; each decision is in the decision journal (kind `rule_proposal`), and one on a stale page is
refused. Commands: `rule-propose` (GPT within `--budget-usd`, JEV with `--verify`, or `--stand-in SET`, free),
`rule-proposals`, `rule-proposal-decide` (`tests/test_rule_proposal_runs_231.py`). D-c7g: Settings › Products ›
Proposals (`ui/src/views/settings/ProposalsView.tsx`): a run started (the stand-in, free, or GPT within a budget its
button shows), its proposals with the code's check, JEV's mark and the decision, a proposal's decision table, findings
and numbers (each with its kind, so a code is shown as written), and a person's accept, correct or reject
(`ui/src/views/settings/ruleProposals231.test.tsx`); the service names the stand-in sets (`product_sets.SETS`). D-c7h: the
golden set `rules:proposals` (`jav/evals/rule_proposal_golden.py`, layer `rules` of `jav/evals/goldens.py`): the invented
documents written as PDFs and proposed from within the round's budget (JEV judging each with the JEV engine), every
proposal matched to the 16 expected rules by its clause and kind, exact, differing (in kind, level, facts, modules, way
of deciding, rows or page), missed or extra, with what code blocked and JEV's false acceptances (a "supported" on a
differing proposal); the stand-in scores 16 of 16 (`tests/test_rule_proposal_golden_231.py`). D-c7f: the same on the
local service (`jav/service/api_products.py`: `/rule-proposals`; a refused decision answers 422 `proposal_refused` with
every problem, a stale page 409).

**The case steps of stage D (231, D-d).** D-d1: a step whose parameters name a rule file (a fact catalogue) keeps it as
the case started (`Capability.reads`, gathered by `process_runtime._kept_rule_files`), so a catalogue changed later
reaches only the cases started after it; a step reference marked optional (`step_ref(optional=True)`), left out, names
no step rather than the pack's only one of its kind; the assessed units are read in the caller's transaction
(`assessed_units.units_in`, `state_in`, `checked_input_in`); an approval task may go to anyone
(`human_tasks.own_approval_waits(assignee=None)`) (`tests/test_kept_catalogue_231.py`). D-d2: the field check
(`jav/app/field_check.py`, `field_check@1`, tables `field_check_units`, `field_check_claims`): the facts the steps it
waits for offer (`claims`, each naming its unit by the offering step's key, level and parent's key) make the units, fill
the facts still missing or agree with them; a differing value is a conflict left to the person, never chosen; each claim
is placed once and traced (point `claims.offered`). The person corrects the units and freezes them with the day cover
starts: the checked input, for the product version the case kept, becomes the step's own approved version with the
result versions it built on (the owner's decision of 2026-10-26); a stale page is refused, and a reopened check waits for
a new freeze (`tests/test_field_check_231.py`). D-d3: the rules and the premium as steps (`jav/app/evaluation_steps.py`,
table `evaluation_records`): `rule_evaluation@1` evaluates the rules of the product version the frozen input names, for
the cover modules its top unit asks for (`requested_modules`; an input that does not say them has every module's rules
evaluated, is no better than unknown and names the missing fact), each rule's outcome on each unit traced
(`rules.outcome`); `rating@1` computes the premium from the tariff the case kept at its start (refused when it is not the
product's), itemised and traced (`rating.premium`), and a blocker of the rules or the tariff stops it with the record
kept, never a default. U-A8: a tariff's blocker is a sentence with its template (`user_messages.said`; its text, which
the trace keeps, as before); the record keeps it in a person's words too (`blockers_said`: the unit by its name, the
fact, factor and module by their titles, a code by its label, which the interface translates: `Said.labels`), and the
stopped step gives them as its reasons (`kinds.Fail.reasons`, kept in the failure's history row), shown by the
calculation's card and the case's Overview instead of the note. Each record belongs to the result version it was made from (`rule_evaluation.to_json`,
`from_json`: the evaluation read back as the same typed values), so a later turn makes nothing new and a new freeze
makes a new evaluation and premium; every reference case of the invented set runs as a case to the verdict, missing
facts, total and blockers it expects (`tests/test_evaluation_steps_231.py`). D-d4: a person's decision on another step's
result (`jav/app/case_decisions.py`, `decision@1`), one capability for each decision a case makes (risk, then premium: the
owner's decision of 2026-10-26): the pack names the step decided on and its choices (their titles, the values of the
result a choice needs, the outcome a choice closes the case with, which needs a reason); the decision is the step's own
approved version with the result version it was made on, so a stale page is refused, a changed result puts it out of
date and a person may return it (`case_views.return_step`). Above a limit of `configs/case_decisions.json` (kept as the
case started; the one limit there is invented), the decision is refused to the maker of the earlier decision the pack
names (`AnotherDeciderNeeded`), its task goes to anyone, and whether a second person was needed is traced
(`decision.second_person`); an amount in another currency is refused, never converted. An own version's record names
its approver's id too (`approved_by_id`) (`tests/test_case_decisions_231.py`). D-d5: an outside check
(`jav/app/lookups.py`, `lookup@1`): one register asked through its connector with facts another step offers (the
question's fields by fact; a missing required fact means nothing is asked), its answer's fields offered as facts of the
same unit (`claims`, each naming the register as its reading), which the field check places like a document's: a
differing value is a conflict left to the person. Two lookups of a case are independent branches: a register that
does not answer stops only its own step (busy: asked again in a minute), which a person retries. The fact catalogue
1.3.0 adds what they ask with and answer (company name, tax number, register status, on a sanctions list); no rule
reads them yet (`tests/test_lookups_231.py`). D-d6: waiting for the facts the rules miss
(`jav/app/missing_info.py`, `missing_info@1`): when the evaluation names missing facts, a person's task (`missing_info`)
opens with them and a deadline of the days the pack sets (the step's own deadline, the owner's decision of 2026-10-26 on
the D plan; a reply's follow-up keeps none); the person enters them in the field check and freezes it again, and once
the evaluation misses nothing the task is withdrawn and the step is done (nothing missing at all: done at once, no
task). The deadline passing, or a person saying with a reason that the facts will not come (the step's action), closes
the case with the pack's outcome (`tests/test_missing_info_231.py`). Stage U (the walkthrough): a missing fact is named on the unit that holds it at its own level (`rule_evaluation._holder`), so a site's fact a rule on its asset groups reads is asked for once, on the site. U-A8: given the case's kept tariff (`tariff`,
the premium step's), the step asks for the facts the premium reads that the input does not give too, each on the unit
that holds it at its level (`rating.missing_facts`), so a case no longer stops at the premium for a fact nobody was
asked for (both insurance offer packs). Stage U (the case page): a running step carries what its wait means as its capability says (`waits_on`: a person's start, the machine's work, a person), so the Overview names a live run's wait as the machine's and the page asks again while it works and for a while after a person's action; a step a closed case never reached shows as not run; a closed case offers its pack's follow-ups (`process_runtime.follow_ups`, `follow_up`: `POST /cases/{id}/follow-ups`). The data check lets a person keep the value shown in a disagreement (`field_check.keep`, the step's action `keep_value`, journaled as `field_check_claim`) and offers the day cover starts from a unit's fact or the last freeze (`cover_start_hint`). D-d7: the whole path from these capabilities
alone, in a test-only pack: an invented reading with two facts left out, the two outside checks, the field check, the
evaluation finding them missing and the task that waits for them, the facts entered and frozen again, the risk
decision, the premium from the kept tariff and the premium decision by another person above the limit; the case is done
and every decision on the way replays the same (`tests/test_assessment_path_231.py`). No step names insurance: the
product, its rules, its tariff, the registers and the limit are data.

**The insurance application's documents (231, stage D, D-e).** D-e1: a detection type of its own (the owner's decision
of 2026-10-26: a new main type) with three type packs (see the pack table). D-e2: a document's values become facts of
the assessed units by a rule file kept as a case started (`jav/app/fact_readings.py`,
`configs/fact_readings_property.json`): per type pack, the units its values describe (the policyholder, a site by its
number, an asset group by its row), each fact from a header field, a row's column or a list's column (the covers asked
for), through a code table where the document prints a word (compared without case, extra spaces or punctuation at its
ends), an amount with the currency the document states; what cannot be read is listed with why, never guessed (a word
no table knows, an amount without its currency, a count that is not whole, a unit the document does not name). The
invented documents (`jav/evals/property_documents.py`, `configs/golden_property_documents.json`): eight sets, 23
documents (a quote request form, a property schedule per site, a claims history statement), each kept as what a correct
reading gives; its printed page is made from those values in its layout (a schedule on two pages), so the code's own
source check finds every value on it. Each set records the facts its documents give (the units, what is not read, what
two documents give differently); the five sets of the product set's reference cases give that case's very facts and
covers (`tests/test_property_documents_231.py`, `tests/test_fact_readings_231.py`). The fact catalogue 1.4.0 adds the
site's address. `facts.plain`: a typed value as JSON (moved from the rule evaluation, which keeps the name). D-e3:
their golden set `insurance_application:documents` (`jav/evals/goldens.py`, measured by
`jav/evals/property_documents.py`): each document written as a PDF, recognised (its main and its detailed type) and read
with its own pack, judged field by field and row by row, and each set's facts read from what was read against the facts
it records; without a budget only earlier answers count, so its first round is the paid measurement of D-h. Stand-ins
test the scorer (`tests/test_property_documents_231.py`).

**The insurance quote's process (231, stage D, D-f).** D-f1: the facts a case's processed documents hold
(`jav/app/fact_claims.py`, `fact_claims@1`): what counts now for each document of a document processing step's package
(its latest result, a person's corrections over it, and where each value was printed after them) read by the fact
readings the case kept (`readings`), in the rule file's order of types (a parent first), each fact naming the document,
its file, page and words; a document no reading takes is listed, a value not read is listed with why
(`tests/test_fact_claims_231.py`). D-f2: a person's actions with values (`capabilities.Action.form`, `form_action`): the
case step's action endpoint takes `values`, the form checks them (422), and each such action carries the state of the
page it was made on, so an older one is refused (409); the page marks them (`form`), so its generic buttons leave them to
the step's own element, and an action may be offered on a done step too (`on_done`: a reopening). The field check's:
freeze, reopen (with a reason) and a unit added, saved or removed; the decision's: decide (`tests/test_case_actions_231.py`). D-f3: the process pack `configs/processes/insurance_offer.json` (shared capabilities only):
documents (`process_documents@1`) → facts from them (`fact_claims@1`) → the company register and the sanctions list
(`lookup@1`, two branches) → the data check (`field_check@1`) → the rules (`rule_evaluation@1`) → the missing facts
(`missing_info@1`) → the risk decision (`decision@1`) → the premium (`rating@1`) → the premium decision (`decision@1`,
another person above the invented limit); its five work views are filled by stage D-f's elements. A case runs from the
invented reference set's PDFs to the accepted premium, its processing run written as a correct reading gives it
(`tests/test_insurance_offer_path_231.py`). D-f4: the Data check view: the shared `field_review` element (built from
the planned list; its data the field check's: the units with their sources, the claims, the catalogue, the frozen
version and the page's next step `facts_check`), and two special elements, `document_facts` (`fact_claims@1`: each
document with how many facts it gave and what it could not read) and `lookup` (`lookup@1`: the register, what it was
asked with by fact, the facts it offers; the catalogue of the field check it offers them to); the interface's
`ui/src/elements/FactElements.tsx`. A reading's reason is one of a few sentences (the interface translates it), the
words no table knows in its value (`ui/src/elements/factElements231.test.tsx`). D-f5: the Calculation and versions
view: three special elements, `evaluation` (`rule_evaluation@1`: the latest evaluation in full, its units named by their
own level's facts, the rules' titles and citations, every version), `calculation` (`rating@1`: the latest premium's
lines, the modules' totals and minimums, the total, the tariff's and the factors' titles, the product's module names,
every version) and `missing_info` (`missing_info@1`: what is missing with its unit, the task's deadline or how it ended,
and the page's next step `missing_facts`); `evaluation_steps.records`, `unit_names`; the interface's
`ui/src/elements/AssessmentElements.tsx` (`ui/src/elements/assessmentElements231.test.tsx`). D-f6: the Decision and
release view: the special element `decision` (`decision@1`: what it decides on and its result versions, the choices with
whether the result allows each, the second person, the approval's state, the decision that holds, and the page's next
step `decision_make`), recorded through the step's form action with the page's result versions
(`ui/src/elements/DecisionElement.tsx`, `ui/src/elements/decisionElement231.test.tsx`). D-f7: the offer's Word
document. `jav/app/rated_offer.py` registers the output `rated_offer` (`configs/outputs.json` 1.4.0; its built-in
template's Hungarian texts in `configs/offer_documents.json`, document vocabulary) and how an accepted premium decision
becomes its data: the decision's approved version names the premium record it accepted, that record the evaluation it
was computed after, the evaluation the checked input and the product version; from these the policyholder, its tax
number, the product, the day cover starts, the verdict, each module's premium and their subtotal, each factor of the
tariff on the total (by the tariff's title), the total (marked rounded when it is not the last factor's amount, or the
policy's minimum) and the condition documents' titles, so a client sees how the total follows from the modules
(`RatedOfferError` when the decision names no premium the case kept). The
shared `priced_document@1` builds a `VersionDocument` with `build(content, case_id=...)` (`reads_case`: the builder reads
the case's other records) and, for a builder whose domain does not store its document at the approval (no `file`; the
quote's does), stores the file with the version it printed, once, with the case's chosen template (`templates.render`,
`package_release.store_own_files`): its result names the file and its sha256 (`StoredFile`), a rerun stores nothing new,
and `step_file` and `GET /api/cases/{case_id}/steps/{step_id}/file` give that file ever after (404: none). The shared
`document_view` element is built from the planned list for such a step (the file, its fingerprint, the version it
printed and from which step; `ui/src/elements/DocumentElement.tsx`). `insurance_offer.json` 1.4.0 adds the step
`document` after the premium decision and the element on the Decision and release view (`tests/test_rated_offer_231.py`,
`tests/test_insurance_offer_path_231.py`, `ui/src/elements/documentElement231.test.tsx`). D-f8: the covering letter,
a general capability (`jav/app/letters.py`, `letter@1`; the quote's reply letter stays the quote's, as it asks a model
for a paragraph). It goes with a priced document's step that stored its file (`from_step`, expecting its version, output
and file; the quote's result names none and does not fit). Its draft is written by code from a frame
(`configs/letter_frames.json`, kept at the case's start): the greeting with the addressee's name or without one, the
paragraphs and the closing, each marker a value of the printed document's output as its builder makes it, written as
the document writes it; the address is the value the step names (`to`, the contact's email: the fact catalogue 1.6.0 and
the readings 1.1.0 carry the request form's contact). A person changes the address, the subject and the text and
accepts it through the step's form action with the page's document version (refused: an older page, a marker left in
it, an address that is none, an empty or too long text); the letter is the step's own approved version, stored as an
email file (`X-Unsent: 1`) with the document's stored file attached, and a new document makes it out of date. The
message builder `letter` hands that file to the send step (`send@1`). `approved_document.step_file` and its route give
any done step's file its result names by version and sha256 (the letter's email file too). The special element
`letter` (`ui/src/elements/LetterElement.tsx`); `insurance_offer.json` 1.5.0 adds the letter and the send (a person
starts it) to the Decision and release view, and the case closes once the file is handed over
(`tests/test_letters_231.py`, `ui/src/elements/letterElement231.test.tsx`). D-f9: the shared `blockers` element is built
from the planned list (`case_views._blockers`, any pack's): what holds an open case, step by step in the pack's order,
from the process core's step states alone - a step that did not finish (its note says why) or must run again, one
waiting for a person's start, one waiting for a person's decision (a human capability's), one waiting for something
else (a task's deadline, a run, a handover) - each with the first tab whose element shows that step, where it is
resolved; a step not reached yet, or working by itself, holds nothing, and a closed case nothing at all
(`ui/src/elements/BlockersElement.tsx`). `insurance_offer.json` 1.6.0 opens its Overview with it, and the five work
views are complete: Overview (what holds the case, the documents' processing, the cost), Data check, Calculation and
versions, Decision and release (the two decisions, the offer document, the covering letter, the send) and History.
D-g, the second small composition (the owner's decision of 2026-10-26: a second composition uses the same new
capabilities without code): `configs/processes/insurance_renewal.json`, the renewal of an existing client's insurance,
composed only of the quote's capabilities in another structure - no company register, no wait for missing facts, a
stricter risk rule (only a risk the rules allow is renewed; one they refer, refuse or cannot decide goes to the full
quote and the case closes as referred), a lower limit above which another person decides the premium
(`case_decisions.json` 1.1.0) and the renewal's own letter frame (`letter_frames.json` 1.1.0). No module names it; its
test runs the reference set to the sent letter and the referred set to the full quote
(`tests/test_insurance_renewal_231.py`).
U-A6, the real corporate property product's quote (the owner's decisions of 2026-10-27):
`configs/processes/allianz_property_offer.json` is the invented insurer's quote with the same capabilities and screens
