and the real product's data: its documents read into `facts_allianz_property.json` by
`fact_readings_allianz_property.json`, the product `az_corporate_property` (from its condition documents, made active by
a person) and its example tariff (`product_set_allianz_tariff.json`, invented rates). The rule evaluation names the
case's total sum insured (`sums`), and the premium decision compares it (`amount_step`, `amount_field`) with the
authority's plan value (`case_decisions.json` `az_authority_sum_insured_plan`): above it, another person than the risk's
decides the premium. Its example material is the tariff and three invented customers' documents
(`golden_allianz_property_documents.json` printed into `examples_allianz_property_documents.json`); the quote request
form states the chosen deductible, the contract term and the waiting days (`insurance_request` 1.1.0), and the schedule
its protection class as printed (`property_schedule` 1.2.0). Tests: `tests/test_allianz_tariff_231.py`,
`tests/test_allianz_documents_231.py`, `tests/test_allianz_offer_path_231.py`.

**An editor's save behaviour (231, stage D0, D0-g; the reassessment of 2026-10-26, F15, P12).** The interface's shared
save behaviour (`ui/src/components/editing.ts`, `SaveConflict.tsx`): an editor's save line names its state (no change,
changed, saving, saved, conflict, not known; `saveState`, `SaveLine`). Its unsaved work lives in the page's memory only,
by what it edits (`keepWork`, `useWork`, `updateWork`: a quote's by its case and step), so it survives switching to
another case and back and a save finishing after the editor closed still settles it; it never reaches the browser's
storage (business data is not kept on the machine, unlike the document review's per-tab drafts), and the browser warns
before the page is left with any. A save the service refuses as made on an older state (409) keeps the person's work:
their changes since the version they started from are shown beside the version saved now, field by field and row by row
(`compare`; a row with an `id` by its id, a row added or removed), marked where the other person changed it too; keeping
them applies only their changes over the version saved now (`merge`), so the other person's other changes stay; a save
whose outcome is not known (the connection broke) is kept and sent again, and one the version saved now already holds
(an earlier try went through, an added row with its new id) settles by itself. The quote editor uses it
(`QuoteEditor.tsx`), and its approval waits while the quote has unsaved work (`useHasWork`). Tests:
`ui/src/components/editing231.test.ts`, `ui/src/domains/quotes/quoteSave231.test.tsx`. Stage D (D-a; the reassessment
after D0, P01): the first typing records the version it starts from (`QuoteWork.base`); a newer version met before
the save (another person's, or the editor opened again) is compared with it: where that person changed a field the
person changed, the comparison opens and nothing is sent; elsewhere the typing goes on over the newer version. A change
to a row the other person removed is marked (`Change.gone`) and is never settled by itself; a row the person removed
that the other person changed is marked as changed by them too. While anything is not saved, the editor's head says
"save the changes first" instead of "ready to approve" (`ui/src/domains/quotes/quoteSaveEdges231.test.tsx`).

**A process's settings page (231, plan stage B, B4).** Settings › Processes (`ui/src/views/settings/ProcessSettingsPanel.tsx`,
`ui/src/api/processes.ts`) shows each process pack's resources and rule values, each with the value in use, the file's
value and whether it is the process's own, and the roles its partners play. A setting opens with a new value (a
resource's options, a rule's whole number within its bounds or its options), a note, the drafts' preview, making a
draft active and setting the own value aside, and its versions. The names and the values come from the capabilities:
a resource's name is the title of the parameter it sets (`"@<key>"` in a step's parameters) and the values it may take
are that parameter's `Capability.choices` (the active price lists, the letter frames, the Word outputs; the send step
offers the output folder and the Outlook draft only, the real send staying a change of the pack's file); an output's
template takes the built-in one or an active own template of that output (`process_settings.resource_info`). A
process's own value of a resource with known values is one of them. The view also names the steps that read a rule
value by their titles and each preview example's case by its name. Tests: `tests/test_process_settings_231.py`,
`ui/src/views/settings/processSettings231.test.tsx`.

**Taking a package over as a case (231, plan stage C, C0).** The owner's decision of 2026-10-25: the live packages of the
processes that move onto process packs (the email routing, the reconciliation) become cases under their own ids, so
the rows keyed by a package (decisions, versions, line locks, approved statements) stay where they are.
`process_runtime.adopt_case(pack_id, wp_id, scope, actor)` checks the scope by the pack's parts, makes the package the
container's kind (`work.retype_in`: its log records the kind it had, the activity log shows it as `retyped`; the case's
data keeps it as `adopted_from`), saves its scope, links the calculation snapshot, takes the process's active values as
a new case does, and starts the process core's case with `cases.adopt`: each step in the state its capability reads
from the work the package already holds (`Capability.adopt(wp_id, params)`; without one, or None, the step has not
run). A second take-over answers "exists" and changes nothing. The before/after snapshot reads the mailbox page, the
cases and handovers with each case's page, the partner register and each process's settings too
(`before_after._process_views`). Tests: `tests/test_process_adopt_231.py`, `tests/test_before_after_171.py`.

**A case an email starts by itself (231, plan stage C, C1b).** The owner's decision of 2026-10-25: a process pack's email
intake may start its case by itself (`intake` `starts: case`; the pack check refuses it without a record scope part
and a step whose capability reads the record, or on a person's intake). The worker's turn (`intake.tick`, at most once
a minute per store) asks `intake.start_due`: for every email a run read, or a person corrected, with the intake's intent
(the email kind's handler `found_as`, the intent corrections), the record step's capability previews the record, and a
case starts once (`process_runtime.start_case` as the machine `email intake`, named by the process and what its partner
link reads). Each email is remembered before its case starts (`intake_starts`: `starting`, `started` with the case,
`had_case` when a person started one first), so an interrupted start is never repeated; the emails already there when a
process's intake first starts cases are remembered as `before` without a case. Only the case's free steps run: a paid
step waits for a person's start, as its pack says. The IT quote's pack 1.3.0 starts its case by itself; the email's page
still offers another case. Tests: `tests/test_email_intake_231.py`, `tests/test_process_packs_231.py`.

**The email routing as a process pack (231, plan stage C, C1c).** `configs/processes/mail_routing.json`: a case reads
the work packages its scope names (the general scope part `sources`, `jav/app/scopes.py`: one to a hundred existing
packages) and has one step, `file_placement@1` (`jav/domains/email/capabilities.py`, human): the email routing's
proposals and a person's decisions (`jav/domains/email/email_routing.py`, unchanged; it reads a case's `sources` as a
routing package's `emails`), waiting while any attachment it can route is undecided, done once every one is; the case
then closes. A routing package made before is taken over with its decisions (`Capability.adopt`: done, or waiting).
Its tabs: Emails, Routing and Routed (the special element `file_placement` with `show: sources | decide | routed`,
`ui/src/elements/FilePlacement.tsx`, the routing package's three stages until now), Started cases (the shared element
`started_cases`: the cases started from an item of the packages the case reads, `package_scope.holders`) and History.
Tests: `tests/test_mail_routing_case_231.py`, `ui/src/elements/filePlacement231.test.tsx`.

**The routing's own kind of package gone (231, plan stage C, C1c).** The email routing has no logic pack and no workflow
step kind of its own any more; its addresses (accept, every clear one, reject, undo) are the email domain's
(`SERVICE`), on a routing case's id. A workflow step of a process pack's case is the general step kind `process`
(`process_runtime`, its template step names the `pack`): it starts a case that reads the packages of the steps it waits
for (scope part `sources`; the workflow's party and period when the pack needs them), and the step counts once the case's
result is approved or (`CONTAINER_PACK.approved`) its case closed as done. `configs/workflows.json` 1.3.0: the kind
`process` instead of `routing`, the "From the mailbox to the packages" template's routing step and the one-step template
`mail_routing` (the wizard's general form, `ui/src/elements/ProcessStart.tsx` `ProcessCreate`: the packages of the kinds
the pack's package intake takes, and a name). A pack's intake may take a work package (`on: package`, `package_kinds`):
the package's page offers a case that reads it, with the cases already reading it (`intake.package_offers`, the view's
`intake`; `PackageIntake`). A pack names the kinds of package made before it that it takes over (`adopts`: the kind and
which stored scope part gives each of its scope parts); `process_runtime.adopt_all` and the command `cases-adopt
[--write]` take them over (`adopt_case`), run once at the release that moves a process onto its pack, after the backup.
The strictness guard's lists lose the email routing (logic pack, step kind, interface folder, logic key, form).
Tests: `tests/test_email_routing_181.py`, `tests/test_mail_routing_case_231.py`, `tests/test_workflow_steps_181.py`.

**The pairing as a general capability (231, plan stage C, C2b).** `pairing@1` (`jav/domains/finance/capabilities.py`, human, effects on the store, reading `reconcile.json` and `finance.json`, no paid service): a step that proposes by code which item of one list settles which item of the other under the rules it names (`rules`; the only ones so far are `invoice_statement`: the invoices against the bank and card statement lines of the case's scope, the reconciliation engine), and waits for a person; it is done with the version a person approves as the case's own (`package_release.own_approval`), the same rule as a workflow's reconciliation step, so a reconciliation package taken over (`Capability.adopt`) keeps its state. No pack names it yet: the monthly reconciliation's pack (C2d) will, with the pairing cards as its screen element. A registry of rule sets is left until a second set exists (the repository's rule: a framework module only once two flows wrote the same thing). The reconciliation engine itself was split by what each part does (see Invoice–bank reconciliation), its whole answer pinned on the golden cases. Tests: `tests/test_pairing_capability_231.py`, `tests/test_pairing_fingerprint_231.py`.

**The reconciliation as a process pack (231, plan stage C, C2d).** `configs/processes/reconciliation.json`: a case belongs to an own party and a period (scope parts `party`, `period`) and reconciles `accounts`, a scope part of the finance domain (`reconcile_package.ACCOUNTS_PART`) whose default is the party's accounts and cards (a scope part may have a `default`, taken from the parts checked before it; `scopes.asks` names the parts a person gives, and the process list returns them). Its one step is `pairing@1`; the invoices and the statements are processed in their own packages, as before, and read by the scope. A case keeps its party and period in the shared scope's own columns too (`process_runtime._scope_columns`), so a party's merge reaches it and `reconcile_package` reads it as its own packages (`holds_reconciliation`: a reconciliation package, or a case container whose scope names accounts; its scope change keeps the case's party and period parts and its pack reference; the decisions' conflict check counts every scope that names accounts). Its tabs: Invoices, Bank statements, Pairing and Reconciliation statement, each the special element `pairing` (`ui/src/domains/finance/Pairing.tsx` over `ReconcileView`, the package's three stages until now: `show: invoices | lines | decide | result`; the package's own page maps its stages onto the same view, unchanged), and History. The element's data is the scope, the counts, the approval and the next step with the `show` it is done on; a next step naming a `show` opens on that tab (`case_views._next`). The wizard's one-step reconciliation template is the pack's (`configs/workflows.json` 1.4.0, id `reconciliation`; the general form asks the party and the period); the workflows' reconciliation steps keep their kind until the live workflow is taken over (C2e). Two general fixes came with it: a step of a human capability says so (`by_person`), so a case's page stops asking again by itself while it waits for a person; and reopening a case's approved own version reopens the step done with it (`Capability.own_version`: the pairing and the quote's composition; `process_runtime.own_version_reopened`, called by `package_release.reopen_own`), a case closed as done opening again. Tests: `tests/test_reconciliation_case_231.py`, `tests/test_scope_defaults_231.py`, `tests/test_case_page_231.py`, `ui/src/domains/finance/pairing231.test.tsx`.

**The reconciliation packages made before taken over (231, plan stage C, C2e).** The reconciliation pack takes over the packages of the old reconciliation kind (`adopts`: kind `reconcile`, the case's party, period and accounts from the package's stored scope), so `cases-adopt --write` at the release makes each of them a case under its own id. A needed scope part that is not given may take the value the given parts imply (`scopes.imply`, said by the domain that knows: the finance domain's `reconcile_package.party_of_accounts`, the one own party every account of the scope is registered to; none when an account belongs to no party or to another); since C3a-2 the reconciliation's party is optional, so a package made without one is taken over as it was (no party is filled in). A workflow step whose package is taken over reads the container (approved once its statement is). Tests: `tests/test_reconciliation_adopt_231.py`.

**A case that approves its own version (231, plan stage C, C3a-1).** A capability whose step is done with the case's own approved version may say how that version is reopened with a reason and what the approval is about (`Capability.reopen`, `approval_what`: the pairing's "Reconciliation statement"); a case of a pack with such a step owns its approval (`process_runtime.owns_approval`). The general container answers per package (`LogicPack.owns_approval`, `approval_what_of`; read through `logic_packs.has_own_approval` and `approval_label`), so such a case waits for its approval as a person's task from its start (and the repair at a store's schema change opens a missing one, on that change's own connection), "Waiting for me" names it as the package did, and a workflow's chain reopens its approved version when an earlier step is approved again (rule 218), as it reopened the reconciliation package's. `adopt_all` refuses a package whose scope its pack refuses (no party given or implied), names the reason and goes on; `cases-adopt` then ends with 1. The before/after comparison reads a case that shows the pairing under the reconciliation keys a package's views had. A workflow's process step takes its needs (the parts of the workflow's scope its pack takes) and the meaning of its case's next step codes from its pack (`StepKind.needs_of`, `statuses_of`; `workflow_steps.step_needs`, `step_statuses`; a capability's `statuses`: the pairing's `reconcile_pair` is "pairing"), so it speaks as the reconciliation step it replaces. Tests: `tests/test_case_own_approval_231.py`, `tests/test_before_after_cases_231.py`, `tests/test_workflow_process_step_231.py`.

**The reconciliation kind retired (231, plan stage C, C3a-2).** Every reconciliation is the reconciliation pack's case: `reconcile_package.create` starts it (its scope and its log as the package's were), the three monthly templates' reconciliation step is the pack's process step (`kind: process`, `pack: reconciliation`), and a workflow saved before reads its old step as that (`configs/workflows.json` `retired_kinds`, resolved by `workflows.resolved` where a saved template is read; the saved text and its case's fingerprint stay). The pack's party is optional (`Scope.optional`, `scopes.check(..., optional=)`): checked when given and left out when not (nothing fills it), so a reconciliation without one reconciles its accounts alone, every invoice its own, as a package could. A case's container refuses the shared adding of documents (its documents are in the packages its scope names), as a reconciliation package did. The reconciliation's addresses are the finance domain's (`SERVICE`); its logic pack, workflow step and the interface's package entry went. A package of the retired kind is unknown to the new code until `cases-adopt --write` takes it over at the release (the service and the worker stopped before); "Waiting for me" shows its approval under a general label meanwhile. A case's container is not deleted while its case exists, so an empty reconciliation is hidden, not deleted. The workflow's maker owns the case its process step starts, as they owned the package a retired step made. Tests: `tests/legacy_reconcile.py` makes a package of the retired kind for the takeover tests.

**A package rule by name (231, plan stage C, C3b1).** What a package adds to the processing of its items (`work.PackageRule`: the statement package's stop at a document that is no statement, its account check, its months read once its run is approved) was only its kind's, through its logic pack. A domain also registers it by name (`work.register_rule`; the finance domain: `statements`), and a plain processing package whose stored scope names one (the scope part `rule`, `work.RULE_PART`) runs by it (`work.package_rule`: the logic pack's rule first, then the named one; `rule_named` refuses an unknown name), so a process pack's step can own a package that reads statements without a kind of its own. Tests: `tests/test_package_rules_231.py`.

**Document processing as a capability (231, plan stage C, C3b2).** `process_documents@1` (`jav/app/document_processing.py`): a process pack's step makes its own processing package from the case's source (the scope part `source`: a folder, with its subfolders when asked, or chosen files) by the package maker its pack names (`Maker`, `register_maker`; `documents` by default, the finance domain's `statements` choosing the party's statements in the period as the workflow's statements step does), binds it to itself (the package's scope part `owner_step`, `package_of`) with the maker's package rule (C3b1) and the case's party and period, and is done without a package when the source holds nothing. The package's live run may call AI, so a person starts it (the step's action `start_run`, within the package's own estimate, as any live run); the step waits for it (`run_start`) and then for its approval (`run_approval`), and is done with the approved runs. The capability names no provider: making the package is free. Tests: `tests/test_document_processing_231.py`.

**The documents and the bank statements as process packs (231, plan stage C, C3b3).** `configs/processes/documents.json` (scope `source`) and `configs/processes/bank_statements.json` (scope `party`, `period`, `source`; the step's maker `statements`): one step, `process_documents@1`, and the tabs Documents (Statements) and History. The case page's special element `package_work` (`configs/screen_elements.json`; data `document_processing._package_view`; `ui/src/elements/PackageWork.tsx`, its tab line `packageWorkStatus.ts`) shows the package the step made (its name and documents), its live runs, the start of the live run with the package's estimate on the button (`start_run`), the blockers when it cannot start, and the links to the package's own Processing, Review and Result pages, which stay the package's. The owner's decision of 2026-10-25: the cases are the main list, so the work packages list leaves out a package a case's step owns (`document_processing.owned_packages`, asked by `datasets._workpackages`) and it opens from its case. The service's `POST /api/cases` takes the source (`api_cases.Source`: a folder with `recursive`, or files; a path outside the allowed roots is refused, as for a package), and the wizard's general form asks for it when the pack's scope does (`ProcessCreate`: a folder with its browse button and the subfolders choice, or the files one a line). The wizard's one-step documents and bank statements templates are the packs' (`configs/workflows.json` 1.6.0; the statements template's id is its pack's, `bank_statements`, as for every one-step template of a pack); the statements case takes the period's statements the store has not got yet, as the workflows' statements step does (the old form let a person untick statements before the package was made). The workflows' documents and statements steps keep their kinds until C3b4. Tests: `tests/test_document_processing_231.py`, `ui/src/elements/packageWork231.test.tsx`, `ui/src/domains/finance/statementPackage140.test.tsx`.

**The statement packages as cases (231, plan stage C, C3b4).** A package with runs cannot be a case's container, so a statement package made before the bank statements were a pack gets a new case around it (`process_runtime.wrap_case`; the pack's `wraps`, `process_packs.Wrap`: the kind, the step that takes the package, the scope part that names it and the stored scope parts the case's scope comes from). In one write transaction: the container (the package's name, owner and archive state), the case's scope (the package's party and period; its `source` is `{"kind": "package", "workpackage_id"}`, a source kind of `document_processing` that the step takes as it is), the step's binding (`Capability.bind` / `bound`: `document_processing.bind` adds `owner_step` and the maker's rule to the package's scope) and the case, its step in the state its runs give (`Capability.adopt`: done with an approved live run and none open, else waiting). The package keeps its id, kind, items, runs, approvals and versions; its log records `case_wrapped`. `cases-adopt [--write]` wraps every such package not yet a step's (`adopt_all`); a package whose folder is gone is wrapped all the same. From now on a new statement package is a case's from the start (`statement_package.create` wraps it, except the one a case's own step makes): a workflow's statements step still starts its live run by itself within the budget a person approved there (rule 146), so its case's page offers no start of its own (`_workflow_starts`). The kind and the logic pack of the statement package stay until C3b5. Further general fixes: a person's party, period or role on a package's page keeps the further parts of its scope (a case step's binding, its rule; before, the package lost its case and the step would have made a second one); the document processing step waits for `run_start` (a person's start), `run_end` (the machine's work) and `run_approval` (a person's approval), and the case list says so (`Capability.waits`); a case whose step's package was deleted (one without runs may be) closes as cancelled instead of making the package again. Tests: `tests/test_statement_wrap_231.py`, `tests/test_document_processing_231.py`, `tests/test_workflows_146.py`.

**A package rule carries what a logic pack gave its kind (231, plan stage C, C3b5a).** A named package rule (`work.PackageRule`) also says what a package of the rule is beyond its processing: its page part (`page`), whether its scope is its own (`own_scope`: a person can neither give nor take it; shown as a logic pack's), why the shared adding refuses it and how its page adds (`add_refusal`, `adds`), and how another domain adds files to it or makes a new one (`take_files`, `make_with_files`; the email routing asks the rule after the logic pack). A role may name the rule a package must run by to hold it (`package_scope.Role.rule`, `holds`; `fitting` and the routing's adding use it). The statement package's rule has all of these (`statement_package.RULE`, `RULE_NAME`); the statement logic pack is still registered, asked first, until its packages are retyped. `work.rule_name(wp_id)`: the rule a package's scope names. Tests: `tests/test_package_rules_231.py`.

**The statement logic pack retired (231, plan stage C, C3b5a).** No logic pack is left (`logic_packs.packs()` is empty). A statement package is a plain package (`manual`; a folder's kind would title its items by the folder) whose scope names the statements rule (`statement_package.RULE_NAME`): `create` makes it so, and a store of before is retyped once at start-up (`statement_package._retype`, the meta mark `retype:statements`, checked again inside one write transaction): the kind becomes `manual`, a scope without the rule's name gets it (a revision further), the log records `retyped`; the package's revision and its runs stay, so its readiness, approvals and released versions read as before. The bank statements role holds a plain package of the rule (`Role("statements", …, ("manual",), rule="statements")`), and a role without a rule holds only packages of no rule, so an invoice never goes into a statement package; the workflow's statements step links only a package of the rule (`StepKind.rule`, `workflows._check_package`); the reconciliation's targets tell a statement package by its rule. The bank statements pack (1.2.0) wraps packages by their rule (`Wrap.rule`), so a retyped package is found by `cases-adopt` whichever runs first. The package's view says its rule (`rule`: its name, how a person adds to it, its page parts), and the interface finds the panel by the page part's name (`ui/src/logic/packageParts.tsx` `registerPackagePart` since C3e, `partOf`, `addsOwnWay`), not by a kind (the logic packs' registry `LOGIC` went). The statement package's own form and its two addresses (`POST /api/statement-packages/survey`, `POST /api/statement-packages`) and the documents' own form went: both one-step templates are their process packs' (the general form, which now takes a pasted path without its quotes and adds each picked file once). The statement addresses are the finance domain's (`SERVICE`). Tests: `tests/test_statement_wrap_231.py`, `tests/test_package_rules_231.py`, `tests/test_process_strictness_231.py`, `ui/src/folderBrowse081.test.tsx`.

**The workflows' documents and statements steps are their packs' (231, plan stage C, C3b5b).** A process pack may serve a workflow step kind (`ProcessPack.workflow`, `process_packs.Serves`: the kind of `configs/workflows.json`, the pack's step that takes the package and the scope part naming it): `documents.json` 1.1.0 the `documents` kind, `bank_statements.json` 1.3.0 the `statements` kind. The process runtime makes the step kind from the pack and the facts its step's capability gives (`process_runtime.pack_step_kind`: a paid step that runs its own package, needing the party and period the pack takes), and the document processing capability registers one for each such pack, last in `registrations.load_all`, once every domain registered its package makers (`document_processing.register_workflow_steps`). A package maker carries its workflow hooks (`Maker.package_kinds`, `only_new`, `subfolders`, `step_offer`, `repeat_from`, `estimate`): the documents maker's offer is the folder's or the files' documents (`workflows.documents_offer`, the documents step's start until then), the statements maker's the period's statements the store has not got yet (`statement_package.workflow_offer`). The step's start (`document_processing._workflow_start`): the maker's offer, a case of the pack around the package (`process_runtime.wrap_into`; the owner's decision of 2026-10-26: the documents step's package gets a case too), then the package noted on the step and its live run started within the step's budget (rule 146), as the kind's own start did; the case's page offers no start of its own. The kinds keep their names, so the templates and the stored workflows read as before; no domain registers a workflow step kind for them any more (the strict principle's list keeps `emails` and `quote`). Tests: `tests/test_workflows_146.py`, `tests/test_workflow_steps_181.py`, `tests/test_workflow_restart_160.py`.

**The quote as a process pack's case in the wizard (231, plan stage C, C3c).** A capability whose step reads its record from the case's scope may also find the records a person may start a case from (`Capability.records(q, params)`: each with its package, its label, plain-value detail, the name it gives a case and the cases already started from it; the IT quote's record step: the quote requests, `quotes.capabilities._records` over `quote_package.candidates`). `process_packs.records(pack_id, q)` asks the pack's first such step, and `GET /api/processes/{pack_id}/records?q=` serves it; the general form chooses the record when the pack asks for one (`ProcessCreate` `RecordChooser`: the service's search, the record's package, the cases already started from it; the default name the pack's title and the record's name). The wizard's quote template is the IT quote pack's (`configs/workflows.json` 1.7.0, id `it_quote`); the quote's own kind of work, its form (`QuoteCreate`) and `GET /api/quote-requests` went. The strict principle's lists keep only the mailbox download's step kind and form (C3d). Tests: `tests/test_quote_package_191.py`, `tests/test_process_packs_231.py`, `ui/src/domains/quotes/quote191.test.tsx`.

**An asynchronous package maker (231, plan stage C, C3d-1).** A package maker may fetch instead of making at once (a mailbox download): `Maker.fetch(scope, name, owner, taker)` starts the fetch and returns its id, `Maker.collect(fetch_id)` says where it stands (`document_processing.Collected`: running, done with its package or none, failed). The document processing step starts the fetch once (its id kept in the step's result, so a restart goes on with it), waits `fetching` (the case list: the machine's work), fails with the fetch's note (a person's retry starts a new fetch), is done without a package when nothing new came, and binds the package otherwise (a fetch interrupted after part of it arrived records `fetch_interrupted`). A case's `source` may also be a domain's source kind (`workflow_steps.SourceKind`, checked and named by its own module, `SourceKind.words`). The tests never run the Outlook bridge (`tests/conftest.py` `_no_live_mailbox`). Tests: `tests/test_document_processing_231.py`.

**The mailbox download as a process pack (231, plan stage C, C3d).** `configs/processes/mailbox.json`: a case's `source` is a mailbox download (`{"kind": "mailbox", "request"}`, the email domain's source kind), its one step `process_documents@1` with the mailbox maker (`mailbox.case_fetch` queues the download for the step, `taker` `case:<case>:<step>` on `mailbox_pulls`; `mailbox.case_collect` reads its outcome), and it serves the workflows' `emails` step kind (the maker's `workflow_offer` queues the step's download with its taker, `workflow_poll_offer` gives its package once it has finished; the process runtime's step kind has a `poll`, and the package gets a case of the pack as the documents step's does). The owner's decisions of 2026-10-26: a download no step takes (a scheduled one, one from the settings) wraps its package by a mailbox case too (`mailbox._own_case`, after the download; a failure is logged and the package stays an ordinary one), with no paid run started; the mailbox packages downloaded before stay as they are. The email domain registers no workflow step kind any more (the strict principle's list of step kinds is empty). Tests: `tests/test_mailbox.py`, `tests/test_workflows_146.py`.

**One work list, the cases (231, stage U, U2; the owner's decisions of 2026-10-28).** The interface's rail holds the cases and the settings; a case opens at its own address (`#/cases/<id>`, the case list's `case` link). A case step's document batch (the work package, *iratköteg* in the Hungarian interface) leads back to its case: its view names `owner_case` (the case, the step and the case's tab that shows the step; `document_processing.owner_of`), and the list of every batch (`workpackages`, reached from Settings › System) leaves out the cases' containers and the case steps' batches. The batches made before every batch was a case's are wrapped by their kind (U2b): the documents pack 1.4.0 wraps a folder's, chosen files', a watched folder's and an upload's batch, the mailbox pack 1.3.0 a download's (`Wrap.kind`); by its kind, `adopt_all` leaves out a hidden batch (it stays in the list of every batch) and one whose scope names a rule (that rule's process takes it). A watched folder's new batch gets its documents case at once (`app_settings._own_case`), as a download no step takes already did. Tests: `tests/test_batch_wrap_231.py`, `tests/test_document_processing_231.py`, `ui/src/views/singleList231.test.tsx`.

**A step that runs a case of another process (231, stage U, U2c; the owner's decision of 2026-10-28: a chain is a multi-step process).** The shared capability `run_case@1` (`jav/app/sub_cases.py`) starts a sub-case of the pack its parameters name, with the parts of the case's scope that pack takes, the step's own documents (the scope part `step_sources`, {step: source}, each checked as `source`) and, by `sources_from`, the batches the sub-cases of earlier steps made (`document_processing.case_packages`); `process_runtime.start_case(parent=, then=)` names the step in the sub-case's data and notes the sub-case on the step in the same transaction. The sub-case's closing sends the step `case_closed` in the closing's transaction (`cases.on_leave`): done makes the step done, another outcome stops it with why, and its retry starts a new sub-case. Two general hooks of a capability check what a step reads from the whole pack: `Capability.pack_check` (a missing process, one that runs itself, a part the sub-case needs that the pack neither holds nor can fill, `scopes.fillable`, a `sources_from` step it does not wait for) and `Capability.scope_check` at a case's start (a step's documents missing; nothing is made). The element `sub_case` shows the sub-case with its state and its own next step; a sub-case's page leads back to its case (`process_runtime.parent_of`); the case list says a step waits for its sub-case. The list of every batch leaves the cases out before it reads their next steps (`work_views.workpackage_list(batches_only=True)`), so it builds no reconciliation's workspace. Tests: `tests/test_sub_case_231.py`, `ui/src/views/subCase231.test.tsx`. The four multi-step workflow templates are process packs of such steps (`monthly_reconciliation`, `mailbox_reconciliation`, `two_sources_reconciliation`, `mailbox_routing`), started from New case: `GET /api/processes` names a pack's steps that take their own documents (`step_sources`: the step, its sub-case's process, the source kinds it takes and whether it has invented documents), and `POST /api/cases` takes them (`step_sources`, each a source as `source` is); the general form asks each one with the same source fields (`ui/src/elements/ProcessStart.tsx` `SourceFields`). What a step's sub-cases spent is the step's cost (`Capability.spent`). Tests: `tests/test_multi_step_processes_231.py`, `ui/src/views/newChain231.test.tsx`.

**Each process its own settings page (231, stage U, U3a; the owner's decision of 2026-10-28).** Settings › Processes lists every process with what it is for, and each process has its own address (`#/settings/processes/<id>/<tab>`, the settings route's `item` and `tab`) and its own item under Processes in the Settings menu. Its page has tabs: Description (how a case of it starts, its steps with what each capability does, who starts it and what it waits for, its outputs and the golden sets that measure it: `process_settings.view`'s `description`), What it uses (the resources), Rule values, Partner roles and Examples (the shared example material element). Tests: `tests/test_process_page_231.py`, `ui/src/views/settings/processSettings231.test.tsx`.

**What a process uses is chosen from a list (231, stage U, U3b).** Every resource of every pack offers its values (`process_settings.resource_info`; a test holds it for every pack). A parameter whose values depend on the step takes them from `Capability.choices_for(pack, step)`: a priced document's output offers only the outputs whose domain prints the version of the step it names (`VersionDocument.prints`: the quote prints `line_draft`, the rated offer `decision`), and the capability's `pack_check` refuses a pack naming another. The insurance packs' letter frame, offer document, send mode and offer template are resources (`insurance_offer` 1.9.0, `insurance_renewal` 1.3.0, `allianz_property_offer` 1.2.0; the values unchanged). An empty list says where its values come from. Tests: `tests/test_process_pickers_231.py`.

**The product brings its tariff (231, stage U, U3c; the owner's decision of 2026-10-28).** A pack may say that a resource follows from another (`ProcessPack.derived`, `{"tariff": "product"}` in the three insurance packs): the source's keeper brings it (`AtStart.brings`; a product's `product_rules.product_tariff`), `process_settings.effective_resources` takes it from there (an own value of it is ignored), a case keeps the active version of the tariff its product names, the settings page shows it read only and `propose` refuses one. The Tariffs list is a tab of the Products page (`#/settings/tariffs` opens it). Tests: `tests/test_product_tariff_231.py`.

**Every shared thing names the processes that use it (231, stage U, U3d; the owner's decisions of 2026-10-28).** `process_settings.used_by(kind)` lists, for each thing of a kind (a product, a tariff, a price list, a letter frame of the insurance letters or of the quote, an output template, a partner role), the processes that use it now: a resource names one when its capability says which kind the parameter refers to (`Capability.refers`, `{"frame": "letter_frame"}`), its keeper kept one at a case's start (`AtStart.kind`: product, price list) or its key is an output's template (`template:<output>`); the value is the process's own active one or its file's, a derived one as its source brings it, and a partner role comes from the pack's partner links. `GET /api/library/used-by/{kind}` serves it; the Products and Tariffs lists show it in a column, a price list and an own output template under their names, the partner register for the chosen role (`ui/src/components/UsedBy.tsx`, one request per kind). Tests: `tests/test_used_by_231.py`, `ui/src/components/usedBy231.test.tsx`.

**Settings in three groups; the own parties among the partners (231, stage U, U3e; the owner's decisions of 2026-10-28).** Every Settings section names its menu group (`SettingsSectionSpec.group`: `processes`, `library`, `system`; `SETTINGS_GROUPS` in `ui/src/logic/settingsSections.ts`), a domain's section too, and the menu shows each group under its name: Processes (the overview, named "All processes" in the menu, and each process), the shared library (Partners, Own parties, Products, Price lists, Output templates) and the system (Users, Appearance, Language, Work folders, Mailboxes, Processing, Rules - read only -, Operations, until then "System"). An unknown section opens the processes. The partner list shows the own parties with the role `own` (`partners.OWN`, `partners.own_listing`: by name, with the first tax number; `GET /api/partners` `own`), each a link to the own parties' register, where it is still edited; no partner record can be given the role, and a process whose scope has an own party (`scopes.PARTY`) uses it (`process_settings.used_by`). Tests: `tests/test_own_partners_231.py`, `ui/src/views/settings/settingsGroups231.test.tsx`.

**The letter frames, edited as versions (231, stage U, U3f; the owner's decision of 2026-10-28).** `jav/app/letter_frames.py` keeps a person's text of a letter frame as versions over the rule file's (`letter_frame_versions`, keyed `<kind>/<frame>`, through the shared versioning): a module that writes letters registers its kind of frame (`FrameKind`: the rule file and the key prefix that hold its frames, the fields a person edits - a line, a text, or paragraphs - under their group, and the outputs whose values its markers may name): the covering letters (`letters.FRAME_KIND`, `letter_frame`, `configs/letter_frames.json` `frames.`) and the quote's reply letters (`quote_letter_frame`, `configs/quote_documents.json`, a text per language). `propose` saves a draft of the text in effect with the changed fields, refused when a field is empty or too long, a line holds a line break, or a marker is malformed or names no value of the documents (`parts.version_outputs`, the output contract); `activate` follows the process values' rule of who may (`process_settings.may_activate`) and checks again; `retire` brings the file's text back. A case keeps the text active at its start: `process_settings.effective_rules` adds the active version of each frame its resources name (`letter_frames.in_effect`) to the case's own rule values (`capabilities.RULES`, with the version as its origin), so the letter steps read it over the rule file. `GET /api/library/letter-frames`, `POST /api/library/letter-frames/{kind}/{frame}` (a draft), `.../versions/{n}/activate`, `.../retire`; the Settings section Letter frames (shared library; `ui/src/views/settings/LetterFramesPanel.tsx`): each frame's text field by field, the processes that use it, its versions, a form of its fields with the markers to use. A frame is not added or removed here: the processes name the rule files' frames. Tests: `tests/test_letter_frames_231.py`, `ui/src/views/settings/letterFrames231.test.tsx`.

**The installation's defaults (231, stage U, U4a; the owner's decisions of 2026-10-29).** `jav/app/rule_defaults.py` keeps the installation's own value of a rule key a person may set (`editable()`, for now the capabilities' `RuleKey`s) as versions over the rule file's (`rule_default_versions`, keyed `<file>.<key>`, through the shared versioning): `propose` checks the value against the key's declaration, `activate` follows the process values' rule of who may (`process_settings.may_activate`, the owner's decision: as for the processes), `retire` brings the file's value back. The config reader takes them from a provider a long-running process names (`cfg.use_defaults`; the service and the worker call `rule_defaults.use()` after `preload`): a read outside any `cfg.overlay` context sees the active defaults over the files (`cfg.current_overlay`, read from the store again at most every `REFRESH_S` seconds), while a case's context and `cfg.overlay(None)` (the files alone) never do; `capabilities.case_rules` binds a context only for a case, so a package that is no case reads the defaults. A case keeps the defaults active at its start among its own values (`process_settings.effective_rules`: the defaults first, the process's own values over them), and the decision trace records each one's origin (`default:<file>.<key>` and its version) as it records a process's values, so a replay reads them. A document batch's run item reads the defaults active when it runs. A command or a test names no provider: a measurement reads the files (the owner's decision). `config_hash` stays the files'. `/api/health` names the keys with an active default (`own_defaults`). Tests: `tests/test_rule_defaults_231.py`.

**What may be a default (231, stage U, U4b; the owner's decisions of 2026-10-29: every value of the rules view, the cost ones never).** Every value the rules view shows declares in `configs/rule_help.json` (`edit`, 1.6.0) whether the installation may keep its own value of it: a kind with its bounds or options, `capability` (the capability's own `RuleKey`), or `fixed` with why (`cost`: the paid text recognition and a run's item budget; `structure`: a product catalogue's levels and verdicts; `format`: the tax number's stem). `rule_defaults.editable()` builds a `capabilities.RuleKey` from each declaration, which gained the kinds a default may be (`number`, `bool`, `amount`, `set`, `map`; a capability's own keys stay `int` or `choice`), its options from the declaration, from a registry (`rule_defaults.register_options`: the document types and the fields here, the email's routes and warning signs registered by the email domain, as only it reads `configs/email.json`) and always from the file's own value; `fixed()` names the others. Tests: `tests/test_rule_defaults_editable_231.py` (every value shown is either, the cost ones fixed, the file's value fits).

**A default tried before it is made active (231, stage U, U4c).** `rule_defaults.preview(file, key, value)` runs again every traced decision that read the key, in a case or outside one, with the values it was made with and with the tried value in place (`process_settings.replayed`, shared with a process value's preview, which keeps only its own cases' rows); a decision a process's own value of the key decided is counted apart (`covered`), as a default would not have reached it, and one made under an earlier default (its origin `default:<file>.<key>`) is tried like the others. The service: `GET /api/defaults` (the keys, the fixed values with why, the rule of who may), `POST /api/defaults/{file.key}` (a draft), `.../preview`, `.../versions/{n}/activate`, `.../retire`. Tests: `tests/test_rule_defaults_preview_231.py`, `tests/test_rule_defaults_231.py`.

**Settings › Defaults (231, stage U, U4d; the owner's decisions of 2026-10-29).** The rules page (the Settings section `rules`, named Defaults) opens a decision's sheet, where each value shows under it the default editor (`ui/src/views/settings/DefaultEditor.tsx`, data from `GET /api/defaults`): whether the file's or the installation's own value counts, a form fitting the key's kind (a percentage or a gap in percentage points edited as one, a whole number, an amount, yes or no, a choice, a set of checkboxes, the email's routes per intent), the try on the earlier decisions (counts and examples, the ones a process's own value decided named apart), the save as a draft, and its versions with the activation and "Back to the file's value"; a fixed value says why it stays the file's. Tests: `ui/src/views/settings/defaults231.test.tsx`.

**Only the cost values stay the file's (231, stage U, U4z; the owner's decision of 2026-10-29).** The product catalogue's levels and verdicts and the digits compared in a tax number, first fixed as structure and format, may be defaults too (`rule_help.json` 1.7.0): a `RuleKey` gained the kind `list` (an ordered list of distinct codes, at least `least` of them), edited one code a line. `rule_defaults.fixed()` now names only the values that change a paid request or a budget (`cost`). The installation's defaults are read only once the application's registrations are made (`registrations.loaded`): a long-running process that names them first reads the files at its modules' import, as what may be a default needs the domains' option registries.

**Several workers on one store (231, stage E, E1; the owner's decisions of 2026-10-29: several workers, one by default).** `configs/service.json` `workers` (1.11.0) says how many workers may run on a store; each takes a numbered slot, an operating-system lock next to the store (`worker.lock_path(slot)`: the first keeps `worker.lock`, then `worker-2.lock`, ...; `worker --slot N`, or the first free one; `lock.AlreadyRunning` when every one is taken), and `scripts/dev.ps1` starts, lists and stops as many. A worker's claim records its process's holder (`queue.claim(..., holder=calls.holder())`, the lock file a process keeps while it lives, as a paid call's reservation and a case's claim do; `jobs.holder`), and `queue.recover_orphans` resets only the claims whose holder has stopped (one without a holder counts as stopped, as before); every worker runs it at start-up and every `worker.RECOVER_EVERY_S` seconds, so a crashed worker's items go back to the queue at once while the others' stay theirs. Only the first slot runs the scheduled ticks (the domains' ticks, the watched folders, the workflows, the email intake, the cases' due turns). A stop request stops every worker after its item in progress (it counts for the workers started before it; the last to stop clears it). `worker.status()` and `/api/worker` name the running workers of the slots; Settings › Operations shows it when more than one may run. The Burr state stays one SQLite file the workers share. Tests: `tests/test_several_workers_231.py`, `ui/src/views/settings/workers231.test.tsx`.

**The start-up description and the store's installation (231, stage E, E2; the owner's decisions of 2026-10-29).** `jav/core/config.py` reads the start-up description the environment variable `JAV_START` names, when it is first imported, before any config is read: `configs` (`CONFIG_ROOT`, the rule files, registries and prompts; `cfg.CONFIG_DIR`), `data` (`DATA_ROOT`: the store and everything next to it, the runs with the caches and the logs, the inbox), `env` (the keys) and `installation` (`INSTALLATION_ID`); without it every root is the program's folder, as before. A description that cannot be read, names an unknown key or a data folder that does not exist raises `StartError`. Every writable path comes from these roots (`RUNS_DIR`, `CACHE_DIR`, `STORE_PATH`, `INBOX_ROOT`, `LOG_DIR`; the OCR cache, the folders the output folder may not overlap and the service's allowed roots under `DATA_ROOT`). The installation's store is stamped with its installation in `meta` (`installation_id`) when it is first set up; `store.check_installation()` (read only, at the service's and the worker's start) and every set-up refuse a store of another installation (`ForeignStore`) before anything is written; a store a command names (a copy) is never stamped. `/api/health` names the installation. Setup: `docs/guides/SETUP.md` 5.1. Tests: `tests/test_start_description_231.py` (two invented installations on one machine, another installation's store untouched, an unusable description).

**The store's order (231, stage E, E3; the owner's decisions of 2026-10-29).** Every write lock goes through `store.begin_immediate` (the 8 modules that took their own, and a deferred `BEGIN`, now use it), and no runtime module writes an insert only SQLite reads: a row already there stays with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, a proposal asked again starts afresh with `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE` (until then `INSERT OR IGNORE` / `INSERT OR REPLACE`). The other SQLite-specific constructs (PRAGMA, sqlite_master, json_extract, instr, a direct connection: the Burr state, the backup, a native reading's copy) stay where they are, their count per module frozen by a guard test. `store.SCHEMA_VERSION` (2) is now read: a store records the newest program version that set it up (`meta.schema_version`, raised, never lowered), and a store a newer program set up stops this one before it writes (`SchemaTooNew`, at the set-up and in `store.check_installation` at the service's and the worker's start). A release raises the version when it adds a change an earlier program cannot work with. Tests: `tests/test_store_order_231.py`.

**Measuring (231, stage E, E4; the owner's decisions of 2026-10-29: into a local file, off by default).** The measuring point (`jav/core/observability.py`) gives a measurement the shape of an OpenTelemetry span (its name, start, duration, status and attributes, `span`) and lets an attribute pass only when its name holds none of `FORBIDDEN` (a document's text, a request or a response, a file's name or path, an email address, a subject or a body, a case) and its value is a number, a yes or no, or a short code (`clean`). Every call written to the call log is measured there (`store.ledger_add`: `call.<step>`, its provider, model, tokens, cost and whether it was cached or failed), the process core's steps and handovers as before, and the call sites time through one helper (`observability.start`, `since`; the clock is read directly only by a page reading's own figure). `configs/service.json` `metrics` (1.12.0, off) switches the local file sink on (`FileSink`: one JSON line a measurement in `runs/metrics/<day>.jsonl` under the data folder), which the service and the worker name at their start (`use_config`). Tests: `tests/test_observability_231.py`.

**The general form takes a domain's source, and the wizard's own forms are gone (231, plan stage C, C3d-4).** `GET /api/processes` names each pack's source kinds (`document_processing.source_kinds`: its maker's `sources`), and `POST /api/cases` takes a mailbox download as the source (its request, checked by the scope's part). The general form finds the chooser of a source kind a domain adds in a registry (`ui/src/elements/sourceChoosers.tsx` `registerSourceChooser` since C3e, `chooserOf`: the mailbox download's `MailboxRequestFields`, which Settings › Mailboxes uses for a schedule too), and so does the new work wizard for a workflow step's source (`MailboxStepFields`, until then in the wizard). The wizard's emails template is the mailbox pack's (`configs/workflows.json` 1.8.0, id `mailbox`); every one-step template is a process pack's case, so the kinds' own forms went (`ui/src/logic/steps.tsx` kept only `SingleForm`, which went with the wizard in stage U), and so did the mailbox page's download variant (a download now is a mailbox case's). The owner's decision of 2026-10-26: the case page's package element offers the processes that read its package (`package_work`'s `offers`, `intake.package_offers`: an email package's email routing), as the package's page does. Tests: `tests/test_mailbox.py`, `ui/src/elements/packageWork231.test.tsx`, `ui/src/views/workflows/workflows146.test.tsx`.

**The interface's domains (231, plan stage C, C3e).** The interface has the service's domains too: a domain's screens live in `ui/src/domains/<domain>/` (the last part of a name in `jav/domains/__init__.py` `DOMAINS`) and register themselves with the shared registries when the interface loads (`ui/src/domains/index.ts`, imported by `ui/src/main.tsx` and the tests' setup), as the service's domains register their addresses. The shared code reaches them only through the registries: a source kind's chooser (`ui/src/elements/sourceChoosers.tsx` `registerSourceChooser`: the wizard's choice, the case's fields, the workflow step's draft, fields, stored source and words), a section of Settings or a part a domain adds to a shared one (`ui/src/logic/settingsSections.ts`, the shared sections listed there with their order), a capability's element of a case's tabs (`ui/src/elements/registry.tsx` `registerElement`; a case page finds the shared and the registered ones with `elementOf`), a package rule's page part (`ui/src/logic/packageParts.tsx` `registerPackagePart`, which also gives the rule's own add form, `Add`, and whether the scope is the rule's, `ownScope`), a view of the Result section with its panel above the table (`ui/src/logic/resultTables.ts`, the shared views listed there with their order), and a panel under an item's to-dos in the review (`ui/src/review/itemPanels.ts`). A domain's calls and records live in its `api.ts`: it adds the calls to the shared client (`ui/src/api/index.ts` `registerCalls`) and their types by declaration merging (`DomainCalls`; a refused upload's check likewise, `ui/src/api/client.ts` `UploadChecks`; the package's and the item's view parts, `WorkpackageView`, `ItemResult`), so a screen keeps calling `api.<call>` and the shared code imports no domain's records. The own parties' overview is the shared code's (`ui/src/api/parties.ts`: the shared scope part `party` chooses from it; the register is `jav/app/parties.py`), though the finance domain's addresses serve it. A domain's screens load on first use; its `index.tsx` loads only what registering needs. The email domain (`ui/src/domains/email/`) registers the mailbox download (`Mailbox.tsx`, its step's draft and words in `mailboxSource.ts`) and Settings › Mailboxes; the quotes domain (`ui/src/domains/quotes/`) its calls, the quote's four elements (their status lines in `quoteStatus.ts`) and Settings › Price lists; the finance domain (`ui/src/domains/finance/`, the reconciliation's screens in `reconcile/`) its calls, the statements rule's part (its add form `AddStatements.tsx`), the duplicate and statement panels, the utility cost's view, the pairing's two elements, Settings › Own parties and the bank files under Settings › Processing. The 133 choice list's two rules (`.rc-accounts`), which the shared party chooser uses, moved from the reconciliation's stylesheet to the end of `ui/src/styles.css`, where they keep coming after every other shared rule. The old reconciliation part of the package's view (131) went from its type; nothing has sent it since C3a-2. The strict principle's test checks that the domain folders are the service's domains, that nothing outside them imports a domain's module but the interface's start, that no domain imports another, that the shared code calls no domain's call and that only a domain registers an entry (`tests/test_process_strictness_231.py`).

**The statement reader in parts (231, plan stage C, C3g).** The 1,343-line statement table module was split verbatim: `statement_common.py` (the reading's records, its configuration, the money readers, a table's header and what every reader fills alike in a line), `statement_tabular.py` (136: a tabular export's rows, blocks and accounts), `statement_ledger.py` (137: a ledger export and its PDF companion), `statement_text.py` (141: a statement printed as text) and `statement_camt.py` (141: a camt.053 statement); `statement_table.py` keeps reading a file or a folder, the survey and the derived files, and re-exports the names its callers use (a fixed list: new code imports from the defining module). Every one of the 87 definitions moved with an unchanged syntax tree (checked against the commit before the split); the isolated reader's helper now runs `statement_tabular.parse_rows`. The tests replace the PDF line reader in both readers that call it (`tests/statement_pdf.py`).

**The mailbox module in parts (231, plan stage C, C3h).** The 1,012-line mailbox module was split verbatim: `mailbox_bridge.py` (what a download reads, the free count and the download through the Outlook bridge, the package of the fresh messages with its owner, the PDF attachments added later, the mark on a suspicious email's attachment) and `email_items.py` (an email item on the review page and a person's decisions on its task proposals; the decision kind `email_task` is now owned by this module); `mailbox.py` keeps the downloads and their schedules (its tables), the mailbox case's maker, the workflow's emails step and its source kind, and re-exports the names its callers use. Every one of the 86 definitions moved with an unchanged syntax tree. The tests' guard against the Outlook bridge replaces the default runner in `mailbox_bridge`, where the count and the download call it, and a test proves that a call without a runner of its own fails (`tests/test_mailbox.py`).

**The workflows module in parts (231, plan stage C, C3i).** The 1,359-line workflows module was split verbatim: `workflow_records.py` (the workflows' tables and migrations, the hooks of an own party's merge and a package's deletion, the templates, a workflow's row, steps, events, step status and view, and the checks a new workflow's period, budget, engine, source and package go through) and `workflow_advance.py` (a step's start, link and update, the worker's tick and the stalled starts, the process core's turns with the step kind `workflow_step` and the budget gate's decision point, and the wizard's estimate); `workflows.py` keeps making and repeating one, the workflow log's actions (`activity_kinds`), and re-exports the names its callers use. Every one of the 105 definitions moved with an unchanged syntax tree. The step kind's and the decision point's owner module is now `workflow_advance` (an in-memory registry name, stored nowhere). The tests patch a moved name in the module whose code calls it.

**The AI pairing proposal as a step a person starts (231, plan stage C, C2c; the owner's decision of 2026-10-25).** `pairing_proposal@1` (`jav/domains/finance/capabilities.py`, machine, paid: JEV; reading the reconciliation call site, `finance.json` and `reconcile.json`) is the reconciliation pack's optional step `propose`, started by a person from the Pairing tab (the element `pairing_proposal`, `ui/src/domains/finance/PairingProposal.tsx`: the button with the run's cost cap, the run's state, what it answered). Its run (`jav/domains/finance/pairing_proposal.py`, table `pairing_proposals`, one row per round; the worker's job `pairing_propose`) asks the engines and at most the cap of `configs/finance.json` 1.1.0 `reconcile.ai_proposal` (JEV only, 0.20 USD a run; the GPT path is a separate decision) about the lines still to decide (`reconcile_ai.ask_lines`, split out of the command line's `run`, which stays a measurement under the owner's sub-budget), inside the round's own budget scope (`capabilities.step_budget`: `case:<case>:propose:<round>`, business work, found by the case's cost element); the raw answers go where the pairing cards read them, and a person still decides each pair. A run whose every question failed fails the step once with its reason; starting it again (the general retry) is a new round with its own cap. A pack step may be `optional` (only one a person starts, `process_packs.Step.optional`, carried as `_optional`): while nobody starts it, it looks again every minute (`capabilities.OPTIONAL_CHECK_S`), and once every other step of its case is done its start task is withdrawn and it is done as skipped (phase `skipped`), so the case closes. The pairing element reloads its workspace when a step of the case moves on. Tests: `tests/test_pairing_proposal_231.py`, `ui/src/domains/finance/pairing231.test.tsx`.
